In the two real disconnects of 2026-10-06 every such reading said "not connected", so it was not seen there; device check 271 looks for it.
- **One fix that is wrong by kilometres and claims to be good can become a trip, if it is the first usable fix of a wait.** That fix is checked against a place that is at least ten minutes old, so the speed check lets a position up to 30 km away through. If it is more than about 4.5 km off, the fixes that follow are taken for jumps and then believed, and the wrong distance stays counted: a finished trip the truck never drove. It needs a fix that is wrong by kilometres with a stated accuracy of 25 m or better. Shown in a test, never seen; it is the distance calculation's known limit ([GPS recording and distance](#gps-recording-and-distance), "Bad fixes that are still believed"), which the watch inherits.
- **A trip that the parked rule discards still counts for the daily check.** A trip that started beside a standing truck (a new link while it is parked, MilO opened after the three days, a phone carried about) and was discarded with a few metres counts as "a trip was started today". On such a day the daily "nothing recorded" check stays silent even if the real drive is then missed ([Diagnostics and reminders](#diagnostics-and-reminders)).
- The grace period, the parked limit and the minimum distance are Shawn's to set on the [Settings](#settings) screen. Nothing tells the trip controller about a change: it reads all three at every trigger, so the stored value is the one in force from the next event on. A grace period that is already running keeps the deadline it was started with.
- If storage fails in the middle of an event, the controller logs the failure, forgets what it held in memory, and reads the state from storage again at once: a service that came up for a trip which could not be stored is stopped, and a trip that is still open in storage carries on. The fix or the event that failed is lost. If the hold-off could not be stored after End, the truck starts a new trip straight away. If storage fails that second time too, nothing more is tried until the next trigger.

---

## GPS recording and distance

**Status:** Built, proven on the phone: fixes are asked for, arrive and are stored during a trip (2026-10-05: the first one 0.3 seconds after a start), and a parked phone adds no distance (2026-10-06: a trip of six and a half minutes with 59 fixes, closed at 0 m). Built, not yet proven on the phone: the distance of a real drive, and the start and end addresses, which ran on an emulator only. A fix every 2 seconds during a trip (2026-10-09): on an emulator only · **Platforms:** Android · **Last updated:** 2026-10-09

**What it does**
Records the truck's position during a trip and turns it into a distance in km, with start and finish addresses.

**Required behaviour**
- Record a GPS fix every 2 seconds using `FusedLocationProviderClient`, and count distance only after 10 m of real movement. Shawn's brief said "every 5 seconds or 10 m", and until 2026-10-09 it was 5; that day he found the distance short of his truck's and chose "every 1 to 2 seconds" (ADR-002, amendment 37). The location API cannot express "or" (its interval and distance settings combine as "and"), so the 10 m rule is applied in MilO's own distance calculation.
- Discard points with accuracy worse than 25 m, and impossible jumps (implied speed over 180 km/h).
- Store the raw points so distance can be recalculated later.
- Discard trips under 0.3 km (configurable), for example moving the truck around the yard.
- Reverse-geocode the start and end points to street addresses and store them with the trip. Start and finish locations and mileage are tracked automatically. Until 2026-10-07 there was no odometer entry anywhere in the app; since then Shawn types the truck's odometer reading in Settings, and MilO keeps it current from the trips ([Settings](#settings), "Odometer").

**How it works today**
*Recording.* When the controller tells the trip service that a trip is open, the service starts `LocationRecorder`, which asks the fused location provider for a fix every 2 seconds at high accuracy (`FixRate.RECORDING`; the log says "Location fixes requested, one every 2.0 s"): no minimum distance, never faster than 2 seconds, no batching, and no cached position (the first fix must be a new one). Each fix arrives in a `LocationCallback` on the main thread and is handed to the controller, which stores it in `raw_points` under the open trip and adds it to the running distance (`TripProgress`). The home screen and the notification show that running distance. The recorder also writes to the event log when fixes are requested and stopped, how long the first fix took and how accurate it was, and when location has been unavailable for 10 seconds and when it comes back. The fixes stop when the trip closes.

*Beside a parked truck.* When the trip rules close a trip because the truck stood still and it is still connected, the service is told to watch instead of record (`FixRate.WATCHING_PARKED`): the same request with 30 seconds between fixes, a change of rate the log records as "Location fixes are now one every 30.0 s". Those fixes are stored nowhere, because no trip is open. `ParkedWatch` runs the calculation below over the parked place and the fixes as they come. The place is where the closed trip ended; a trip that never moved has no end position, and its newest usable fix is the place (`ParkedWatch.placeAfter`). A fix that shows the truck somewhere else counts once a later fix bears it out, within two minutes; one that nothing bears out in that time is dropped. When the truck has moved, the fixes that showed it are stored as the first points of the new trip, after one point for the parked place itself, which has no speed because it is not a fix: exactly the points `ParkedWatch.tripStart` names. The service goes to the rate of a trip, a fix every 2 seconds. The wait's own rates did not follow the trip's on 2026-10-09: 30 seconds, and 5 seconds for ten minutes after a report of getting into a vehicle (`FixRate.WATCHING_CLOSELY`), are what seeing the truck drive off was proven at.

*Closing.* When the trip rules end a trip, the controller reads the trip's stored fixes back, cuts off those recorded after the truck was found gone, and works out the distance, the end time and the start and end positions from the rest (`TripClosing`). It stores them on the trip and marks the trip `FINISHED`, or `DISCARDED` if it is under the minimum distance or was a false start. The event log gets the outcome, the distance and how many fixes were stored, used and rejected.

*The calculation,* `DistanceCalculator` in `core/trip/`, is the same for the running distance and for the final one. It takes the fixes in the order recorded and applies four rules to each:
1. **Accuracy.** A fix with no accuracy, or an accuracy radius over 25 m, is not used.
2. **Impossible jumps.** A fix that would need more than 180 km/h to reach from the last good fix is not used. The time between them comes from the phone's elapsed-realtime clock, which never jumps. A long gap (a tunnel) is not a jump, so the distance across it is counted as a straight line. If three rejected fixes in a row agree with each other, they are believed instead and counting restarts from them: this is what stops one bad fix, such as a stale first position, from poisoning the rest of the trip. The leap itself is never counted.
3. **Only real movement counts.** Distance is added only once the truck is at least 10 m from where distance was last counted, and at least twice the two fixes' own stated error: 20 m with 5 m fixes, 40 m with 10 m fixes, 100 m with 25 m fixes. What is added is the straight line from where distance was last counted. On a straight road that loses nothing; through a turn it cuts the corner.
4. **Out and back is not a drive.** If the fix after a counted step is nearer to where counting stood before the step than to where the step went, the step was a bad fix and is taken back. This is what keeps one bad fix 200 m away, while the truck stands at a light or sits parked, from adding 400 m.

Distance between two fixes is the haversine great-circle distance, in pure Kotlin so it runs in unit tests. It differs from Android's own ellipsoid calculation by at most about 0.5 %, far less than GPS error over such short steps.

Every fix is stored in `raw_points` whether or not these rules use it, with both clocks, its accuracy and its reported speed, so a trip can be recalculated later with better rules. The thresholds are named constants gathered in `DistanceLimits`.

*Addresses.* `TripAddresses` in `platform/address/` turns the stored start and end positions of a trip into two addresses.
1. **It only watches.** It reads the trip controller's published state and the stored trips, and writes nothing but the four address columns of a finished trip. Ending a trip never waits for it, and a failure in it leaves the trip as it was.
2. **When it looks.** One pass over the trips that are due is made when a trip stops being in progress, at every process start, and each time the Trips screen comes to the front. The pass at process start is asked for through the trip controller (`TripController.whenCaughtUp`), so it runs after the controller has dealt with what the last process left behind: a trip the restart rules close there was never seen in progress by the lookup, and is looked up by that pass. Passes run one at a time; a request made during a pass leads to one more pass after it. There is no scheduled job (WorkManager is not in the build yet): a trip whose lookup failed waits for the next of those occasions.
3. **Which trips are due** (`isDueForLookup` in `AddressRetry.kt`): a `FINISHED` trip that lacks an address for an end whose position is stored, has not used up its attempts, and whose last attempt is long enough ago. **An address that is Shawn's own is never lacking:** one he typed, changed or emptied on the edit screen, and both addresses of a trip he added by hand (see [Trip log](#trip-log-home-day-and-month-views)). The lookup neither asks about such an address nor writes to it, and the update in storage refuses it too, so an answer that was on its way while he saved cannot undo what he typed. A `DISCARDED` trip is never looked up, and neither is a `DELETED` one. A deleted trip that is restored, or a discarded one that Shawn counts after all, is a finished trip again: it is due like any other, and the Trips screen asks for a pass at once. Trips recorded before addresses existed are due like any other, and are caught up by the first pass.
4. **The lookup itself** is Android's `Geocoder` (`GeocoderAddressLookup`, behind the interface `AddressLookup`): no key, a network connection, up to five candidates asked for, 20 seconds waited at most. The answer arrives on a listener, on one of Android's own threads; an answer that comes after the 20 seconds is dropped. This is the one place where a position leaves the phone.
5. **One line per address** (`addressLine` in `AddressLine.kt`), in this order of preference: the street and the town, with the house number in front when the geocoder gives one ("10103 104 Avenue Northwest, Edmonton"); the street alone; the town alone (the district, county or province stands in where the geocoder names no town); and last the geocoder's own line with the country and the postal code taken out. A country or a postal code is never shown. Of several candidates the one that says the most is used, and the geocoder's first when two say as much. A house number does not count as saying more: the geocoder lists its best match first, and a numbered candidate further down can be the building next door or one on another street.
6. **No network is not an attempt.** Before a pass asks anything, `NetworkStatus` asks Android whether the phone has a working internet connection. Without one nothing is asked and nothing is counted. The log gets one line, and another only when what is waiting has changed: a trip that starts and ends without a network gets one while its start is waiting and one, "Waiting: 1 finished trip.", once it has ended.
7. **Giving up.** A lookup that leaves an address missing (the geocoder knows none there, or it failed) is counted on the trip. After the first the trip waits at least 2 minutes, after the second 1 hour, after the third 1 day; after the fourth (`MAX_ADDRESS_ATTEMPTS`) it is not asked about again. An address that was found is kept, so a trip can end up with one of the two. A lookup that fails outright also ends the pass: the trips waiting behind it would fail the same way and each lose an attempt.
8. **A trip in progress.** Its row has no position until it closes, so the lookup reads the position of the trip's first usable fix from the trip controller (`CurrentTrip.startLatitude`), asks for its address once, and keeps the answer in memory for the Trips screen (`TripAddresses.openTripStart`). When the trip closes with that same start position, the remembered address is stored without asking again.
9. **The event log,** category `ADDRESS`: one line per lookup of a trip ("Trip 3: start address found; end address none (the geocoder knows no address there). Failed attempt 1 of 4; the next one is at least 2 min away."), one per lookup for a trip in progress, and one when a pass was put off for lack of a network. The lines name no address and no position.

**Where the code lives**
- `platform/address/`: `TripAddresses.kt` (the passes), `AddressRetry.kt` (what is due, when to give up, what a row says about each end), `AddressLine.kt` (the one line), `AddressLookup.kt` and `GeocoderAddressLookup.kt` (the geocoder), `NetworkStatus.kt`, `AddressLogText.kt` (the log lines). `app/MiloApplication.kt` asks for the pass at process start, through `TripController.whenCaughtUp`.
- `data/trip/`: the four address columns on `Trip`, `findTripsLackingAddress` and `recordAddressLookup` on `TripRepository`. The migration that added the columns is `data/MiloMigrations.kt`.
- `platform/trip/LocationRecorder.kt` (the request and the callback); `platform/trip/TripLedger.kt` (stores each fix, closes the trip).
- `core/trip/`: `DistanceCalculator.kt` (the four rules and the thresholds), `Haversine.kt`, `TrackPoint.kt`, `TripProgress.kt` (the running distance, the time of the last movement, and what the trip rules are told about movement after each fix), `ParkedWatch.kt` (the same calculation, run beside a parked truck), `TripClosing.kt` (minimum distance, end time and positions).
- `data/point/`: `RawPoint.kt` (the table), `RawPointDao.kt`, `RawPointRepository.kt`; the database is `data/PointsDatabase.kt`.
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../core/trip/DistanceCalculatorTest.kt`, `DistanceCalculatorParkedTest.kt`, `DistanceCalculatorOutlierTest.kt`, `DistanceCalculatorTurnsTest.kt`, `HaversineTest.kt`, `TripProgressTest.kt`, `TripClosingTest.kt`; for the addresses `app/src/test/.../platform/address/AddressLineTest.kt`, `AddressRetryTest.kt`, `TripAddressesTest.kt`, `TripAddressesWithControllerTest.kt` (the lookup beside a real trip controller) and `app/src/test/.../data/MiloMigrationsTest.kt`. The call to Android's geocoder itself has no unit test: it needs a phone.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- The distance shown during a trip can be a little higher than the stored one: what is recorded during the grace period counts while the trip is open, and is cut off when it closes.
- A fix's time of day is worked out from the phone's clock and the fix's age, not taken from the fix. The trip rules compare fix times with times read from the phone's clock, and the satellite time inside a fix can differ from it.
- A fix that arrives after the trip has closed is not stored.
- If the location permission is taken away during a trip, Android ends the app; the trip is picked up or closed at the next start.
- A stationary truck with Bluetooth connected still produces GPS jitter, which must not add phantom km.
- The 10 m rule alone does not achieve that. In a simulated hour of parked fixes scattered as a stated accuracy of 5 m implies, 10 m alone added about 360 m, enough to be kept as a trip; with the stated-error rule the same hour adds nothing. So the brief's "10 m" is a floor, not the whole rule. The thresholds are to be tuned from the raw points of the first test drives.
- **The stated-error rule costs distance on turns.** All numbers are simulations, not this phone. On a town route with a right-angle turn every 300 m at about 25 km/h, a fix every 5 seconds alone misses about 2 %. The calculation is short by the same 2 % with 5 m fixes, about 4 % with 10 m fixes and about 11 % with 25 m fixes.
- **A fix every 2 seconds gains on bends taken at speed, and next to nothing on slow turns** (`DistanceCalculatorTurnsTest`; simulations again). With the 4 m fixes Shawn's phone gives for most of a drive, a step is counted only once the truck is 16 m on. At 25 km/h that is every 28 m, where a fix every 5 seconds counted every 35 m: the same route is 1.8 % short instead of 2.2 %. At 50 km/h it is every 28 m where it was every 70 m: 1.7 % short instead of 4.2 %. On an emulator on 2026-10-09 a drive of 2,520 m with a right-angle turn every 400 m at 50 km/h was stored as 94 fixes, 89 of them 2.0 seconds after the one before and the rest 2.5 or 2.6, and measured 2,458 m; the same fixes thinned to every third measured 2,371 to 2,423 m. A real truck slows for a turn, so the gain on the road is smaller than either figure. What it is on the phone has not been measured. A truck circling inside a space smaller than the threshold (a yard, a small roundabout) with fixes of 8 m or worse measures nothing.
- **The end of a trip is short by up to the threshold:** up to 20 m with 5 m fixes, 40 m with 10 m, 100 m with 25 m, because the last stretch never crosses it. A real 350 m trip recorded with poor fixes can therefore come out under the 300 m minimum and be discarded (it is kept as `DISCARDED`, see below).
- **Bad fixes that are still believed** (simulated in review, not seen on this phone). A wrong first fix less than about 700 m from the truth stays as the trip's start position and adds up to its own distance from the truth. One wrong fix straight after a GPS gap is believed if the truck could have got there in the time of the gap, and the way back to the road is counted as well: after a 60-second tunnel, a fix 600 m to the side added 500 m to a 5.3 km drive, and one 2 km to the side added about 1 km. Two bad fixes in a row that agree with each other look like a real there-and-back. Not fixed, because the right cure depends on what this phone's bad fixes look like; the likely one is to hold a fix that follows a gap until the next fix agrees with it.
- Rule 4 has a price: pulling forward and reversing within one fix (shunting in the yard) is not counted.
- A trip under the minimum distance is marked `DISCARDED` and kept with its points, not deleted. If the distance was wrong, the trip is recovered on the Trips screen with "Count this trip" (see [Trip log](#trip-log-home-day-and-month-views)). It is then counted with the distance that was measured; nothing recalculates it, and the edit screen is where a wrong distance is put right by hand.
- After a reboot in the middle of a trip the elapsed-realtime clock restarts, so the distance covered across the reboot cannot be checked and is not counted.
- The phone's own speed reading is stored but not used yet. It is the likely next refinement: for the parked-truck rule in place of stated accuracy (which would end the corner cutting), and to reject a leap from a fix that says it is standing still.
- If the phone's clock was wrong at the start of a trip and corrected during it, the distance is still right (points are cut by stored order, not by time), but the trip's stored start time is the wrong one and can even be later than its end.
- **An address can arrive late.** A trip that ends with no signal, or whose lookup is cut short because the phone puts MilO to sleep once the trip service has stopped, gets its address at the next occasion: the next trip ending, MilO being started, or the Trips screen being opened. Until then the Trips screen says it is still being looked up. The same goes for a trip the restart rules close later than at process start (after the trip service could not be started, or together with the start of a new trip): the lookup never saw it in progress, so it waits for the next occasion, which for a new trip is its first GPS fix.
- **A trip MilO has given up on stays without that address** unless Shawn types one in on the edit screen. Nothing asks again by itself; "Restore recorded values" on an edited trip does start the attempts afresh for an address he had typed.
- **An address is what the geocoder says, not a measurement.** It names the nearest address it knows, which at a large yard or a rural site can be a neighbour's, an "Unnamed Road", or nothing. The stored coordinates remain the record. An address once stored is never replaced by a later lookup; Shawn can replace it on the edit screen, and what he types is kept as he typed it.
- The house number is written before the street, as in Canada, whatever the phone's language.
- The same place can be worded differently on two trips if the first fixes fell on different sides of a lot.
- The start address shown for a trip in progress is held in memory. If the process is restarted in mid-trip it is looked up again.
- A trip discarded as a false start may still have had its start looked up in its first seconds, for the screen. Nothing is stored for it.

---

## Connect and trip-start sounds

**Status:** Two sounds since 2026-10-08. **The connect sound** is the trip-start sound of before, renamed: built, proven on the phone at a trip started with the button (2026-10-05, when it was still a notification sound, and Bedtime mode silenced it). Built, not yet proven on the phone: that it is played as an alarm and heard in the phone's quiet modes (changed on 2026-10-07; device checks NL-70 to NL-74), the sound at a trip the truck starts, and the Settings tile (it ran on an emulator only). **The trip-start sound** (2026-10-08) and the second tile are built and proven with unit tests only: nothing of them has run on an emulator or on the phone (device checks TS-1 to TS-10) · **Platforms:** Android (the sounds play from the trip service whatever screen is up; the settings are on the phone only) · **Last updated:** 2026-10-08

**What it does**
Plays two short sounds, Shawn's audible proof that the automatic part is working:
- **The connect sound,** once, when the app has connected to the truck and is ready to track the trip, or when Start is pressed. Out of the box an R2-D2 style droid chirp.
- **The trip-start sound,** once, when the trip is under way: the first time MilO sees the truck driving, at 15 km/h or more. Out of the box a short rising chime.

Each has its own switch and its own choice of sound: its built-in one, or one of the sounds Shawn added from the phone. The sounds he adds are one list that both choose from (Shawn, 2026-10-08: "i want to add a second sound setting. i want to use the current one for connecting to a vehicle and another custom one when a trip starts. i want to include these sounds that can also be used for either sound").

**Required behaviour**
- **The connect sound** plays once per trip start, at the moment recording has really begun. No sound on trip end. **Not for a trip that starts because a parked truck moved** (since 2026-10-06 (evening); [Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), rule 15): it says "connected to the truck" or answers a press, and such a trip has neither. It plays for a trip started by a connect, by a reconcile or by a button (`TripTransition.tripReallyBegan`).
- **The trip-start sound** plays once per trip, at the first fix of the trip that shows the truck driving (`TripProgress.drivenAtMs`, Shawn's choice "When the truck drives off", over "Right after the connect sound" and "Only when there's no connect"). "Driving" is the parked watch's own test ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), rule 15, `DRIVING_OFF_KMH`): a usable fix whose speed reading is 15 km/h or more; a usable fix without a speed reading counts if distance was counted up to it at that speed from the fix before. Unlike beside a parked truck, one such fix is enough: the answer decides when a sound plays and nothing else. It plays for every kind of trip: after the connect sound at a connect, after the connect sound for a press of Start, and **for a trip a parked truck's moving starts, at once**, because that trip's first points are the fixes that showed it driving off (silent until 2026-10-08).
- **Not twice for one trip.** A trip picked up after a restart that had driven off already plays no second trip-start sound: whether it had is read from its stored points, as everything about an open trip is. A trip picked up before it drove off plays it when it does.
- **A trip the companion callback alone opened waits** until the truck is confirmed, for both sounds; a false start has neither. When the truck is confirmed after the trip was already seen driving, the two play at the confirmation, the connect sound first.
- **One sound at a time, and the second waits.** In the trip service a sound asked for while another plays starts when that one has ended, so a long clip of his own for the connect sound is not cut off by the trip-start sound seconds later. One waits at most; a newer one replaces it.
- Both come from the phone speaker. **Both are heard even when the phone is quiet** (Shawn's decision of 2026-10-06, "Sound: Always play", after he missed the sound once because Bedtime mode was on): on silent, on vibrate, in Do Not Disturb and in Bedtime mode. Until 2026-10-07 the sound behaved like a notification sound and all four silenced it.
- Played by the trip service itself, not attached to a notification channel, because MIUI is reported to switch channel sounds off by default.
- The built-in sounds are original and synthesised: a droid-style chirp for the connect sound and a four-note rising chime for the trip-start sound, made by one script. The film recording the chirp is meant to recall is copyrighted, so it is never committed.
- **Shawn's own clip replaces the chirp in builds made on his Mac.** A file placed at `app/src/debug/res/raw/trip_start_chirp.<ext>` overrides the bundled chirp in debug builds, which are the builds installed on the phone. That folder is git-ignored, so the clip stays on his Mac and his phone and never reaches GitHub. Since 2026-10-05 it holds his R2-D2 clip (4.5 seconds). CI and any other machine build with the synthesised chirp. The event log says "playing the bundled chirp" either way, because it is the sound built into that build. The resource keeps the name `trip_start_chirp` for this reason, although it is now the connect sound's. The trip-start sound's chime (`trip_go_chime`) has no such override.
- A setting to choose any audio file on the phone, without rebuilding. Built on 2026-10-06; since 2026-10-07 a list of his own sounds (Shawn: "I want to add more sound files to choose from"; he chose "A list of my own sounds"); since 2026-10-08 that list serves both sounds, and one file can be both. See "Choosing a sound" below.
- **No recording that belongs to someone else is committed or bundled**, as for the R2-D2 film recording. The five clips Shawn sent on 2026-10-08 (two Mario sounds, the Mario Kart 64 race start, the Super Mario Bros. mushroom and "Giggity") are Nintendo's and Fox's, and the repository and its APK download are public since 2026-10-08; asked, Shawn chose "Add them on the phone" over a git-ignored folder on the Mac. Like the Mario "1-up" sound of 2026-10-07, each is added from the phone with "Add a sound" and lives only there and in MilO's private storage.

**How it works today**
1. **The connect sound:** when the controller has opened a trip row, with the service already in the foreground, it tells the service that a trip has just started. That happens once per trip: not when a trip is picked up after a restart, and not when the grace period is cancelled. One exception: a trip opened by the companion "appeared" callback alone is not yet known to be a trip. Its sound plays when the truck's connection is confirmed, and a false start makes no sound at all (`TripTransition.tripReallyBegan`).
2. **The trip-start sound:** the open trip's running picture (`TripProgress`) notes the time of the first fix that shows the truck driving. After every piece of work, the controller's worker looks whether the open trip shows it for the first time (`TripWorker.noticeDrivingOff`), and if so tells the service, once per trip id. A trip picked up from storage that already shows it is noted as told, without telling.
3. Both go to the service in one order (`TripRecorder.record`, its `sounds`), the connect sound first when the two fall in one step. The service takes down any "could not start this trip" notification, reads the settings once, and plays each sound that is switched on, through one player (`TripSoundPlayer`), in that order.
4. **They are played as an alarm** (since 2026-10-07): with `MediaPlayer` and alarm-type audio attributes (`USAGE_ALARM`). The phone therefore plays them at the **alarm volume**, not the notification volume, and the ringer being on silent or on vibrate does not mute them. Do Not Disturb and Bedtime mode let alarms through as they come; a Do Not Disturb that is set to let nothing through silences them, like every alarm. MilO reads no ringer mode and changes no volume. The player asks for the phone's built-in speaker as its preferred output.
5. If the settings hold a sound of his own for a sound, that is played. It is always MilO's own copy of the file Shawn chose, never the file he picked. If it cannot be opened, or fails while preparing or playing, that sound's bundled one is played instead and the event log says why.
6. The bundled sounds are `res/raw/trip_start_chirp.wav` (1.4 seconds of sine sweeps, warbles and short beeps) and `res/raw/trip_go_chime.wav` (0.73 seconds: C, E, G and the C above, the last held), both generated by `tools/make_trip_start_chirp.py`. The script is committed and produces the same files every time; the build does not run it.
7. The trip service's player waits its turn (item "One sound at a time" above). The Settings screen's Play buttons have a player of their own that cuts off: pressing Play again starts the sound again from the beginning.

*Choosing a sound* (the Settings screen, tiles "Connect sound" and "Trip-start sound", one under the other, laid out as Shawn drew the one tile there was; see [Settings](#settings))
1. **A switch,** at the end of each tile's title; the title, the line under it and the switch are pressed as one. Off means that sound never plays. It does not change which sound is chosen, and it leaves the other sound alone.
2. **The line under the title** names the sound in use: "Built-in chirp" (connect) or "Built-in chime" (trip-start), or the name of the file that was picked ("Your own sound" if the phone gave no name). **A grey line under it says when the sound plays.** **Under it, once he has added a sound, the list:** one row each, the built-in sound first and his own sounds after it in the order they were added, the one in use marked (`ChoiceRow`). The list is the same in both tiles. A press on a row makes it that tile's sound.
3. **Play** plays the sound that tile's moment would play now, through the same kind of player, whether or not its switch is on.
4. **"Add a sound"** opens Android's own file picker, set to audio files. Which tile opened it is remembered, also if Android puts MilO away meanwhile. The file Shawn picks is then, in this order:
   - copied into MilO's private storage (`no_backup/trip_sound/`), under a new name, beside the copies already there. A file over 10 MB, an empty file and a file that cannot be read are refused here. "Cannot be read" includes whatever the app that holds the file answers with: the file is opened by that app's code, and an exception it throws is a refusal like any other, never a crash;
   - handed to Android's player to prepare. A file the player cannot prepare (not audio, or damaged) is refused, and its copy is thrown away;
   - added to the one list of his own sounds in the settings (`SoundListStorage.kt`), and made the sound of the tile it was picked on, and of no other;
   - and only then is any copy that is not on the list removed. There is one copy for each sound on the list.
5. **A refused file changes nothing.** The settings and the copies are exactly as before. The tile it was picked on says why in one sentence ("MilO could not copy that file", "That file is too large for a sound (more than 10 MB)", "That file cannot be played as a sound", each ending "The sound was not changed."), and the event log gets an `ERROR` line naming the sound ("Connect sound: …" or "Trip-start sound: …") with what Android or the file system said. While a file is copied, the buttons of both tiles wait.
6. **"Remove this sound"** is offered while a sound of Shawn's own is in use on that tile. It takes that sound off the list and removes its copy; **each of the two sounds that played it plays its built-in sound from then on.** There is no question first: nothing is lost that cannot be added again from the phone. Going back to the built-in sound without removing anything is a press on its row.
7. Closing the file picker without choosing changes nothing and says nothing.
8. A change that has begun is finished even if Shawn leaves the Settings screen in the middle of it.
9. One grey line, under the second tile, says that both sounds play like an alarm, at the alarm volume, also when the phone is on silent, on vibrate, in Do Not Disturb or in Bedtime mode.

**Where the code lives**
- `core/trip/TripProgress.kt` (`drivenAtMs`, the first fix that shows the truck driving) and `core/trip/ParkedWatch.kt` (`DRIVING_OFF_KMH`, the speed both go by); `TrackPoint.metresPerSecondFrom`, the speed between two fixes, shared by the two.
- `platform/trip/TripWorker.kt` (`noticeDrivingOff`, once per trip) and `TripServiceLink.kt` (the two moments, passed on with the next order); the call in `platform/trip/TripService.kt` (`announce`).
- `platform/trip/TripSoundPlayer.kt` (the player, its turn-taking, `playAsSet`, and `playbackProblem`, the check that a file can be played).
- `platform/trip/OwnTripSound.kt` (choosing a file for a sound, going back, removing, step by step) and `PickedAudio.kt` (reading the picked file through Android's content resolver).
- `data/sound/OwnSoundStore.kt` (MilO's copies: where they are kept, the size limit, one for each sound on the list). `data/settings/SoundListStorage.kt` (`TripSound`, the two sounds and their keys; `SoundChoice` and `sound(which)`; the list and its setters). The keys are in `data/settings/SettingsStore.kt`: `sound_enabled`, `custom_sound_uri` and `custom_sound_name` for the connect sound (the names they had when it was the only sound), `driving_off_sound_enabled`, `driving_off_sound_uri` and `driving_off_sound_name` for the trip-start sound.
- `feature/settings/` (`SoundTile.kt`: one tile for either sound, and the words for a refusal; `SettingsScreen.kt`: the two tiles and the file picker, which remembers its tile). `app/AppContainer.kt` builds `ownTripSound` and `soundPreview`, the player behind the Play buttons.
- The script is `tools/make_trip_start_chirp.py`.
- Tests: `core/trip/TripProgressTest.kt` (driving off: 15 km/h by the speed reading, the fallback from positions, a standing truck and walking pace, a fix too poor to use, the first moment kept); `platform/trip/TripControllerDrivingOffTest.kt` (once per trip, after the connect sound; for a press of Start; not again after a restart; when it drives off after a restart; waiting for a companion trip's confirmation) and `TripControllerWaitingTest.kt` (a parked truck's drive-off has the trip-start sound at once and no connect sound); `platform/trip/OwnTripSoundTest.kt` (refusals change nothing; the list; since 2026-10-08 one list for two sounds: a file for one leaves the other, a sound chosen for both, removed from both, going back on one); `data/settings/SoundListStorageTest.kt` and `SettingsStoreTest.kt` (the two sounds' keys and the list); `platform/transfer/BackupAftermathTest.kt` (after a restore both are forgotten); `feature/settings/SettingsUiStateTest.kt`. The player itself and the file picker have no unit tests: they need Android.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **Nothing of the trip-start sound has run outside unit tests** (2026-10-08): not the moment it plays, not the chime, not the second tile, not a sound waiting its turn behind another. Device checks TS-1 to TS-10.
- **What "driving" is depends on the phone's speed reading.** The fused location provider gives one with nearly every GPS fix in a moving vehicle. Without one the speed is worked out from two fixes, after distance was counted, which needs the truck to have moved further than GPS jitter. A drive that never reaches 15 km/h (a yard, a car park) has no trip-start sound.
- **A trip a parked truck's moving starts can turn out to be a drive in another vehicle** (rule 15): its trip-start sound has played by then, and the trip is removed afterwards.
- **Choosing a sound has run on an emulator only, for one sound** (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06): a WAV file was picked, copied, played by the Play button and at the next trip start, and replaced by a second pick; a text file with an audio name was refused. On the phone it is device checks 117 to 123 and SL-1 onwards. What HyperOS's own file picker looks like, and whether it offers audio files from the places Shawn keeps them, is not known.
- **The copies are not in any backup,** on purpose: they can be megabytes, and Android's backup takes nothing at all from an app that is over its 25 MB limit. So after a restore by Android the settings name copies that are not there. The first start after a restore removes the list and both sounds' own files from the settings ([Backup, export and import](#backup-export-and-import)): the built-in sounds play, and the sounds have to be added again on that phone. An export file does not carry the sounds either, and an import leaves the phone's own as they are. **An export carries the connect sound's switch and not the trip-start sound's** (`[DEBT]`, FINDINGS_LOG 2026-10-08): adding it changes the export file's form; an import leaves this phone's trip-start switch as it is.
- "Can be played" means that Android's player can prepare the file. A file with a video track and no sound in it would pass and play nothing.
- A long file is played to its end. Nothing limits the length, only the size (10 MB). A long connect sound holds the trip-start sound back until it ends.
- **Play is heard as a trip would play it,** and at the same volume. The second tile says in one grey line that the sounds play like an alarm. The event log gets its line either way ("Settings screen, Play pressed. Connect sound: playing …").
- **What still silences them: whatever silences the phone's alarms.** A Do Not Disturb that is set to let nothing through, or whose list of what may sound has "Alarms" switched off. And an alarm volume of zero, on a phone that lets it go that low: the sound is then played and not heard, and the Log says "playing …" all the same. MilO does not check the volume and does not turn it up. (Stock Android keeps the alarm volume at a first step or higher; whether HyperOS does is device check NL-74.)
- **The volume is the alarm volume.** The phone's ring and notification volume do not change how loud the sounds are. If the morning alarm is set loud, so are they.
- **Seen on an emulator (Android 16) on 2026-10-07, with Play, for the one sound there was:** Android reported the sound as an alarm and as not muted with the ringer on silent, with it on vibrate, in Do Not Disturb "priority only" and in "alarms only". In Do Not Disturb "total silence" Android muted it. The emulator's alarm volume could not be set below 1 of 7. **None of this has been on the phone** (device checks NL-70 to NL-74).
- Play uses a player of its own, not the trip service's. If a trip plays a sound while one from Play is still playing, the two overlap.
- In a build made on Shawn's Mac "the built-in chirp" is his R2-D2 clip, because that build has it in place of the chirp (above). The chime is the same in every build.
- The phone's speaker is a preference, not a guarantee: Android may route the sound elsewhere. Where it comes out with the truck connected is a check on the phone.
- The sounds do not lower music that is playing and do not check for a phone call. The research suggests both; neither was asked for.
- **Heard on the POCO X5 once, at a trip started with the button** (2026-10-05, FINDINGS_LOG, "First results from the phone"): Shawn's R2-D2 clip played, then as the only sound. Before that he heard nothing, although the log showed the sound being played without error: the phone was in Bedtime mode. That is what the change of 2026-10-07 is for.
- **On Android 17 the sounds of an automatic start would be silent.** MilO targets API 37, and Android 17 lets such an app play audio in the background only from a foreground service that was started while the app was visible or by the user. A service started by a Bluetooth broadcast, the companion callback or boot is not one, and playback then fails without an error: the log would still say "playing …". The POCO X5 runs Android 14 and is not affected. If MilO ever runs on Android 17, the sounds have to become the sounds of notifications, which the system plays (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-05).
- If the process dies in the 15 seconds a companion start waits to be confirmed, and the truck is confirmed after the restart, that trip makes no connect sound. Its trip-start sound plays when it drives off after the restart; if it had been seen driving before the process died, it has no trip-start sound either.

---

## Safety net: manual button and driving alert

**Status:** Built, proven on the phone: the Start trip button (2026-10-05). Built, not yet proven on the phone: the driving alert, which **has never notified Shawn**: its rules run in unit tests, its registration, Setup row and Settings card ran on an emulator, and the build as it stands has had no report of driving, on an emulator or on the phone. Device checks DA-1 to DA-14 · **Platforms:** Android (phone) · **Last updated:** 2026-10-06

**What it does**
Catches the case where Bluetooth never connects at all.

**Required behaviour**
- A Start/Stop button on the home screen starts or ends a trip by hand.
- During scheduled hours, if the phone's driving detection says Shawn is in a moving vehicle and the truck is not connected, notify him with a tap-to-start action. Needs the Physical activity permission.
- Driving detection never starts a trip by itself, because it would also log rides in other vehicles.
- A manually started trip still respects the schedule: it is classified Business or Personal by its start time, the same as an automatic one. (Built on 2026-10-06: the rule that sorts a trip is not told what started it. See [Schedule and Business/Personal](#schedule-and-businesspersonal).)

**How it works today**

*The button.* The home screen has one button. With no trip open it says "Start trip" and sends the `MANUAL_START` trigger; with a trip open it says "End trip" and sends `MANUAL_END`. Both go through `HomeViewModel` to `TripController.onTrigger`, the same function every other trigger calls, so a manual trip is recorded, closed and logged exactly like an automatic one. Each press reads the truck's connection first; if it cannot be read, the press goes by what was last believed. A manual trip the truck never joins ends on End trip, or after 30 minutes without movement.

The [Android Auto screen](#android-auto-screen) has the same button, under the same words, and sends the same two triggers. A trip started there is the same kind of manual trip. In the event log the two are told apart by the source: "Start button" and "End button" on the phone, "Android Auto Start button" and "Android Auto End button" on the car.

*The driving alert* (since 2026-10-06; phone only).
1. **What it is.** A notification, "You seem to be driving" / "No trip is being recorded. Tap to start one.", on a channel of its own ("Driving alert", high importance). **It never starts a trip.** A trip starts only when the notification is tapped. The tap starts the trip service with the `MANUAL_START` trigger, through the same intent the "could not start this trip" notification uses, so the trip is an ordinary manual trip; its `TRIGGER` line has the source "tap on the driving alert". The code that decides about the alert is given neither the trip controller nor the trip storage, so it has no way to start one.
2. **Where the report comes from.** Google Play services' Activity Recognition (the Transition API, part of the location library MilO already uses). MilO asks to be told when the phone enters and leaves a vehicle, and nothing else. The phone says "a vehicle", never which one, and Google gives no figure for how long it takes to notice: other apps report about a minute, sometimes several. The report is delivered to a receiver in the manifest (`DrivingReceiver`), so it can start MilO's process when MilO is not running.
3. **When the alert is shown** (`judgeDriving` in `platform/driving/DrivingAlertRules.kt`, a pure function). All of these must hold:
   - the newest report says the phone has **entered** a vehicle, and it is at most 5 minutes old;
   - the alert is switched on in [Settings](#settings);
   - no trip is open in storage;
   - **a truck is paired** (a truck's address is stored). Without one there is nothing a trip could be for, and every ride in any vehicle during the work hours would alert;
   - a fresh reading of the truck does not say "connected", **or it does and MilO has stopped watching the truck**: connected, parked for three days, no trip open, the one state in which a connected truck starts nothing when it drives off ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), rule 15; added on 2026-10-06 (evening)). The log line then reads "Alert shown: no trip is being recorded, and the truck is connected but has stood so long that MilO stopped watching it";
   - a trip that started at this moment would be saved as **Business** by the work schedule. That is `classifyTrip`, the rule that sorts a finished trip, so "the work hours" mean the same here as on the Trips screen: on a tracked day, from the stroke of the start time to just before the end time, in the phone's time zone;
   - **no trip ended in the last 5 minutes** (`AFTER_TRIP_QUIET_MS`). The phone takes a minute or more to notice a drive and can report it again, so a report that comes just after a trip is taken to be about the drive that trip recorded. The time is the end time of the newest closed trip as the Trips screen shows it, whatever became of the trip (kept, discarded or deleted). For a trip the truck ended that is its last recorded point up to the moment the truck was found gone, not the end of the grace period, so with the grace period set to 5 minutes or more nothing of the span is left once the trip has closed;
   - **no alert was posted in the last 30 minutes** (`ALERT_QUIET_MS`). The time of the last alert is kept in the settings file, so this holds after MilO's process has been restarted.

   The edges: 4 minutes 59 seconds after a trip's end there is no alert, at 5 minutes there is; 29 minutes 59 seconds after an alert there is no second one, at 30 minutes there is. A stored time that lies ahead of the phone's clock (the clock was set back) silences nothing.
4. **A truck that cannot be read does not stop the alert.** Without the Bluetooth permission, or when Bluetooth gives no answer, the reading is "unknown". The trip rules never start anything on that; the alert is shown on it, because the worst it can cause is one notification, and it is exactly the situation in which no trip starts by itself. That is about a paired truck whose connection cannot be read: **with no truck paired at all there is no alert.**
5. **When it is taken away again:** when it is tapped; when a trip starts being recorded, however it started; when the phone reports having **left** the vehicle; when a later report of entering finds that one of the conditions no longer holds; and when the alert is switched off or the permission is gone. One condition is the exception: a later report of entering that only fails "no alert in the last 30 minutes" changes nothing, so an alert that is still showing stays, and one that has gone is not posted again. It is not taken away by the clock: an alert shown at 16:25 is still there at 16:35 if nothing else happens.
6. **One alert in 30 minutes.** A drive with stops makes the phone report "left" and "entered" again and again, and each "left" takes the alert away; the limit is what keeps each "entered" from sounding it anew. Posted again while it is showing (after the 30 minutes), it makes no second sound. The time is stored when the alert is posted, seen or not; if it cannot be stored, the alert has been posted all the same and the log line says that the next report may bring it up again. A report of entering that is more than 5 minutes old changes nothing either way: nothing promises that a report is fresh, and MilO asks to be told again at every process start. Whether this phone hands over old reports then is device check DA-12.
7. **Asking the phone** (`DrivingAlert.arm`). Reports are asked for while the switch is on and the Physical activity permission is granted, and the request is taken back when the switch goes off. The request is made at every process start, which is what renews it after a reboot and after an update of MilO: both start a new process, and Google does not say that a request outlives either. It is looked at again each time MilO comes to the front (the permission may just have been granted) and when the switch is pressed; the phone is asked only if something has changed since this process last asked.
8. **A report that starts MilO's process** is judged after the trip controller's first look at storage. A trip the last process left open long ago is closed by that look and no longer counts; one that carries on does count, although it is published only seconds later, when the trip service is up again. That is why the stored row is asked and not what the controller publishes. The end of the last trip is read from storage in the same way.
9. **A failure inside the alert stays inside it.** The alert runs in the process the trip service runs in, and a report arrives about a minute into every truck drive, while the trip is being recorded. Each piece of its work (judging a report, asking the phone, taking the notification away for a trip that began) is run so that an exception is written to the event log as one `ERROR` line, "The driving alert failed while …", with its stack trace, and goes no further; the receiver is let go whatever happened. If the event log is what failed, the failure goes to a crash file and reaches the log at a later start (`DrivingFailures`, the pattern of the address lookup).
10. **The event log** has a category of its own, `DRIVING`. One line for every delivery of reports, acted on or not: "Driving detection: entered a vehicle 4 s ago. Alert shown: no trip is being recorded and the truck is not connected", "…No alert: it is outside the work hours", "…No alert: the truck is connected", "…No alert: a trip is being recorded", "…No alert: no truck is paired", "…No alert: a trip ended less than 5 min ago", "…No new alert: the last one was less than 30 min ago", "…The alert is withdrawn if it was showing: the drive is over", "…Not acted on: the report is more than 5 min old and says nothing about now". An alert posted while notifications are off ends "…so nobody saw it". The detail of each line lists every fact the decision went by: the switch, whether a trip is open, whether a truck is paired, the reading of the truck with how it was reached, what the schedule makes of this moment, and how many whole minutes ago the last trip ended and the last alert was posted ("never" if there is none). And one line whenever what MilO asks of the phone changes: "Driving alert: watching for driving (process start)", "Driving alert: not watching for driving (MilO in front): the Physical activity permission is not granted", "…: it is switched off in Settings". A request the phone refuses is an `ERROR` line with the reason, written once and tried again the next time.

**Where the code lives**
- The button: `feature/home/HomeScreen.kt` and `HomeViewModel.kt`; on the car, `platform/car/TripStatusScreen.kt` and `CarAction` in `platform/car/CarScreenContent.kt`. The rules for a manual trip are in `core/trip/` (see [Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection)).
- The alert, in `platform/driving/`: `DrivingAlertRules.kt` (`judgeDriving`, the three spans of time, `vehicleReport`, which reads an event of the phone as "entered" or "left" with its age, `DrivingWatch`), `DrivingAlert.kt` (asks the phone, judges a report, shows and withdraws the notification, stores the time of an alert, writes the log), `DrivingFailures.kt` (keeps a failure of the alert away from the recording), `DrivingDetection.kt` (the request to Play services and its `PendingIntent`), `DrivingReceiver.kt` (the manifest receiver), `DrivingLogText.kt` (the wording of the log lines).
- The two things the alert reads and writes in storage: the end of the newest closed trip (`TripRepository.findNewestTripEndMs`, a read of the `endedAtMs` column) and the time of the last alert (`lastDrivingAlertAtMs` in `data/settings/`).
- The notification and its channel: `platform/trip/TripNotifications.kt` (`showDrivingAlert`, `cancelDrivingAlert`), beside the "could not start" notification whose tap it shares.
- The switch: `feature/settings/SettingsTiles.kt` (`TruckAndAlertTiles`); the stored value is `drivingAlertEnabled` in `data/settings/`. The permission's row: `platform/system/SetupRules.kt` (see [Permission checklist](#permission-checklist)).
- `app/MiloApplication.kt` and `app/MainActivity.kt` call `arm`; `app/AppContainer.kt` builds the alert.
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../platform/driving/`. The rules: `DrivingAlertRulesTest` (every reason for and against, which report counts, the edges of the work hours) and `DrivingAlertLimitsTest` (no truck paired, the 5 and the 30 minutes to the second, how an event of the phone is read). The alert beside a real trip controller on stand-ins: `DrivingAlertReportsTest` (no report ever asks for the trip service or opens a trip; a report waits for the controller's first look at storage), `DrivingAlertQuietTest` (the three limits, across a restart of the process too), `DrivingAlertParkedTest` (no alert beside a parked truck MilO is watching, and one once it has stopped watching), `DrivingAlertWatchTest` (what is asked of the phone and when) and `DrivingAlertFailureTest` (a failure is logged and gets no further). `DrivingLogTextTest` for the wording, `DrivingFixtures.kt` for what they share. The Settings switch storing first and telling the alert second is `app/src/test/.../feature/settings/SettingsViewModelTest.kt`.

**Depends on**
Google Play services (Activity Recognition), the Physical activity permission, the notification permission, the work schedule (see [Schedule and Business/Personal](#schedule-and-businesspersonal)), the paired truck and the reading of its connection, and the stored trips (see [Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection)).

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **This build has had no report.** An emulator cannot be made to report driving, and the phone has not been driven with this build. Everything from "a report arrives" on rests on unit tests and on reading the code: the delivery to the receiver, the notification, its tap. The reviewers of 2026-10-06 did see reports judged, the notification posted and its tap start a trip on an emulator, with the build as it was before their findings were acted on; how the reports were produced there is not recorded (FINDINGS_LOG). Device checks DA-3 to DA-10, DA-13 and DA-14.
- **Nothing happens until a truck is paired.** Without one the alert stays silent, and every report is logged as "No alert: no truck is paired" (device check DA-13). The phone has had the truck paired since the morning of 2026-10-06.
- **A tap on the alert does not run the preflight.** It starts the trip service directly, as a tap on "could not start this trip" does (ADR-002, point 10). A missing location permission is refused by Android and ends in that warning. But with **location switched off for the whole phone** the tap starts a trip that says "Trip in progress" and records nothing; Home's Start button refuses in that state and says why. Seen by a reviewer on an emulator, and left as it is: `[DEBT]` in FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, with the ways out.
- **Whether a report can start MilO on HyperOS is unknown.** The delivery names MilO's receiver directly, which ADR-002 noted might get past the Autostart gate; the research thinks it likely needs Autostart like every other start of a dead app. Nothing has tested it (ADR-002, amendment 27; device checks DA-8 and DA-9). If it cannot, the alert only works while MilO's process is alive, which is when Bluetooth is least likely to have failed.
- **The alert is late by design.** The phone needs a minute or more of driving to notice, so a trip started from the alert misses the beginning of the drive. It starts when the notification is tapped; nothing is added for the distance already driven.
- **It fires in any vehicle:** a colleague's car, a bus, a taxi during the work hours. That is why it only notifies. The switch in Settings is the way to silence it.
- **In stop-and-go traffic the phone may report leaving and entering a vehicle again.** The "left" takes the alert away, and for 30 minutes after the alert a new "entered" does not bring it back. So after the first stop of a drive the alert may be gone although the drive goes on, and it does not return within the half hour. Device check DA-10 counts what this phone does.
- **The 30 minutes also cover the next drive.** An alert at 10:00 on a ride in a colleague's car means that a drive in the truck at 10:20, on a day Bluetooth fails, brings no alert before 10:30. And the phone reports entering a vehicle once for a drive, so a drive that began inside the 30 minutes gets no alert at all unless the phone reports again. Thirty minutes is what was asked for; the figure is one constant, `ALERT_QUIET_MS`.
- **The 5 minutes after a trip can hide a real drive** that begins within 5 minutes of the last trip's end on a day Bluetooth fails for the second drive and not the first (device check DA-14 is the ordinary case).
- **The alert's sound is the phone's, not MilO's.** It is an ordinary notification on a high-importance channel. MIUI is reported to switch the sound of an app's channels off by default (`docs/research/2026-10-03-miui-dev-bluetooth-audio.md`, finding 33), so on this phone it may arrive silently until the channel is set in the phone's settings (device check DA-5).
- **A report older than 5 minutes is ignored,** which also drops a real one that reaches MilO that late. The limit is a judgement (`MAX_DRIVING_REPORT_AGE_MS`), and every ignored report is in the log with its age.
- **Settings that cannot be read do not silence it:** the alert then runs as it does out of the box (switched on, Monday to Friday 08:00 to 16:30), and the detail of each line says so.
- **A trip that is open but not being recorded counts as a trip.** If Android refused to restart the trip service for a stored trip, no alert is shown; the "could not start this trip" notification is the one that speaks then.
- **Beside a parked truck that MilO is still watching there is no alert:** the truck reads "connected", and its moving starts the trip. The report does something else there (since 2026-10-07): after the first hour of the wait it turns GPS on again for 10 minutes, and the fixes decide whether the truck is driving off ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), rule 15). The receiver hands a fresh "entered a vehicle" to the trip controller for that before the alert judges it (`enteredVehicleAtMs`); the report starts no trip by itself. Switched off in Settings, the alert asks the phone for no reports, so GPS then stays on for the whole wait. Only once the three days of rule 15 are over is the alert shown for a connected truck, and then only during the work hours, like every alert. That state is known to the process that reached it and to no other: after a restart a reconcile starts a trip instead.
- **The alert does not know about the hold-off.** After End trip with the truck still connected the truck reads "connected", so there is no alert. After End trip on a manual trip without the truck there is none for 5 minutes; after that a report of entering a vehicle shows it, unless the last alert was less than 30 minutes ago.
- **An older build must not be installed over this one once a `DRIVING` line is in the log** (ARCHITECTURE section 6).

---

## Android Auto screen

**Status:** Built. **Seen running for the first time on 2026-10-09, in Google's desktop emulator of a car display** (the Desktop Head Unit, installed on the Mac that day), with Shawn's phone behind it: the three-row screen of that day was listed, opened, showed the status and the button, and came back by itself after MilO was updated under it. Shawn's verdict: "this needs some tlc". The two-row screen that replaced it the same evening **was seen there too** (21:19): the header, both rows with their lime icons, "Today 63.9 km · October 202.5 km, $142" on one line, the lime button, and nothing to scroll. Its button was not pressed, and no trip was open. Never on the truck. Device checks 88 to 100, and AA-1 to AA-6 · **Platforms:** Android Auto (projected from the phone) · **Last updated:** 2026-10-09

**Kilometres or miles (2026-10-07, evening).** The "This trip" and "Today" rows write their distance in the unit chosen in Settings ("7.7 mi · 23 min", "4 trips · 17.9 mi"), and a change of the unit is drawn at once. The unit is part of the screen's content (`CarScreenContent.unit`), so it does not wait for the 15 seconds the running figures wait. In kilometres the rows read as they always did. See [Units](#units-kilometres-or-miles).

**Google Play (2026-10-09, ADR-004).** Shawn chose to keep the screen when MilO goes to Google Play ("i want the android auto screen"). A copy from Google Play comes from a trusted store, which is what Android Auto needs to show the screen in a car; Google reviews the screen first, and it is declared as IoT, the closest of the categories the library is open to. Whether the review accepts it is not known.

**What it does**
Shows MilO on the truck's Android Auto display, built with the Car App Library (`androidx.car.app`). Shawn asked for it so he can see more easily that the app is running, and override it without touching the phone. It is in phase 1 because that visibility matters most during the first test drives.

**Required behaviour**
- Shows tracking status, the current trip's km and duration, and what Home shows of today and the month. Until 2026-10-09 it showed today's session count and total km instead; that evening Shawn, looking at it in the desktop head unit, asked for three things: "it needs to be on one screen no scrollable", "it should have all the stuff on the home screen less todays trips", and "make it similiar to the widget".
- Start Trip and End Trip buttons act as a manual override. They drive the same trip logic as the phone's Start/Stop button, and a trip started here still respects the schedule (it is sorted into Business or Personal when it ends, like any other; see [Schedule and Business/Personal](#schedule-and-businesspersonal)).

**The known risk, before anything else**
Google's documentation says two things. Android Auto's "Unknown sources" developer setting covers media apps, messaging notifications and parked apps, and "doesn't apply to apps built using the Android for Cars App Library". And to test an app in a real vehicle, "you must install it from a trusted source such as Google Play". MilO is installed from Android Studio. So by Google's own words this screen is expected to show in the desktop emulator of a car display and **not on the truck**. Reports from other people conflict, and one unanswered report says sideloaded apps do not show on a POCO X5 Pro. Shawn tests it on the truck (device checks 88 to 100). If MilO is not listed there, there are three choices:
1. **A private Google Play install.** Play's "internal app sharing" or an internal test track delivers MilO to the phone with no review and no public listing, and Android Auto then treats it as trusted. It needs a Play Console account (25 US dollars once, with identity verification). Play signs its copy with its own key, so that copy and a build from Android Studio cannot update each other: changing over means uninstalling, which deletes the trips unless they are exported first. It also ends "never on the Play Store".
2. **A media-app style entry.** "Unknown sources" does allow a sideloaded media app on a real car. MilO would appear as a media app, with the status as its "now playing" text and Start and End as its buttons. A workaround, and not the screen that was asked for.
3. **Dropping the screen.** Everything else works without it: the rule "do not end the trip while Android Auto is connected" does not depend on it, and the phone's notification still shows the trip.
Unofficial tools that pretend an app came from Play exist. They are untrusted software from strangers and the reports on them are mixed, so they are not one of the choices. Source for all of this: `docs/research/2026-10-03-android-auto-screen.md`, findings 8 to 12.

**How it works today**
1. **One screen, never another.** A car allows an app five steps and then closes it. A redraw is a harmless "refresh", and not a step, only while the header's title, the number of rows and every row's title stay exactly the same. So the screen is one pane with the header "MilO" and always two rows, titled **This trip** and **Business**, each with a small icon in the app's lime. Whatever changes is written under a title, never into it, and nothing is ever opened on top of this screen. **Two rows, so that it fits one screen:** on the smallest display a car may have (800 by 480, the desktop head unit's) two rows and the button fit, and the three rows it had until 2026-10-09 did not, so the car put scroll arrows beside them. **The layout is Google's, not MilO's:** an Android Auto app chooses one of the library's templates, and the car decides the type, the sizes and most of the colours. Shawn was shown two drawings that evening, a grid of tiles and "two lines and a big button", and chose the second.
2. **The status line** is the "This trip" row's words while no trip is open, and the start of them while one is. The home-screen widget prints the same line. The first of these that applies is shown:

   | The line | When |
   |---|---|
   | Truck disconnected. Waiting for it to reconnect | A trip is open and in its grace period |
   | Recording. Truck not connected | A trip is open and the trip rules believe the truck's Bluetooth is not connected: a trip started by hand that the truck has not joined, or one that Android Auto alone is holding open |
   | Recording | A trip is open |
   | Truck parked. A trip starts when it moves | No trip: the truck stood still, so its trip was ended, and it is still connected. MilO is watching it ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), rule 15) |
   | Could not start: (why) | No trip, and the last attempt to start one was refused. The reason is the first thing the preflight found (location permission, "Allow all the time", location switched off, battery set to Restricted, Nearby devices), or "Android did not allow it" |
   | Not recording. MilO's setup is incomplete | No trip, and a required row of the setup checklist is not in order (the home screen's rule, `needsAttention`) |
   | Truck parked a long time. Press Start trip | No trip: the truck is connected and has stood for three days, so MilO has stopped watching it |
   | Not recording. Truck not connected | No trip, and the trip rules believe the truck is not connected |
   | Not recording | No trip, and nothing above applies: ended by hand with the truck still connected, or MilO has only just started |

3. **This trip** is the status line alone while no trip is open ("Truck parked. A trip starts when it moves"), and while one is, the status followed by the kilometres to one decimal and the time since the trip started in whole minutes: "Recording · 12.4 km · 23 min", or "Recording · 12.4 km · 1 h 5 min".
4. **Business** is one line of what Home's tiles show: "Today 17.9 km · October 412.3 km, $289". **Today** and the month are the Business trips only, as on Home's two tiles, picked and added up by the same functions (`todayTrips`, `categoryTotals` in `data/trip/TripTotals.kt`): each trip rounded to a tenth first, a discarded or deleted trip not counted, the trip in progress not counted until it ends. Until 2026-10-09 the row was "Today" and counted every finished trip, Business and Personal together. **The dollars** are the month's Business kilometres at the one rate set in Settings, in whole dollars, as the widget prices them (`allowanceCents`; always from kilometres, whichever unit is shown); they are left out while the rate cannot be read. **The odometer is not on the car.** Shawn asked for everything on Home but today's trips, and the first build of this row ended "· Odometer 102,684 km"; in the head unit that made the line wrap, and the car put its scroll arrows back. The line is kept to what fits the smallest display with a busy month's figures in it, and the truck's own dashboard shows the odometer. "Not available" until storage has answered, if it could not be read, and for the moment after the unit was changed in Settings.
5. **One button,** in the app's lime if the car allows it (the car may paint a button in a colour of its own). "Start trip" while no trip is open, "End trip" while one is, the grace period included. It sends `MANUAL_START` or `MANUAL_END` to `TripController.onTrigger`, exactly as the phone's button does. It works while the truck is moving: it is an ordinary button, not one of the library's parked-only ones. A press does what the button said when it was drawn.
6. **Where it reads from.** The screen has no ViewModel and decides nothing. It follows the trip controller's `TripController.activity` (the trip, why the last start failed, and what the trip rules believe about the truck), the trips that started this month (`TripRepository`), the stored settings (the rate; read only), the shared setup checklist, and the clock. Two pure functions, `businessFigures` and `carScreenContent`, turn them into what is shown.
7. **When it redraws.** Only while the car is showing the screen, and only when what is printed has changed: kilometres are compared after rounding to one decimal and the time after rounding down to whole minutes. The running figures of a trip are redrawn at most every 15 seconds. Everything else (the status, the button, a trip starting or ending, the Business row) is drawn at once.
8. **A start that is refused** changes the Status line to "Could not start: (why)", which stays until a trip does start. If it happens while the screen is showing, the car also shows a short message, "MilO could not start this trip". The reason is in the event log twice: the trip controller's own `SERVICE` line ("Could not start recording for Android Auto Start button: MANUAL_START: BACKGROUND_LOCATION_MISSING"), and an `ANDROID_AUTO` line saying that the car screen said so. The phone gets its "could not start this trip" notification as for any other start.
9. **Who may connect.** Android Auto reaches the screen through a service that has to be open to other apps. What it lets in is checked by the fingerprint of the app's signing certificate against the list the Car App Library ships: Android Auto itself, and Google's host for cars that run Android themselves. Any other app is turned away. The same list is used in every build.
10. **What it writes to the event log,** all as `ANDROID_AUTO` lines: "car app service created, a host is connecting"; "session created by (the host's package name), Car API level (the level agreed)"; "shown on the car's display"; "no longer shown"; "session destroyed"; "car app service destroyed"; and "said that the trip could not start". Each press of the button is the trip controller's own `TRIGGER` line, with "Android Auto Start button" or "Android Auto End button" as its source and the state before and after in its detail.
11. **If the screen's own code fails,** the failure is written to the log as an `ERROR` line and MilO closes itself on the car's display. An exception left alone would end the whole process, and that is the process recording the trip.

**Where the code lives**
- `platform/car/MiloCarAppService.kt`: the service Android Auto binds, and the check of who may connect.
- `platform/car/MiloCarSession.kt`: one visit of Android Auto. Builds the screen and writes the session's lines to the log.
- `platform/car/TripStatusScreen.kt`: the screen. Follows the four sources, redraws, builds the pane.
- `platform/car/CarScreenContent.kt`: `carScreenContent` (what is shown), `businessFigures` (the Business row, from the month's trips and the rate), `CarStatus`, `CarAction`, and `differsOnlyInTripFigures` (which changes wait for the 15 seconds). Pure functions.
- `res/drawable/ic_car_trip.xml` and `ic_car_business.xml`: the two rows' icons, and `milo_car_accent` in `res/values/colors.xml`: their colour and the button's. They are resources, not the design system's Compose icons and colours, because the car draws the screen outside Compose; `NotificationAccentTest` keeps the colour equal to the theme's accent.
- `data/trip/TripTotals.kt`: `todayTrips` (what counts as today), shared with the phone's home screen since 2026-10-06.
- `platform/trip/TripActivity.kt`: `truckConnected`, added for this screen.
- `core/util/TimeSpan.kt`: `daySpan`, today as a span of stored time. `core/util/DurationFormat.kt`: `wholeHoursAndMinutes`, the time since a trip started.
- The manifest declares the service (category `androidx.car.app.category.IOT`), the car app descriptor `res/xml/automotive_app_desc.xml` (a "template" app) and the lowest Car API level the screen works with, 7. The words are in `strings.xml` under "The Android Auto screen".
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../platform/car/CarScreenContentTest.kt` (every state: no trip, recording, the grace period, a parked truck waited for and one no longer watched, truck not connected, setup incomplete, each kind of refused start; the figures; the button), `app/src/test/.../data/trip/TripTotalsTest.kt` (what counts as today), `CarScreenRefreshTest.kt` (what is redrawn at once and what waits), `MiloCarSessionTest.kt` (the session's log line), `app/src/test/.../platform/trip/TripActivityTest.kt` (what is reported about the truck) and `app/src/test/.../core/util/TimeSpanTest.kt` (a day). The service, the session and the screen themselves have no tests: they need a car, and there is no Robolectric (STANDARDS §11).

**Depends on**
`androidx.car.app:app` and `app-projected` 1.8.0-rc01 (a release candidate since 2026-10-06, see the gotchas; 1.7.0 before), and `androidx.lifecycle:lifecycle-runtime`. The Android Auto app on the phone, which is what draws the screen and decides the Car API level. The trip controller, the trip repository and the setup checklist.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **It has run in the desktop head unit only.** On 2026-10-09 the head unit listed MilO, opened it and drew first the three-row screen and then the two-row one from Shawn's phone, with the lime kept on the icons and the button. The button was not pressed (it would have started a real trip in his log), and no trip was open, so the screen during a trip has not been seen. For a moment after the screen opens, the head unit draws a thin scroll bar at the right edge, which fades; no arrows, and nothing below the button to scroll to. Whether the truck lists MilO, and how the screen looks on the truck's display: device checks 88 to 100 and AA-1 to AA-6.
- **How to see it at the desk.** The Desktop Head Unit is at `~/Library/Android/sdk/extras/google/auto/desktop-head-unit` (version 2.0, from Google's SDK repository). On the phone, once: Android Auto's settings, Version, tap "Version and permission info" ten times, then in the three-dot menu "Developer settings", "Unknown sources" on (a copy of MilO installed from the Mac is not from a trusted store). Each time: three-dot menu, "Start head unit server", phone on the cable and unlocked; on the Mac `adb -s <the phone> forward tcp:5277 tcp:5277`, then run `desktop-head-unit`. Its console takes `screenshot <file>`. A connection does not always hold at the first try: of 2026-10-09's, one was ended by the phone after a few seconds ("ByeBye") and two more dropped at once ("Failed to read from transport"), each time with the next or the third try holding; the head unit also drops when MilO is updated under it. It is the phone's real MilO: Start trip there starts a real trip.
- **A long status with a trip's figures may wrap.** "Truck disconnected. Waiting for it to reconnect · 12.4 km · 23 min" is longer than a line of the smallest display. The row then takes a second line of text, which the car allows; whether the button still fits without scrolling in that state has not been seen.
- **"Truck not connected" is what the trip rules believe, not a fresh look at the truck.** While no trip is open nothing reads the truck's connection unless a Bluetooth event arrives or MilO is opened on the phone. If MilO missed the truck connecting, the car says "Not recording. Truck not connected" while the truck is connected. That is the moment the button is for: a press of Start trip reads the truck first. Opening MilO on the car's display does not make the trip controller read the truck, the way opening it on the phone does; whether it should is Shawn's to decide (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-05).
- For up to 15 seconds after the companion service alone has started a trip, the line is "Recording. Truck not connected": the truck is not confirmed yet.
- **The lowest Car API level is 7.** The header is built with the library's current `Header` class, which needs level 7. The level belongs to the Android Auto app on the phone, not to the truck, and Google does not publish which level which version speaks. An Android Auto too old for level 7 would not open MilO at all; the log would then show "car app service created" with no "session created" after it. A host that failed the certificate check leaves the same trace; the library's own Logcat lines (their tags begin with `CarApp`) tell the two apart.
- If Google changes Android Auto's signing certificate, the list in a pinned library goes out of date and MilO is turned away until the library is updated.
