- **The library is a release candidate, on purpose.** Until 2026-10-06 MilO was on 1.7.0, the newest stable release, and one release behind a security fix: the release notes of 1.8.0-rc01 say it includes one, without naming it, and tell every lower version to update. It matters here because the check of who may connect (9 above) is the library's code, and the service behind it is open to every app on the phone. On 2026-10-06 Shawn decided to take 1.8.0-rc01 as a one-time exception to the stable-only rule (ADR-001, "Exception, 2026-10-06"). No stable 1.8.0 existed that day. **The stable 1.8.0 replaces it the day it is released**; until then Dependabot is set to pass over this library's other pre-releases (the 1.9.0 alphas, and a later release candidate of 1.8.0), and no other version of it exists. Nothing of the screen's code changed, and the classes it is built from hold the same code in both versions. The one difference found in the check of who may connect: 1.7.0 also let in any app that holds a permission meant for cars that run Android themselves; 1.8.0-rc01 does that only on such a car, so on the phone only Android Auto and Google's host are let in (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06 (evening, decisions)).
- **A refused start that repeats exactly** (the same reason again) shows no second message: the Status line already says it and does not change.
- A Status line that says "Could not start" stays after the cause was put right, until the next trip starts. The phone's home screen does the same.
- **Android Auto decides how long the screen lives.** Google's own reference host drops an app about three minutes after the driver leaves it; what Android Auto does on this phone is not documented, and the log lines are how it will be learned. Nothing depends on the session: the trip is recorded by the trip service whether or not the screen exists.
- **Being shown on the car does not count as being in front for location.** A trip started from the car's button is started like one started by the truck: it needs "Allow all the time", and battery use unrestricted or the companion association. Without them the start is refused, and the screen says so.
- Opening MilO on the car when its process is dead starts the process, which reads the truck as every process start does. Whether HyperOS lets Android Auto start a dead MilO with Autostart off is unknown (device check 99).
- The setup checklist is read each time the car shows the screen. A setting changed while the screen stays up shows only after one of MilO's phone screens has read it again.
- The button has no icon and there is one button, not two. Google's design guide prefers an icon on every button and frowns on a button that changes its label; one button was the brief.
- The category `IOT` is the least wrong of the library's categories (none is for logging trips). It decides which rules Google would review the app against, and nothing is reviewed for an app that is not on Play.
- `todayTrips` was in `platform/car/` while the car screen was its only user. Since the home screen counts today's trips too it is in `data/trip/TripTotals.kt`, and both surfaces call that one function.
- The rule "do not end the trip while Android Auto is connected" does not depend on this screen and works either way: `platform/car/AndroidAutoWatcher.kt` watches `CarConnection` during a trip, and while MilO waits beside the parked truck (see [Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), rules 13 and 15). Outside a trip a second watcher of the same class, held by `AndroidAutoLog`, writes each change to the event log and does nothing else ([Diagnostics and reminders](#diagnostics-and-reminders), "Android Auto outside a trip").

---

## Home-screen widget

**Status:** Built; **seen working on the phone** (Shawn, 2026-10-08: "i teset it and it works"). Which of its checks, W-1 to W-14, he went through was not written down, so they stay open one by one · **Platforms:** Android (the phone's home screen; Android Auto has no widgets) · **Last updated:** 2026-10-08

**Kilometres or miles (2026-10-07, evening).** The open trip's distance and the small unit after it follow the unit chosen in Settings ("0.9 mi"), at once. **The dollars do not:** they are worked out from kilometres at the rate per kilometre, in either unit, and the line still ends "at $0.70/km". With Miles chosen, the tile in Settings adds one grey line under the rate, for reading only: "$0.70 per km is $1.13 per mile". See [Units](#units-kilometres-or-miles).

**What it does**
A widget for the phone's home screen. Shawn's requests of 2026-10-07: "add the ability to add a widget to the home screen with the dollar amount for business activity. this is just for reference. please use the cra value for this number. add the ability to turn it on and off in app", and "add the ability to view the current trip in this widget and start stop a trip". And the same evening: "lets just use .70 for a blended rate", then "or let the person set it under the settings screen".

**Required behaviour** (Shawn's answers the same day)
- The dollars are **this month's and this year's** Business kilometres ("This month and this year").
- **One rate for every kilometre, set in Settings, $0.70 out of the box** ("Settings, starts at 0.70", the evening of 2026-10-07). It replaced the CRA's two tiers by year, built in ("Built in, updated each year", the same afternoon), which were never on the phone.
- **The switch in Settings is for the whole widget** ("The whole widget"): switched off, it cannot be added, and one already on the home screen says it is switched off.
- **End ends the trip at once** ("No, end at once"), with no question first.
- Not on the report: the kickoff's "No rate and no amount owed" stands for the report ([Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission)); the widget's figure is for reference only.

**How it works today**
1. **What it shows,** top to bottom, on a tile in the app's colours (three cells by two out of the box, and resizable): the app's mark and the **status line**, which is the Android Auto screen's own line ([Android Auto screen](#android-auto-screen), step 2: "Recording", "Truck parked. A trip starts when it moves", "Not recording. Truck not connected", …); while a trip is open, **its kilometres** in large figures and **its running time** beside them (as the phone's clock shows a stopwatch: "23:05", "1:02:41"); **the dollars**, "Oct $412 · 2026 $3,980 at $0.70/km"; and **one button**, "Start trip" in the accent while no trip is open, "End trip" in the quiet fill while one is, the grace period included. A tap anywhere but the button opens MilO.
2. **Start** starts the trip service directly, as the tap on the "could not start" notification does, so it works from the home screen with MilO closed: a press on a widget is one of the moments Android lets an app start a foreground service from the background (ADR-002, amendment 35). **End** goes to a receiver of MilO's own, which hands it to the trip controller. Both are the app's `MANUAL_START` and `MANUAL_END`, so every rule of the buttons holds: Start beside the parked truck begins the trip at the parked place, End with the truck connected holds automatic start off, and so on ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection)). The event log names them "home-screen widget Start button" and "home-screen widget End button".
3. **The dollars.** The Business kilometres are added up as every total in MilO is (`categoryTotals`: finished trips saved as Business, each rounded to a tenth first, so they agree with the Trips screen and the report). A trip that is open, deleted, discarded, Personal or not yet sorted is not priced. Every kilometre is priced at the same rate, the one set in Settings, however many came before it in the year. Each figure is rounded once, to whole dollars, and the line names the rate it used.
4. **The rate** is set on the widget's tile in Settings, in dollars a kilometre to the cent, from $0.01 to $5.00 ([Settings](#settings), tile 10), and stored in the settings file in cents (`home_widget_cents_per_km`). **Since 2026-10-09 it is not the widget's alone:** Home's month tile and the Android Auto screen price the month at it too, so the tile shows the rate whether or not the widget is switched on, with one line under it: "The month on Home and the Android Auto screen use this rate too." Until one is set it is $0.70. A new rate reprices this month and this year at once, the months before included: there is one rate, not one per period. If the settings cannot be read, the dollar line says "Business dollars: not available".
5. **When it is drawn.** While MilO's process runs (which is always while a trip is recorded or MilO waits beside the parked truck), it follows the trip controller and this year's trips: a change of the status, the button or the dollars is drawn at once; the running kilometres at most every 15 seconds, as on the car; the running time is advanced by the home screen itself. The month is looked at every hour, so the turn of a month shows within the hour. When the process is not running, the home screen asks for a drawing when the widget is added, after a reboot and every three hours, and MilO draws it from storage.
6. **The switch** (Settings, the tile after the two sounds; see [Settings](#settings), tile 10). **On** (out of the box): the widget is in the phone's list of widgets, and the tile's "Add to home screen" asks the home screen to add it. **Off:** every widget on the home screen is redrawn as "MilO's widget is switched off. Turn it on in MilO, Settings." with no button, and then the widget's provider is disabled, which takes it out of the list. Android then removes the widgets of a disabled provider; what a home screen shows in the meantime is the home screen's (HyperOS: device check W-9). Switched on again, the widget is offered again and has to be added again. The switch is stored first and applied after, and applied again at every process start, so a restored settings file takes effect.
7. **If something fails,** the widget keeps its last drawing and an `ERROR` line says "The home-screen widget failed while reading this year's trips" (or "… the settings"). Without this year's trips, or without the rate, the dollar line says "Business dollars: not available"; the trip and the button are still drawn.

**Where the code lives**
- `core/allowance/BusinessAllowance.kt`: the rate out of the box and its limits (`DEFAULT_CENTS_PER_KM`, `MIN_CENTS_PER_KM`, `MAX_CENTS_PER_KM`), the pricing (`allowanceCents`), reading a typed rate (`parseCentsPerKm`), and writing a rate and whole dollars (`formatCentsPerKm`, `formatWholeDollars`). Pure Kotlin.
- `platform/widget/HomeWidgetContent.kt`: `homeWidgetContent`, what is shown, from the controller's state, this year's trips and the rate; it calls the car screen's `carScreenContent` for the status, the trip and the button. `yearSpanOf`. Pure.
- `platform/widget/HomeWidgetViews.kt`: the drawing as `RemoteViews`, and the three `PendingIntent`s (Start, End, open MilO).
- `platform/widget/HomeWidget.kt`: keeps every widget in step while the process runs, draws on the home screen's requests, applies the switch, asks the home screen to add one.
- `platform/widget/HomeWidgetReceivers.kt`: `HomeWidgetProvider` (the widget as Android knows it) and `HomeWidgetActionReceiver` (End).
- `app/WidgetObjects.kt`: the part of the container that builds `HomeWidget`, and the Settings tile's ViewModel factory. `app/MiloApplication.kt` starts the widget after the reconcile at every process start.
- `feature/settings/HomeWidgetTile.kt` and `HomeWidgetViewModel.kt`: the tile. `data/settings/WidgetStorage.kt`: the switch and the rate in the settings file.
- Resources: `res/layout/widget_home.xml`, `widget_off.xml`, `res/xml/home_widget_info.xml`, `res/drawable/widget_*.xml`, the words in `res/values/strings_widget.xml`, and the colours `milo_widget_*` in `res/values/colors.xml`, each the theme's own colour (`WidgetColorsTest` keeps them equal). The manifest declares the provider and the receiver.
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../core/allowance/BusinessAllowanceTest.kt` (70¢ out of the box, one rate past 5,000 km, rounding, what a typed rate may be, writing it, whole dollars), `platform/widget/HomeWidgetContentTest.kt` (the month and the year priced and the rate named, which trips count, another rate, no trips known, no rate known, an open trip, the year's span), `feature/settings/HomeWidgetViewModelTest.kt` (the tile out of the box, without the home screen's requests, stored then applied, not stored; a rate stored, refused, not stored), `data/settings/WidgetStorageTest.kt` (the switch; the rate out of the box, stored, a stored value out of range, a rate out of range refused), `core/designsystem/theme/WidgetColorsTest.kt`.

**Depends on**
Android's `AppWidgetManager` and the home screen app, which draws the widget. No library: the widget is plain `RemoteViews`, not Jetpack Glance, so nothing was added to the build.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **Nothing here has run.** Whether HyperOS's home screen lists it, adds it from the button, draws it in MilO's colours, lets its Start start a stopped MilO, and what it does with it when it is switched off: device checks W-1 to W-14.
- **The rate is Shawn's own figure,** not the CRA's: $0.70 is the blended rate Shawn chose in place of the CRA's 2026 tiers (73¢ for a year's first 5,000 km, 67¢ after). What can be deducted or claimed depends on how Shawn is paid and on the actual costs, which is the accountant's to say. That is why the figure is for reference only and is not on the report.
- **A change of rate reprices the whole year,** the months already shown included: the widget keeps one rate, not a history of them.
- **The rate is not in "Export all data".** Android's backup carries it with the settings file; an import from a file leaves this phone's rate as it is (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-07 (evening), `[DEBT]`).
- **The dollars move when a trip ends,** not while it is recorded (an open trip is not counted), and when a trip is edited, deleted, restored or sorted.
- **A process started by the home screen draws first what it knows at once:** if MilO had been ended in the middle of a trip, the widget can say "Not recording" for the moment until the reconcile has picked the trip up again, and then redraws.
- **At the turn of a month** the figure moves on within the hour while the process runs, and within three hours (the home screen's next request) while it does not.
- **The running time is the phone's stopwatch,** not the car's "23 min": the home screen advances it every second without MilO.
- **A home screen that takes no requests to add a widget** gets no "Add to home screen" button; the line that says how to add it by hand is always there.
- **Switched off, a widget can linger:** a home screen that keeps a removed widget keeps its last drawing, which is why it is redrawn as switched off, without a button, before the provider goes.
- The widget has no today's trips line (the car's "Today"): the brief for it was the trip and the dollars.

## First-start onboarding

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone, and **never run anywhere**: built and unit tested in CI only. Its checks are OB-1 to OB-7 · **Platforms:** Android (the phone; the Android Auto screen is not touched) · **Last updated:** 2026-10-09

**What it does**
The first time MilO opens, a page says what MilO does and how it works, with OK at its end; OK opens Setup, which then has a button **"Done, go to Settings"** at its end; Done opens Settings. After that the app opens as it always has. Shawn's request of 2026-10-08: "when the app starts for the first time i want a onboarding. What the app does and how it works. than a ok. than go to the setup screen so the user can set up all settings for vehicle. once finished please send them to the settings screen."

**Required behaviour** (Shawn's answers the same day)
- **Phone only** ("Phone only"): the Android Auto screen is not changed.
- **One page, then OK** ("One page, then OK", over a few pages with Next).
- **Setup is finished when Done is pressed** ("A 'Done' button", over moving on by itself once every required row is ready): Done can be pressed with rows still to fix, and Home's warning goes on saying so. Moving on by itself would have kept anyone on Setup whose HyperOS rows MilO cannot read.
- **A phone that already has MilO sees it once,** after the update that brings it ("Yes, once"), not only a fresh install.

**How it works today**
1. **The stage** is stored in the settings file as `first_run_stage` (`data/settings/OnboardingStorage.kt`): nothing until OK (`INTRO`), `setup` after OK, `done` after Done. A value a build does not know is read as done.
2. **The page** (`feature/onboarding/OnboardingScreen.kt`) is drawn by `MiloApp` in place of every other screen, without the bottom bar, while the stage is `INTRO`. It scrolls: the top line with the app's mark and "Welcome"; the one accent tile, "MilO logs your business driving by itself" over what MilO keeps; then a plain tile for each thing to know, a title over a few sentences: "How a trip starts and ends", "Business or Personal", "Each month", "Your trips stay on this phone", "Next"; and the main button **OK**, which since 2026-10-09 stays at the bottom of the screen while the tiles scroll (Shawn: "pin the bottom where it says click next or continue so it shows all the time"). Made of the design system's parts (`AppHeader`, `Tile`, `PinnedButtonColumn`, `PrimaryButton`); `PinnedButtonColumn` was added for it.
3. **OK** stores `setup` and opens Setup on top of Home, so Back from Setup leads to Home.
4. **While the stage is `setup`**, Setup has the main button "Done, go to Settings" at the bottom of the screen, in view while the rows scroll (since 2026-10-09; until then after the rows and the HyperOS note), however Setup was opened: from OK, from Home's warning, or from the tile at the top of Settings. **Done** stores `done` and opens Settings as the bar's Settings button does (Home under it).
5. **Back on the page leaves MilO,** as Back from Home does; the page shows again at the next start. Back from Setup keeps the stage at `setup`: Setup keeps its Done button until Done is pressed.
6. **Nothing keeps anyone on the page.** A stage reached counts at once, whether or not it could be stored; one that could not be is an `ERROR` line in the Log ("MilO could not store how far the first start has got (SETUP)"), and the page then shows again at the next process start. A settings file that cannot be read counts as done: the app opens as it always did, with an `ERROR` line.
7. **Not in an export file:** it is this phone's. Android's backup carries it with the settings file, so a phone restored from a backup that was past it does not show it again.

**Where the code lives**
- `feature/onboarding/OnboardingScreen.kt` (the page) and `OnboardingViewModel.kt` (the stage, OK and Done). Words in `res/values/strings_onboarding.xml`, with Setup's `setup_done`.
- `data/settings/OnboardingStorage.kt` (`FirstRunStage`, `readFirstRunStage`, `setFirstRunStage`); `MiloSettings.firstRunStage`.
- `app/MiloApp.kt` (the page before every screen, and what OK and Done open), `app/OnboardingEntry.kt` (the ViewModel's factory), `app/MiloNavigation.kt` and `feature/setup/SetupScreen.kt` (the Done button, passed in only during the first start).
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../data/settings/OnboardingStorageTest.kt` (out of the box, each stage stored and read back, the first stage removes, an unknown value), `feature/onboarding/OnboardingViewModelTest.kt` (out of the box, OK then Done, a write that fails, a file that cannot be read, a stage not given back).

**Depends on**
The settings store, the Setup screen ([Permission checklist](#permission-checklist)) and Settings ([Settings](#settings)).

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **Nothing here has run.** Whether the page is drawn in the app's colours outside the bottom bar's frame, clears the status bar and the gesture bar, and what OK and Done open: device checks OB-1 to OB-7.
- **A notification tapped while the page shows** (the monthly reminder, the daily check) acts on the screens behind it as before; the page stays until OK, and the screen it asked for is under Setup.
- **The page says "a while"** for how long the truck may stand still, not ten minutes: the time is a setting.

## The greeting

**Status:** Built. Seen working on an emulator on 2026-10-09 (Android 16, drawn by the PC's graphics card): the wave over Home, nothing with the phone's animations off, and the safety catch's count cleared after a greeting. **Not yet proven on the phone:** no phone's graphics chip has drawn the mascot. Its checks are MG-1 to MG-9 · **Platforms:** Android (the phone; the Android Auto screen is not touched) · **Last updated:** 2026-10-09

**What it does**
When MilO is opened, its mascot waves hello: he fades in, large, in the middle of the screen, over Home dimmed, waves once with his right hand for two seconds while his two gauge needles rev, and fades away. Shawn's request of 2026-10-09, of the mascot he had built in Blender that day: "For now for the app i am only sure on using one of the animations. the wave one when the app starts up."

**Required behaviour** (Shawn's answers the same day)
- **Phone only** ("Phone only"): Android Auto shows Google's templates and can draw neither a 3D model nor an animation.
- **Over Home, not in it** ("Greeting over Home", over a tile of his own on Home, or a small corner of it): Home's layout does not change.
- **Drawn live from the 3D model** ("Filament 3D", over a picture rendered beforehand): ADR-005, with what it costs.

**How it works**
1. **A fresh start asks for one greeting.** `MainActivity` sets `greetingAsked` when the activity is built for the first time, not when Android builds it again (the phone was turned, or MilO was put away and brought back). So MilO greets when it is opened from nothing, and also when it was left with Back and is opened again; it does not greet on coming back from another app.
2. **The rule** (`greeting()` in `feature/greeting/Greeting.kt`, pure, with unit tests) answers one of four things. *Waiting* while the settings are being read. *Showing* when the first start is over, Home is the screen on top, no tapped notification is waiting, and the mascot's drawing is trusted on this phone (5). *Dropped* otherwise. *None* once the greeting has been shown or dropped.
3. **A greeting that is dropped does not come later,** over a screen Shawn has gone to since: `MiloApp` tells the activity at once. So these go without a greeting: the first start (the page that says what MilO does, and Setup), a start with What's new on top after an update, and a start by a tap on a notification, also the daily check's, which leads to Home.
4. **A greeting that is showing is dropped when the rule stops being true under it.** That is how What's new, which opens a moment after the settings are read, ends a greeting that had begun. It is cut, not faded.
5. **The safety catch** (`GreetingViewModel`, ADR-005). The mascot is drawn by native code, and a failure there ends the app with nothing to catch; a greeting at every start would then lose MilO at every start. So each greeting is counted in the settings (`greetings_unfinished`) before the drawing begins, and the count is removed when the mascot has been seen. A start that finds two counted greets no more, and neither does any after it. **Nothing switches it back on** short of clearing MilO's storage. The Log gets an `ERROR` line at every such start: "The greeting is off: 2 in a row began and never showed the mascot".
6. **What is seen** (`MascotGreeting`): nothing at first, and Home works as ever, while the model is read and the phone's graphics chip gets ready. When the mascot can be seen, he and the dimming fade in together (180 ms), the wave plays, and both fade out (260 ms). The mascot's square is 86% of the screen's width; the screen behind is dimmed to 72% black.
7. **A tap anywhere, or Android's Back, ends it early** with the same fade. While the greeting is seen, nothing behind it can be pressed. A screen reader is told "MilO waves hello", and that a tap will "skip the greeting".
8. **The clip's clock starts when the mascot is seen,** not when he was asked for, so the wave is whole on a slow phone too (`Mascot`). Filament draws up to two pictures behind and takes no new one while it is that far behind; once it has taken three, the first is on the screen. Until then every picture is the clip's first.
9. **The drawing** (`MascotStage`): Filament's engine, the model from `assets/mascot/milo_wave.glb` (1.16 MB, the Wave clip only), two lights and a camera, into a see-through `TextureView`. The engine is built for the greeting and given back when it ends, also when it is ended early.

**Where the code lives**
- `feature/greeting/`: `Greeting.kt` (the rule), `GreetingViewModel.kt` (the safety catch).
- `core/designsystem/component/`: `MascotGreeting.kt` (the dimming, the fades, the tap), `Mascot.kt` (the view and the clip's clock; `MascotClip` names the clips), `MascotStage.kt` (everything of Filament's).
- `data/settings/GreetingStorage.kt`: the count.
- `app/`: `MainActivity` (`greetingAsked`), `MiloApp` (asks the rule, draws the greeting), `GreetingEntry.kt` (the ViewModel's factory).
- `design/mascot/`, outside the app: the mascot's Blender file and the scripts that write the model (its `README.md`).
- Tests: `GreetingTest`, `GreetingViewModelTest`, `MascotModelTest` (the committed model carries exactly the clips `MascotClip` names, the wave is two seconds long, and the file is under 2 MB).

**Depends on**
Filament and gltfio 1.77.3 (ADR-005), the app's only native libraries that are not AndroidX's. The first-start stage ([First-start onboarding](#first-start-onboarding)), the back stack, the settings file and the event log.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **With the phone's animations switched off there is no greeting:** nothing is drawn, the engine is not built, Home does not dim. Seen on the emulator.
- **A phone that cannot draw the mascot goes without,** quietly: no engine to be had, a model that cannot be read, or no picture on the screen four seconds after it was asked for. In the last case the greeting stays counted (5), so a phone that is always that slow stops trying after two.
- **The catch can go off by accident.** A greeting stays counted when MilO ends for any reason between the count and the first picture: about a second on the emulator, less on a phone. Two such accidents in a row, with no greeting seen between them, switch the greeting off for good. Two and not one for that reason.
- **The gauges' rims are dark, not chrome.** Nothing is mirrored in the app's drawing: a light map to mirror would be one more file and one more library (ADR-005). In Blender's pictures they are chrome.
- **The emulator's software graphics cannot draw it,** and die trying: SwiftShader's limit for a shader is too small for a model with a skeleton. Run the emulator with the PC's graphics card (`-gpu host`). FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-09.
- **The needles in the wave are part of the clip,** not the truck's speed. Needles that follow a trip would be a new use of `Mascot`; the model already has a clip for each (`Gauge_Speed`, `Gauge_RPM`, in the full model only).
- **The app's copy of the model is made by hand** (`design/mascot/scripts/export_milo.py`) and committed. A clip that is renamed there, or left out, is no greeting and no error on the phone; `MascotModelTest` is what catches it.
- **Turning the phone during the greeting ends it:** the activity is built again, and that is not a fresh start.

**Related**
[First-start onboarding](#first-start-onboarding) and [Version and What's new](#version-and-whats-new), which come before it; [Design system](#design-system), which holds the mascot's components; ADR-005.

---

## Version and What's new

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone, and **never run anywhere**: built and unit tested in CI only. Its checks are WN-1 to WN-9 · **Platforms:** Android (the phone; the Android Auto screen is not touched), and the website · **Last updated:** 2026-10-08

**What it does**
Settings ends with a **Version** tile: the version of MilO on the phone, "0.1.0 · build 1". A tap opens **What's new**: every version, newest first, and what changed in each, every change tagged New, Improved, Fixed or Security. After an update the screen opens once by itself. The website has the same list at `milotriplog.top/changes`. Shawn's request of 2026-10-08: "make sure we have a version number in the app under settings. also please make it clickable and make it work same as gopherforms and seawingman. with including version and what has changed the only difference is im not sure how we are going to do the backend to edit the list and release of changes. also include this on the website".

**Required behaviour**
- **Like GopherForms and SeaWingman** (read from their repositories that day): GopherForms' Settings has "Version 1.4.2 →", which opens a "What's new" list grouped by version with a category tag on each entry (New, Improved, Fixed, Security, in blue, teal, amber and red), and a "What's new" shown once after a new version. Both keep their list in a database behind a server, with an admin page to edit and release it.
- **No backend** (Shawn's choice, "A file in the repo", over a server like GopherForms', which would need the internet permission and break MilO's "no server" promise): the list is a file in the repository, edited in a pull request like the code, built into the app and turned into the website's page.
- **Shown once after each update** (part of the same choice).

**How it works today**
1. **The list** is `app/src/main/assets/changelog.json`: `releases`, newest first; each a `version` (the build's versionName), a `date` ("2026-10-09", or null while it is not released yet) and its `changes`, each a `kind` (`new`, `improved`, `fixed`, `security`), a `title` and an optional `body`. The app reads it strictly (`data/changelog/Changelog.kt`): an unknown key, a missing one, an unknown kind or a date that is not a day makes the whole list unreadable.
2. **The rules the app does not check, CI does** (`tools/changes_page.py --check`, a step of `ci.yml` before Gradle, and of `website.yml` before a deploy): versions are three numbers, each once, newest first; only the newest may be undated; dates go back in time down the list; **the newest version is the build's `versionName`** in `app/build.gradle.kts`; and `website/changes.html` is exactly the page the list makes.
3. **The Version tile** (`feature/whatsnew/VersionTile.kt`) is the last tile of Settings, under backup, export and import, drawn as Setup's tile is at the top: the grey label "Version" with its arrowhead, the version and build in a title's size ("0.1.0 · build 1"), and "What changed in each version" in grey. The whole tile is one button. It is shown whatever the settings file says, also while it is read. The app hands it to Settings (`versionTile`), as it hands in Setup's: a feature never imports another. The version is read from Android (`platform/system/InstalledVersion.kt`, `PackageManager`), once a process.
4. **What's new** (`feature/whatsnew/WhatsNewScreen.kt`) is a screen of its own on top of Settings, with the back arrow. Each version is a grey label on the page, "0.1.0 · 9 Oct 2026", or "0.1.0 · not released yet", with "· on this phone" after the version installed; under it a tile with a row for each change: the tag in capitals ("NEW") on its colour, the title, and the body in grey. The list is read when the screen opens; while it is read the screen says so, and a list that cannot be read is a red line on the screen and an `ERROR` line in the Log.
5. **Once after an update** (`WhatsNewNoticeViewModel`): when the first start is over (`first_run_stage` is `done`) and the stored `whats_new_seen_version` is not the version installed, `MiloApp` opens What's new on top of whatever shows, and stores the version as seen. **A fresh install never sees it:** the first start's OK stores the version as seen, since the first page says what MilO does. A phone that had MilO before this screen, and had gone through the first start, sees it once. Like the first start's stages, once shown it counts at once, stored or not (a failed write is an `ERROR` line), and a settings file that cannot be read counts as seen.
6. **The website's page** (`website/changes.html`, `milotriplog.top/changes`) is written by `python3 tools/changes_page.py` from the same file and committed: the site stays plain files (ADR-003). Each version is a tile with its changes as rows and the same four tags in the app's colours. It is in the site's menu ("What's new"), the landing page's footer, the sitemap, and `firebase.json`'s rewrites.
7. **Keeping it** (README, "Making a release", and CLAUDE.md's build loop): a change Shawn will notice adds a line to the newest version in the same pull request, and the page is written again. While the newest version is released, that pull request opens the next one: a new number with `"date": null`, `versionName` set to it and `versionCode` raised by one. **A release dates the newest version.** Until then the screen and the page say "not released yet".

**Where the code lives**
- `app/src/main/assets/changelog.json` (the list); `tools/changes_page.py` (the rules, and the website's page).
- `data/changelog/Changelog.kt` (the form, `parseChangelog`), `data/changelog/ChangelogSource.kt` (reads the asset).
- `feature/whatsnew/`: `WhatsNewScreen.kt`, `WhatsNewViewModel.kt`, `WhatsNewUiState.kt` (`whatsNewState`), `VersionTile.kt`, `WhatsNewNoticeViewModel.kt` (`isDue`). Words in `res/values/strings_whats_new.xml`.
- `data/settings/WhatsNewStorage.kt` (`whats_new_seen_version`); `MiloSettings.whatsNewSeenVersion`.
- `platform/system/InstalledVersion.kt` (also the export file's `appVersion` since that day).
- `app/WhatsNewObjects.kt` (the version, the source, the screen's entry, the tile, the notice's factory), `app/MiloBackStack.kt` (`WhatsNewKey`), `app/MiloNavigation.kt`, `app/MiloApp.kt` (once after an update).
- The tags' colours: `MiloTheme.colors.changeTags` (`MiloChangeTagColors`), the design's existing colours ([Design system](#design-system)).
- Tests: `data/changelog/ChangelogTest.kt` (a list read in order; an unknown key, an unknown kind, a date that is not a day, a missing date and text that is not JSON refused; the list built into the app read; the source), `feature/whatsnew/WhatsNewNoticeTest.kt` (when it is due; the screen's headings), `feature/whatsnew/WhatsNewNoticeViewModelTest.kt` (shown once, the first start's OK, a failed write, an unreadable file).

**Depends on**
The settings store and the first start ([First-start onboarding](#first-start-onboarding)); Settings ([Settings](#settings)); the website ([Website](#website)).

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **The list's first version is the first release, 0.1.0,** published on 2026-10-09: it lists what MilO does, all "New".
- **A build from `main` between releases** carries the next version's number and its lines under "not released yet": the phone's own builds are ahead of the download.
- **The screen opens by itself only for a new versionName,** not for each new line under the same version: a phone that installs every build from `main` sees it once per version.
- **Someone who skips versions** sees the whole list, newest first, so nothing in between is lost.
- **Nothing here has run:** device checks WN-1 to WN-9.

## Permission checklist

**Status:** Built, proven on the phone: Shawn went through the Setup screen on 2026-10-05, everything except pairing the truck, and what was read back from the phone afterwards showed the four runtime permissions granted, MilO exempt from battery optimisation, and the Autostart reading following the switch. Built, not yet proven on the phone: whether each HyperOS button opens the right screen (he reported no problem, and nothing was written down), the truck's row with a truck, the Physical activity row, which was added afterwards and has been drawn on an emulator only, and the screen's layout as the owner's design draws it (2026-10-07), which has been drawn on an emulator only; its HyperOS group only in a throwaway copy of the build, because an emulator is not a Xiaomi phone. Device checks 54 to 66 have not been run as written; the layout's are NL-50 to NL-52 · **Platforms:** Android · **Last updated:** 2026-10-09

**What it does**
One screen, Setup, showing every requirement with its state, each with a button that takes Shawn to the place to fix it, and at its top how many of them are ready. The home screen warns while a required one is not in order.

**During the first start (since 2026-10-08):** after the page that says what MilO does, Setup has the main button "Done, go to Settings" at its end until it is pressed ([First-start onboarding](#first-start-onboarding)).

**Where it is (since 2026-10-07):** no longer in the bottom bar. Settings took its place there, and Setup is opened from the tile at the top of [Settings](#settings), which shows the same count, or from Home's warning "Open Setup". Its top line ends in an arrowhead and "Setup", which leads back to where it was opened from, as Back does. Opened from Settings the bar keeps Settings marked; opened from Home's warning, Home. Until that day it was the bar's third button.

**Required behaviour**
- Items: precise location, background location ("Allow all the time"), notifications, Bluetooth connect, companion background permissions, battery optimisation exemption, Physical activity (for the driving alert).
- Xiaomi items for this phone: Background autostart, Battery saver "No restrictions", "Pause app activity if unused" off, the "Other permissions" switches (Show on Lock screen, Start in background, Permanent notification), and MilO locked in recents. Each has a button that opens the right HyperOS screen.
- The screen is honest about what it can verify. Autostart can only be read through an unofficial check, so it shows as "looks on", "looks off" or "unknown". The Battery saver profile and the recents lock cannot be read at all, so they are "confirm you set this" steps.

**How it works today**
1. **The rows.** `setupRows` in `platform/system/SetupRules.kt` is a pure function: given what the phone reports (`SetupFacts`), the state of the truck's pairing and Shawn's confirmations, it returns every row with its state (OK, problem, unknown, needs confirmation), which sentence to show and what its button does. `SetupReader` reads the facts from Android. The five facts that stop a trip from being recorded are read by `TripPreflight.facts()`, the same code that checks them before the trip service is started, so the checklist and the preflight cannot disagree.
2. **The ten Android rows,** on every phone:

   | Row | In order when | Its button |
   |---|---|---|
   | Precise location | The precise location permission is granted | Android's permission dialog |
   | Location: Allow all the time | The background location permission is granted | Asks for it, which makes Android open its own location page. No button until precise location is granted: Android ignores the request before that, and the row says so |
   | Notifications | MilO's notifications are shown | The permission dialog, or the notification settings once Android no longer shows the dialog |
   | Nearby devices (Bluetooth) | The Bluetooth permission is granted | The permission dialog |
   | Location switched on | Location is on for the whole phone | The phone's Location settings |
   | Truck paired and watched | The pairing check says "armed" | MilO's pairing screen. The button is always there: "Pair truck" while nothing is paired or Android has dropped the association (the sentence then says "pair it again"), "Change truck" otherwise |
   | Battery use: unrestricted | MilO is exempt from battery optimisation, and not "Restricted" | Android's list of every app's battery optimisation, where the sentence says to show all apps, choose MilO and "Don't optimise" (since 2026-10-09; until then the dialog that asks for MilO alone, whose permission Google Play restricts: ADR-004); for "Restricted", MilO's page in the settings. On a Xiaomi phone that page is HyperOS's "App info", which has no "Unrestricted", so the sentence names its Battery saver choice "No restrictions" there |
   | Not paused when unused | "Pause app activity if unused" is off for MilO | The page with that switch |
   | Battery Saver off | The phone-wide Battery Saver is off | The Battery Saver settings |
   | Physical activity | The Physical activity permission is granted, or the driving alert is switched off in Settings (the row then says "Not needed: the driving alert is switched off in Settings." and asks for nothing) | The permission dialog. Since 2026-10-06; only the [driving alert](#safety-net-manual-button-and-driving-alert) uses it |

3. **The four HyperOS rows,** only on a Xiaomi, Redmi or POCO phone (`isXiaomiFamily`, from the manufacturer and the brand the phone reports):
   - **Background autostart.** Read through the unofficial check the research describes: `AppOpsManager.checkOpNoThrow` with MIUI's app-op 10008, reached by reflection. Mode 0 is shown as "Looks on", any other mode as "Looks off", and a call that fails as "MilO could not read this setting". Each sentence says that it is not a certainty. Only when there is no reading does the row offer "I have set this".
   - **Battery saver: No restrictions,** **Other permissions** (Show on Lock screen, starting or opening windows in the background, Permanent notification, as one row) and **Locked in recent apps.** MilO cannot read any of them. Each shows an empty ring and the instruction until Shawn presses "I have set this"; then it has the tick and says "You confirmed this on (date). MilO cannot check it." "Not set any more" takes the confirmation back. The dates are in the settings store.
   - Their buttons open the HyperOS screens the research found: the Autostart list (`com.miui.securitycenter`'s `AutoStartManagementActivity`, then the action `miui.intent.action.OP_AUTO_START`), MilO's Battery saver choices (`com.miui.powerkeeper`'s `HiddenAppsConfigActivity`), and MilO's permission editor (`miui.intent.action.APP_PERM_EDITOR`). The recents lock has no button: it is a gesture on MilO's card.
4. **Required and recommended.** A row is required if an automatic trip can fail to start, or be cut short, without it. Three rows are only recommended: "Not paused when unused", "Physical activity" and "Other permissions". Their sentences begin with "Recommended". Physical activity is recommended and not required because nothing but the driving alert needs it, and the alert is a safety net that notifies: no trip starts by itself, or fails to, because of it, so it does not belong under the home screen's "a trip may not start by itself".
5. **Opening a settings screen** (`platform/system/SystemScreens.kt`). Each screen has a list of ways to open it, best first, and the last is always Android's own page for MilO. They are tried in order inside a try/catch, so a screen this build of HyperOS does not have is never a crash. When a fallback was used, or nothing opened, an `ERROR` line in the event log says which screen and why.
6. **Asking for a permission.** The button shows Android's dialog. **"Allow all the time" is explained first** (since 2026-10-09, ADR-004): its button opens MilO's own question "MilO uses your location" (`feature/setup/LocationDisclosure.kt`, made with `ConfirmDialog`), which says that MilO collects the phone's location to record each trip's route and distance, the automatic trips included, even when the app is closed or not in use; that it stays on the phone and goes to no one; that the two ends of a trip go to the phone's own address lookup; and to choose "Allow all the time" next. Android is asked only after "Continue"; "Not now", a press beside it or Back asks nothing. It is shown at every press, never only once, so it always comes before the request, as Google Play wants. The row's fix carries `locationDisclosure`, and no other row's does (`SetupRulesTest`). After two refusals Android stops showing it and answers "refused" at once; MilO then opens the settings page that holds the permission, so the button always leads somewhere (`androidDidNotAsk`).
7. **Re-reading.** Android sends no event when a permission or a setting changes. The Setup and Home screens ask the shared `SetupChecklist` to read the phone again every time they come to the front: coming back from a settings screen, one of Android's dialogs closing, and the quick settings panel closing over MilO. The first two resume the screen; the panel does not, so MilO's window getting the focus back counts as well (`CameToFrontEffect`, see [Design system](#design-system)). The truck row and the confirmations update by themselves.
8. **The home screen's warning.** While any required row is not OK, Home shows the amber tile "Setup needs attention" directly under its top line, with a button to Setup. The rule is `needsAttention`. No truck paired is one of the required rows. Until 2026-10-07 the line at the top of Setup counted by the same rule; the tile that replaced it counts every row (step 9).
9. **The screen, as the owner's design draws it** (since 2026-10-07; phone only).
   - **The tile at the top:** the number of rows that are ready, large, with "of 14 ready" beside it (14 on a Xiaomi phone, 10 on any other); at the right what is the matter with the others, such as "2 to fix · 1 to confirm"; and a bar that is filled by the share that is ready. A screen reader reads the tile as one sentence.
   - **Where the numbers come from:** `setupSummary`, a pure function over the rows of step 1, counts each row once by its state. Ready is the state OK, which includes a setting Shawn confirmed. "To fix" is the state "problem". "To confirm" is a setting MilO cannot read at all. **A row MilO tried to read and could not** (the pairing check failed or has not finished, Autostart gave no reading) is counted by itself, as "1 not checked": nothing says that setting is wrong, nothing says it is right, and for the truck's row there is nothing to confirm. A count of zero is left out. With every row ready the tile says "Everything is set".
   - **The count is of every row, the three recommended ones too,** as the drawing counts "Physical activity". So the tile can say "1 to fix" while Home shows no warning. While Home does warn, the tile never says that everything is set.
   - **The rows that are to be fixed stand first in their group,** as drawn: the red ones, right under the count that says how many they are (`toFixFirst`). The others follow in the order of steps 2 and 3, and so do the red ones among themselves. A row moves to its usual place when it is put in order. A row that waits for Shawn's word, or that MilO could not read, is not moved. "Allow precise location first, in the row above" stays true: those two rows are only ever red together.
   - **The groups:** a small grey label on the page and one tile of rows under it, a hairline between two rows. The first label is "Android", as drawn. The second is "This Xiaomi phone (HyperOS)", the words the app had: the drawing's "HyperOS · POCO X5 Pro" names a model, and MilO does not read the phone's model. The sentence that explains the HyperOS rows stands under their tile.
   - **A row's button** stands at the end of the row, as drawn, when the row has one button, its text keeps at least half of its width beside the button, and its sentence needs no more than three lines there (`buttonStandsBeside`, a pure function). Otherwise the buttons stand under the text, as they always did: a row with two buttons, a long instruction, a large font. Every row kept its full sentence, so several rows that the drawing shows with a short note and a button beside it have their button under the text. The button is lime while the row is a problem and quiet otherwise.

**Where the code lives**
- `feature/setup/`: `SetupScreen.kt`, `LocationDisclosure.kt` (the question before "Allow all the time"), `SetupTiles.kt` (the tile with the count, a group, a row), `SetupSummary.kt` (`setupSummary`: the count; `toFixFirst`: the order), `SetupViewModel.kt`, `SetupTexts.kt` (which words each row shows). The row is `StatusRow` with its button at the end, the tiles are made of `TileRow`, and the label is `GroupLabel`, all in `core/designsystem/component/`.
- `platform/system/`: `SetupRow.kt` (the rows, their states and what a button can do), `SetupRules.kt` (`setupRows`, `needsAttention`), `SetupFacts.kt` (what is read from the phone, the Physical activity permission included, `SetupReader`, the Autostart reading, `isXiaomiFamily`), `SetupChecklist.kt` (the rows as a flow, shared by Setup and Home), `SystemScreen.kt` (the ways of opening each settings screen, as plain values), `SystemScreens.kt` (opens them), `PermissionAsk.kt` (`androidDidNotAsk`), `TripPreflight.kt` (the five facts shared with the trip service).
- `data/settings/`: the confirmation dates (`ConfirmedStep`, `SettingsStore.setConfirmedAtMs`).
- The warning card is in `feature/home/HomeScreen.kt`.
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../platform/system/` (`SetupRulesTest.kt`, `PhysicalActivityRowTest.kt`, `SetupAttentionTest.kt`, `SetupFactsTest.kt`, `SetupChecklistTest.kt`, `SystemScreenTest.kt`, `PermissionAskTest.kt`, `TripPreflightTest.kt`) and `app/src/test/.../feature/setup/SetupTextsTest.kt` (the truck button's words, and the battery sentence for each kind of phone) and `SetupSummaryTest.kt` (the count and the order); `core/designsystem/component/StatusRowLayoutTest.kt` (when a button stands at the end of its row).

**Depends on**
`TruckPairing.status` (see [Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection)), the settings store, and the HyperOS findings in `docs/research/2026-10-03-miui-background-limits.md` (4, 16 to 20 and 27 to 33).

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **Used on the phone once, and not yet checked row by row.** On 2026-10-05 Shawn went through Setup on the POCO, everything except pairing the truck. Read back over adb afterwards (FINDINGS_LOG, "First results from the phone"): all four runtime permissions granted, "Allow all the time" among them; MilO on Android's battery-optimisation exemption list; and the Autostart reading answering "allow", where the same record showed it rejecting nine minutes earlier, so on HyperOS 2.0 the reading works and follows the switch. Still unknown: whether each HyperOS button opened the right screen (he reported no problem), and whether `isPowerSaveMode` and the "pause if unused" reading follow Xiaomi's own switches. Device checks 54 to 66 go through it row by row and have not been run.
- Whether HyperOS's quick settings panel takes the focus from MilO's window, as stock Android's does, is untested. If it does not, a setting changed there shows only after MilO is left and opened again (device check 59).
- Whether Android's "Restricted" can be reached on HyperOS at all, and whether Battery saver "No restrictions" is what lifts it, is unknown (device checks 22 and 61). The sentence names the one battery setting the HyperOS app page has.
- **One row of the brief is not there.** The companion background permissions are granted at install and cannot be taken away, so they have no row; what can go wrong with the companion association is the truck row. (Physical activity, the other one that was missing, has its row since 2026-10-06.)
- **The Physical activity row has never been seen on the phone.** On an emulator (Android 16, 2026-10-06) it was drawn, its Allow button brought up Android's dialog "Allow MilO to access your physical activity?", and after Allow the row said "Allowed." without the screen being left. How HyperOS words the permission and where it keeps it is device check DA-1.
- **The row follows the driving alert's switch by itself,** like the confirmations: switching the alert off in Settings puts the row in order without a new reading of the phone.
- The Autostart reading is known to say "on" for a switch that is off on some phones, and always says "on" while MIUI optimisation is disabled. On this phone it has been seen to follow the switch once (above). "Looks on" still proves nothing about whether HyperOS lets MilO start; the with and without Autostart drive (device checks 36 to 43) does.
- A confirmation by hand never overrides an Autostart that reads as off. If the reading were wrong in that direction on this phone, the warning could not be cleared; that would be a finding to act on.
- The confirmations are Shawn's word, and HyperOS is reported to reset these settings after updates and reboots. A confirmation does not expire. The date is shown so that an old one can be doubted.
- On the POCO the home screen's warning stays until the Battery saver row and the recents lock are confirmed, even with every permission granted.
- Android's battery exemption and HyperOS's "No restrictions" are treated as two settings. The research could not settle whether either sets the other.
- If the settings file cannot be read, the rows are still shown, without the confirmations, and an `ERROR` line is logged.
- If Android refuses one of the readings, an `ERROR` line with the exception is logged and the rows stay as they were. Should that happen at the very first reading, Setup stays on "Reading the phone's settings…"; the Log says why.
- The settings screens open as a task of their own. Back returns to MilO.
- **The battery list is searched by hand** (since 2026-10-09): Android shows only the apps that are not optimised at first, so the sentence says to show all apps. Whether HyperOS has that list, or MilO's App info opens in its place (the fallback), is device check GP-3. The exemption the phone was given on 2026-10-05 stays: removing the permission takes no app off the list.
- **The location question is not kept across a rotation:** it closes, and the row's button shows it again.

## Schedule and Business/Personal

**Status:** Built, proven on the phone: the catch-up that sorts trips recorded before the schedule existed (2026-10-06: it sorted the phone's two test trips at the first start after the update). Built, not yet proven on the phone: everything else, which ran on an emulator only: the Settings cards, a trip sorted as it ends, "Ignore them", marking a trip by hand, and the figures on Trips and Home. Device checks 128 to 152, and 158 and 161 for a trip that is edited · **Platforms:** Android (phone) · **Last updated:** 2026-10-06

**What it does**
Decides whether a trip is Business or Personal from when it started, and lets Shawn overrule it.

**Required behaviour**
- Settings: choose which days to track and a start and end time for each day. Default Monday to Friday, 08:00 to 16:30. An option applies the same hours to all days.
- A trip that starts inside the schedule is saved as Business. A trip that starts outside it is saved as Personal, with a setting to ignore such trips entirely instead.
- Shawn can switch any trip between Business and Personal.
- If a Business trip is still running at the end time, keep recording until the trip ends and flag it "ran past schedule".

**How it works today**
1. **The schedule** is seven days, each with a switch ("tracked") and a start and an end time. It is kept with the other settings. Since 2026-10-07 the Settings screen shows it as Shawn drew it: seven day buttons and one slider for the hours, which sets all seven days at once; a slider for each work day stands in its place when the days differ or when it is asked for. A day's end must be later than its start, both on a whole minute: there are no overnight spans, and the latest end is 23:59. Out of the box: Monday to Friday, 08:00 to 16:30; Saturday and Sunday are off and carry the same hours for the day one of them is switched on. The screen is described under [Settings](#settings).
2. **The rule** (`classifyTrip` in `core/schedule/TripClassification.kt`, a pure function). A trip is **Business** when its **start**, read as a day and a time of day in the phone's time zone, falls on a tracked day at or after that day's start time and before its end time. Otherwise it is **Personal**. On the stroke of the start time is Business; on the stroke of the end time is Personal. Only the start decides, and what started the trip (the truck, or a Start button on the phone or the car) makes no difference.
3. **Ran past schedule.** A Business trip that ends after the end time *of the day it started on* is flagged. The end time is worked out as a point in time, so a trip that runs past midnight is judged against the day it began, and a day on which the clocks change is as long as it really was. Ending exactly at the end time is not past it. A Personal trip is never flagged.
4. **When it happens: when a trip is finalised, and at no other moment.** The trip rules close the trip as they always did; in the same write that closes it, the category and the flag are stored (`TripLedger.close` in `platform/trip/`, `TripRepository.closeTrip`). The schedule that is used is the one stored at that moment, so a change on the Settings screen applies to trips that end after it. **The schedule never decides whether a trip starts, carries on or ends.** Nothing in the trip rules, the Bluetooth triggers or the trip service reads it. (Since 2026-10-06 the driving alert reads it, through the same rule, to send its notification only when a trip started at that moment would be Business. A notification is all that comes of it. Since that evening the daily "nothing recorded" check reads it too, for which days are work days and when each begins; a notification is all that comes of that as well. That check is the one reader that follows a change of the schedule: its daily alarm is worked out from it, so it watches the stored schedule, asks for the alarm again and looks at once.) A truck that connects at 07:55 for a drive at 08:05 is one trip that started at 07:55, recorded whole, saved as Personal, and Shawn can mark it Business.
5. **"Ignore them".** With that chosen for trips outside the schedule, a trip is still started, recorded and measured exactly as usual. When it is finalised and turns out Personal, it is stored as **discarded**, with its times, distance, positions and GPS points, and marked as ignored for that reason. It is then out of the list and the totals, and can be found on the Trips screen under "Show deleted and discarded trips", where it says "Left out: it started outside your work hours, and Settings says to ignore such trips. Not counted." and has the button "Count this trip". Counted, it is an ordinary Personal trip. A trip the trip rules had already discarded (too short, or a start that was never confirmed) is not marked as ignored: it says what it always said.
6. **By hand.** On the Trips screen a finished trip can be marked as Business or as Personal (see [Trip log](#trip-log-home-day-and-month-views)). That writes the category and that Shawn set it, and nothing else. A trip set by hand is never changed by MilO again. With "Ignore them" on, marking a kept trip Personal does not discard it: the setting is applied once, when a trip is finalised, and this trip has now been dealt with by hand.
7. **Trips recorded before the schedule existed** have no category after the update (the migration leaves them "not sorted"). At every process start a catch-up (`TripCategoryCatchUp` in `data/trip/`) looks for closed trips without a category that were not set by hand, and sorts each by the schedule **as it is at that moment** and the phone's time zone then. It writes the category and the flag and nothing else: it never discards a trip, whatever "Ignore them" says, and it sorts deleted and discarded trips too, so that they have their category when they are restored or counted. After its first pass it finds nothing. The first pass after the update runs before Shawn has seen the Settings screen, so it goes by Monday to Friday, 08:00 to 16:30.
8. **A later change of the schedule changes no stored trip,** sorted by the schedule or set by hand. What a trip was saved as is a fact about the day it was driven.
9. **A change of a trip's times by hand** (the edit screen, see [Trip log](#trip-log-home-day-and-month-views)) is the one thing that sorts a stored trip again, and by one rule, `refileTrip` in `core/schedule/TripRefiling.kt`:
   - **A category Shawn picks in the form is his.** The trip is saved as that and marked as set by hand.
   - **A category that was set by hand stays,** whatever is done to the times.
   - **Otherwise a new start is sorted again,** by the schedule as it is at the moment of the save and the phone's time zone then. Only the start decides Business or Personal, so only a changed start re-sorts: a changed end, distance or address leaves the category as it was saved, even if the schedule has been changed since.
   - **"Ran past schedule" follows the times.** Whenever the start or the end changed, it is worked out again from the new times and the schedule as it is now, whoever chose the category: true only if the schedule makes the trip Business and it ends after that day's hours. If neither time changed it is kept as stored. It is still shown only while the trip is Business.
   - **"Restore recorded values" goes by the same rule** for the times that come back: a trip that is not set by hand is sorted again by its recorded start, by the schedule as it is then.
   - **A trip that is added by hand** is sorted by its start like any other, unless Shawn chooses himself. "Ignore them" is not applied to it, and neither is the minimum distance: he typed it in on purpose.
   - The form shows what a save will store before Save is pressed, and says who chose: the work hours, Shawn, or "as this trip was saved".
10. **A trip that is not sorted** (possible only between the update and the first pass, or while the settings file cannot be read) is shown as "Not marked Business or Personal yet", is counted in a line of its own, and can be marked by hand.
11. **The event log.** The `TRIP` line that records the end of a trip now ends with what the schedule made of it and what it went by: "…; saved as Business: it started on a Tue at 10:14:05; that day's hours are 08:00 to 16:30 (America/Edmonton)", "…; saved as Business, ran past schedule: …", "…; saved as Personal: it started on a Sat at 10:02:40; Sat is not a tracked day (America/Edmonton)". An ignored trip's line starts "Trip 12: discarded: it started outside the work schedule, and trips outside it are set to be ignored (12340 m). It can be counted on the Trips screen" and, like the line of a trip that was too short, ends with what the trip is if it is counted after all ("…; if it is counted, it is Personal: it started on a Sat at 10:02:40; Sat is not a tracked day (America/Edmonton)"). The catch-up writes one `TRIP` line for a pass that sorted something ("Business or Personal set for 5 trips recorded before they were sorted (process start), by the work schedule as it is now: 3 Business (1 ran past schedule), 2 Personal. No trip was discarded or otherwise changed."), with each trip and its reason in the detail. Every marking by hand is one `TRIP` line, and a refused or failed one is a line too.

**Where the code lives**
- `core/schedule/`: `WorkSchedule.kt` (the seven days, what hours a day may have, the defaults, the copy), `TripClassification.kt` (`classifyTrip`, and `fileTrip`, which adds the "ignore" decision at the moment a trip is finalised), `TripRefiling.kt` (`refileTrip`: what a change of the times by hand makes of the category and of "ran past schedule"), `TripCategory.kt` (Business, Personal). Pure Kotlin: no file in the package imports anything of Android, its string resources included.
- `core/designsystem/text/TripCategoryWords.kt` (`categoryWordsRes`): which words a row uses for what a trip is saved as. It chooses a string resource, so it stands with the design system and not beside the rule (moved there by the review of 2026-10-06).
- `data/settings/`: `ScheduleStorage.kt` (how the schedule is kept in the settings file), `SettingsStore.kt` (`setSchedule`, `setIgnoreTripsOutsideSchedule`), `MiloSettings.kt`.
- `data/trip/`: `Trip.kt` (the four columns), `TripDao.kt` and `TripRepository.kt` (`closeTrip`, `findUnsortedTrips`, `sortUnsorted`, `setCategoryByHand`), `TripCategoryCatchUp.kt` (the catch-up and its rule `needsSorting`), `TripCategories.kt` (what a trip may be marked as; when "ran past schedule" is said), `TripTotals.kt` (`categoryTotals`), `TripCategoryLogText.kt` (the wording of the log lines). `data/MiloMigrations.kt` has the step from version 2 to 3.
- `platform/trip/`: `TripLedger.kt` (sorts a trip as it closes it), `TripRuleSettings.kt` (reads the schedule with the other settings), `TripLogText.kt`.
- `feature/settings/ScheduleTile.kt` with `ScheduleParts.kt`, `ScheduleTileState.kt` and `ScheduleSlider.kt` (the day buttons, the sliders, the time picker, the choice for trips outside the hours; see [Settings](#settings)), and `feature/trips/` (the buttons, the rows, the totals). `core/schedule/WorkWeek.kt`: the schedule read as one week, for the one slider that sets every day.
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../core/schedule/` (`TripClassificationTest`: the edges of a day, an untracked day, across midnight, both clock changes, the time zone; `TripFilingTest`; `TripRefilingTest`: the rule of step 9, case by case; `WorkScheduleTest`), `core/designsystem/text/TripCategoryWordsTest.kt`, `data/trip/TripCategoryCatchUpTest.kt` and `TripCategorySelectionTest.kt` (which trips the catch-up may touch, compared with the query), `TripCategoryTotalsTest.kt`, `core/schedule/WorkWeekTest.kt` (the week's hours, whether the work days agree, the same hours on every day), `data/settings/ScheduleStorageTest.kt`, `platform/trip/TripControllerScheduleTest.kt` and `TripControllerIgnoreTest.kt` (the trip controller on stand-ins: sorted at the close, recorded in full whatever the schedule says), `feature/trips/TripMarkingTest.kt` and `TripMonthCategoryTest.kt`, `data/MiloMigrationsTest.kt`.

**Depends on**
The settings store, the trips table (the four columns are there since database version 3), and the phone's time zone and clock.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **What the phone has shown, and what it has not.** On 2026-10-06 the build with the schedule was installed over the first build on the phone. The conversion of the database from version 1 to version 3 ran there without error, and the catch-up sorted the phone's two test trips (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, "The phone's own database converted cleanly"). The Settings cards, a trip sorted as it ends, "Ignore them", marking a trip by hand and the figures on Trips and Home have not been seen on the phone. What ran on an emulator on 2026-10-06 is in FINDINGS_LOG under that date.
- **The start decides, to the millisecond.** A trip that starts a minute before the day's start time is Personal however long it runs. The cure is the button: mark it Business.
- **The trip's start is when MilO opened the trip,** which for a trip the truck starts is when the phone connected to it, not when the truck began to move.
- **The time zone is the phone's at the moment the trip is sorted,** not where the trip was driven. A trip is sorted when it ends, so this only shows if the phone changes zone during a trip. For the catch-up it is the zone at the first start after the update. **What it costs:** a work drive from Alberta into the Pacific time zone is read an hour earlier than it began, so one that started in the first hour of the work day is saved as Personal, and with "Ignore them" on it is left out. In winter a drive into Saskatchewan is read an hour later, and one that started in the last hour of the day goes the same way. Nothing on the trip's row says why; its line in the Log names the zone it was read in. It is put right by hand: Mark as Business, after Count this trip if it was left out. The cure is to store the zone with the trip when it starts and to sort by that: a new column, and a change to how a trip is started, which the work on the schedule was not to touch. A reviewer reproduced the case on an emulator on 2026-10-06; it was left as it is, for Shawn to decide.
- **The first catch-up uses the default hours.** If Shawn's real hours differ, trips recorded before the update can be sorted wrongly, and changing the schedule afterwards does not sort them again. They are marked by hand.
- **"Ran past schedule" is stored when the schedule sorts the trip,** and is shown only while the trip is Business. A trip that was Personal by the schedule and is marked Business by hand never shows it, even if it ended after the day's end time: a marking does not work the flag out again, and the schedule gives such a trip no hours to run past. Only a change of the trip's times on the edit screen works it out again (step 9).
- **An edit of the start can change what a trip is saved as.** Moving a start from 07:55 to 08:05 makes a Personal trip Business, and the form shows that before Save is pressed. It is sorted by the schedule as it is on the day of the edit, not as it was on the day of the drive; if the hours have changed since, the form's choice is put right by hand, which also makes it final.
- **With "Ignore them" on, a Personal trip looks like a trip that vanished:** it is recorded, then it is not in the list. The Trips screen's line "N deleted or discarded trips are not shown" is the only sign, and the Log says why.
- **An ignored trip gets no addresses** until it is counted: only a finished trip is looked up.
- **A setting that cannot be read leaves a closing trip unsorted** (it is not sorted by hours that are not Shawn's), and the catch-up logs an `ERROR` line at each start until the settings can be read again.
- A day's hours cannot run past midnight. Night work that crosses midnight would need two days' hours, and the trip is still judged by its start alone.
- **What a trip is saved as decides whether it is on the report for the accountant:** only Business trips are ([Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission)). A trip that the schedule made Personal by a minute is left off until it is marked Business; the Report screen says how many Personal trips its period has.

**Related**
[Settings](#settings), [Trip log](#trip-log-home-day-and-month-views), [Diagnostics and reminders](#diagnostics-and-reminders) (the daily "nothing recorded" check goes by the schedule's days and by each day's start, and follows a change of either).
