## Trip log: home, day and month views

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone. What is built: the home screen, laid out since 2026-10-06 as the owner's design draws it (a tile that starts a trip, today's and the month's Business kilometres, the truck's connection, each vehicle's odometer, a report that has not been sent, and today's finished trips; and while a trip is recorded, its kilometres, the button that ends it, the odometers and today's finished trips), and the Trips screen, laid out since 2026-10-07 as the owner's design draws it, lists one month at a time, one tile per day that opens when it is pressed (where each trip went, its times, Business or Personal, km), where a trip can be deleted and restored, a discarded trip counted after all, and a finished trip marked Business or Personal. Each day and each month shows its Business figures first and Personal apart, added up as the report for the accountant adds up, and a month whose report was sent says when its Business trips have changed since. A finished trip can be edited and a missed trip added by hand, on an edit screen opened from Trips; both are marked in the list. All of it ran on an emulator. On the phone, Home was used to start a trip on 2026-10-05, long before this layout; nothing records the Trips screen or the edit screen being drawn there, and neither Home nor Trips as they are now has ever been on the phone. Home's truck tile, the truck's arrival that it plays since 2026-10-07 and the two states beside a parked truck have been seen on an emulator only, with a stand-in for the truck. The tile's lower form of 2026-10-09 has been seen on an emulator only, as previews of the debug build. Device checks 76 to 86, 101 to 110, 125 to 127, 153 to 176, NL-20 to NL-39, NL-40 to NL-49 and TT-1 · **Platforms:** Android · **Last updated:** 2026-10-09

**Kilometres or miles (2026-10-07, evening).** Home, Trips and the edit screen show every distance in the unit chosen in Settings. Each trip is rounded once to a tenth of that unit, and a day's and a month's figures are the sums of those rounded figures, so the rows still add up to their headings. The edit screen's field is "Distance, mi" with Miles chosen; a trip that is opened and saved without its distance being changed keeps its stored metres. See [Units](#units-kilometres-or-miles).

**What it does**
Shows what was recorded and lets Shawn correct it.

**Required behaviour**
- Home screen: current trip status, plus today's sessions (count, km each, total km, total drive time).
- Day view: each session with start and end time, start and end address, km, and Business/Personal.
- Month view: sessions and business km per day, plus month totals.
- Shawn can edit any trip, delete trips, and manually add a missed trip. Manually added or edited trips are marked as such.
- There was no purpose or note field, dropped at kickoff, until 2026-10-08: a trip now has a label, which the report prints as its purpose ([Trip labels](#trip-labels)).

**How it works today**
*Home* (`feature/home/`). **Since 2026-10-06 it is laid out as the owner's design draws it** (the canvas's two drawings of Home: with no trip, and while one is recorded). On his phone he had put the first package of the new look beside his drawing and asked why the two did not look the same; FINDINGS_LOG of that day has the answer. Top to bottom:
1. **The top line.** The app's mark (an "M" on a lime square), "MilO", today's date under it ("Tue, Oct 6"), and, since 2026-10-09, nothing at its end (Shawn: "remove the home top right corner"; until then "Home", and the bottom bar's lit Home button says it all the same). Since 2026-10-07 every screen starts with this line (`AppHeader`, [Design system](#design-system)), each with its own name at the end. Until then a square button with three sliders stood at its end and opened [Settings](#settings); Shawn had it removed the same day, when Settings took Setup's place in the bottom bar.
2. **Two tiles that are only there when something stands in the way,** directly under the top line, because they matter more than anything below:
   - the amber tile "Setup needs attention", with its button "Open Setup", while the setup checklist needs attention (see [Permission checklist](#permission-checklist));
   - "MilO could not start this trip", which lists why the last start failed, one line for each thing.
3. **With no trip open:**
   - **The lime tile "Start trip".** The whole tile is the button. The small line under the title is "Or just connect the truck" only while that is true: a truck is paired, it is not connected, and Setup is in order. Beside a truck that is connected and parked it is "Or it starts when the truck moves" while MilO watches the truck, and "Press here before you drive off" once MilO has stopped watching. In every other state it is "Press to record a trip by hand".
   - **Two tiles side by side.** "Today · business": the km of today's Business trips. While today has a Personal trip, a small line under the figure says "Personal: 5.0 km · 1 trip"; it is never added to the Business figure. Beside it the month ("October"): the km of the month's Business trips, a thin bar, and "89% business · $289". The first is the Business share of all the month's counted kilometres, Personal ones and unsorted ones included, in whole percent. **The dollars (since 2026-10-09,** Shawn: "can we have on that the business dollar value that the person's getting back") are the month's Business kilometres at the one rate set in Settings, in whole dollars: the widget's figure for the month, from the same function (`allowanceCents`), always from kilometres whichever unit is shown (`MonthFigures.businessKmTenths`). For reference only, like the widget's; the report shows no dollars. They are left out while the settings have not been read. A month with no kilometres says "No kilometres yet", and nothing is divided by zero. A month with any kilometre that is not Business never reads 100%.
   - **The truck's tile** (step 5).
   - **The amber tile "September report / Not sent to the accountant yet / Send",** only while last month's report is waiting. It is the monthly reminder's own rule and not a second one ([Diagnostics and reminders](#diagnostics-and-reminders)): the reminder is switched on, this month's reminder day has come, last month has a Business trip, and no report for the whole of last month is recorded as sent. The notification comes once a day; the tile stays until the report is recorded as sent, or the reminder is switched off. "Send" opens the Report screen for that month, and Back from there leads to Home.
   - **Each vehicle's odometer** (since 2026-10-09, Shawn: "i want the milage on the home screen"; he chose the odometer reading), under the truck's tile: "Odometer" over the figure Settings' odometer tile shows, the last reading typed there plus every trip since, with "Your last reading in Settings, plus every trip since" under it. Several vehicles: a row for each, its name and its figure, or "No reading yet". Before any reading: "Type the number on the dashboard in Settings once. …". Nothing to press. The figures come from `vehicleOdometers` (`data/trip/VehicleOdometers.kt`), which Settings' tiles use too, so the two cannot disagree; Home reads every finished trip for it and follows a trip that ends and a reading typed in Settings. **While a trip is recorded it counts up as the trip is driven** (step 4).
   - **"Today · 3 trips · 1 h 5 min driving":** every finished trip of today, newest first, the same tile as in step 4 without its note about the trip in progress (since 2026-10-09, Shawn: "just have todays trips sorted latest at the top"; until then "Last trip", the newest alone, with "All 4 today"). The whole tile leads to Trips. Before the first trip of the day it says "No finished trips yet today."
4. **While a trip is being recorded:**
   - **The lime tile** shows "Recording", the km so far in the largest figures, the minutes it has run and "since 2:14 PM", "From (address)" once the address lookup knows where it started, and the dark button "End trip". While the trip waits for the truck to reconnect it also says so, in the sentence Home has always used. **It does not say Business or Personal:** a trip is sorted when it closes, and until then MilO has not decided.
   - **Two tiles side by side:** a small "Truck" tile that says "Connected" or "Not connected", and "Today · business" as above.
   - **The odometers,** as in step 3 (since 2026-10-09). **They count up as the trip is driven** (the same day, Shawn: "can we have the mileage on the home screen change as we drive"; asked which figure, he chose the odometer, phone only, and Settings' tile with it). The odometer of the vehicle the trip is in shows its last reading, every finished trip since and the kilometres of this trip so far, so it goes up by one for each kilometre driven (each mile, with Miles chosen). The rules are the finished trip's own: nothing is added until a paired vehicle has been seen connected in the trip, so a trip started with the button counts from the moment the truck joins it, all its kilometres at once, and one the truck never joins counts nothing; a trip that names no vehicle is the first vehicle's; and a reading typed in Settings during the trip already holds that trip, so the odometer then stands at that reading until the next trip. The trip controller hands the open trip over as plain values (`TripActivity.tripSoFar()`, `TripSoFar`), and `drivenTrips` adds it to the finished ones, unless storage already holds it as ended: storage says so a moment before the controller does, and the trip is never counted twice in between. Until that day the trip in progress was added when it ended.
   - **"Today · 3 trips · 1 h 5 min driving":** every finished trip of today, newest first, with where it went, its times, what it is saved as and its km. The count and the drive time are of all of them, Business and Personal. A trip that is not Business has its figure greyed, so that the white figures add up to "Today · business". Under them: "The trip in progress is added when it ends." The whole tile leads to Trips.
5. **The truck's tile says what MilO really knows,** in the design's looks. Not connected: grey dashes between the phone and a faint truck; nothing moves. Connected: a solid lime line with a light running along it, and a ring that spreads from the truck. (The third look, "Connecting…", is step 6.)

   **Since 2026-10-09 the tile is lower.** Shawn: "on the home screen can we please make the connect to vehicle more compact. i would like it not as tall". He was shown three lower forms drawn at real size and chose "Slim". Built, not yet proven on the phone (device check TT-1). Top to bottom the tile now shows three things:
   - **the title, with the vehicle's name at the end of the same line** in small letters;
   - **one short sentence;**
   - **the drawing** of the phone, the line and the truck, as wide as the tile, with circles 30 dp across where they were 48. It moves as before.

   The word "Truck" in the tile's corner and the two labels under the drawing ("Your phone" and the truck's name) are gone. With a sentence of one line the tile is 104 dp high, and 120 with two. Before, it was 152 to 187 dp high (each measured on an emulator set to a phone 360 dp wide, at the usual font size; FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-09, has every state).

   Which state is shown is decided in one place (`truckState` in `TruckState.kt`):

   | What is so | Look | Title | Sentence |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | The settings or the truck's connection have not been read yet | not connected | Checking… | Reading the truck's connection |
   | No truck is paired | not connected | No truck paired | Tap to pair one, so a trip can start by itself. (The tile then opens the pairing screen.) |
   | Paired, not connected, Setup in order | not connected | Not connected | A trip starts by itself when the truck connects |
   | Paired, not connected, Setup needs attention | not connected | Not connected | A trip may not start by itself. Setup says why. |
   | Connected, no trip, because End was pressed with the truck connected | connected | Connected | You ended the trip. The next one starts by itself after the truck reconnects. |
   | Connected, no trip, for any other reason (a start that failed) | connected | Connected | No trip is being recorded. Press Start trip. |
   | Connected and parked: the parked rule ended the trip, and MilO watches the truck ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), rules 14 and 15) | connected | Truck connected and parked | A trip starts by itself when the truck moves |
   | The same after three days of standing: MilO has stopped watching | connected | Truck connected. MilO has stopped watching it | It has not moved for a long time. Press Start trip before you drive off. |
   | A trip is recorded, truck connected | small tile, lit dot | Connected | Only on the large tile, for a moment after the truck has arrived (step 6): "Trip recording started" |
   | A trip is recorded, truck not connected (started by hand, or held open by Android Auto) | small tile, grey dot | Not connected | |
   | A trip is recorded and waits for the truck to reconnect | small tile, grey dot | Not connected | On the lime tile: "Trip in progress. Waiting for the truck to reconnect" |

   **The sentences are short on purpose** (since 2026-10-09). Most of the old tile's height was its sentence, which took up to three lines. At the usual font size on a phone 360 dp wide each sentence now takes one line, except two that take two: the one after End, and the one for a truck MilO has stopped watching. They say what they said before in fewer words, with one thing left out: the tile beside a parked truck no longer says that the last trip ended where the truck stopped. The two sentences of the arrival (step 6) were short already and are unchanged.

   **The name at the end of the first line** is the vehicle the tile is about: the one that is connected or in the trip, and otherwise the first one paired. With two vehicles paired and neither connected, it names the first one only, as the tile did before. A vehicle the phone knows no name for is "The truck". With no vehicle paired nothing stands there, and while MilO is still reading ("Checking…") only a name it has read already. The name is light while the tile is in its connected look and grey otherwise.
   - **The title comes first.** A name too long for what the title leaves of the line is cut with "…", and never sends the title onto a second line.
   - **A title that would leave the name no room for about five letters goes onto a second line instead.** "Truck connected. MilO has stopped watching it" always takes two lines; with a large font, "Truck connected and parked" does too.
   - The rule for what is named is `vehicleShown` in `TruckState.kt`; the layout of the line is `TruckTile`'s ([Design system](#design-system)).

   **A screen reader is read the tile as one thing** (since 2026-10-09): the title, the vehicle's name, the sentence. While no truck is paired the tile is a button, and the screen reader adds "double tap to pair the truck". Not yet tried with a screen reader.

   "Connected" is what the trip rules believe, as on the [Android Auto screen](#android-auto-screen): it is read again every time MilO is opened, and by every event from the truck. Whether End was pressed with the truck connected is read from the stored hold-off ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection)). The two parked states are the trip controller's own word (`TripActivity.parked`), and they are shown whatever the Bluetooth reading says, because Android Auto on the cable can hold the wait alone. Their titles are the ones the notification uses. While MilO waits there is no End trip button: the lime tile reads "Start trip", and a press starts a trip at once.
6. **The truck's arrival is played, as the design plays it** (2026-10-07; `ShownArrival.kt`, `HomeShown.kt`). Android tells MilO that the truck is connected, never that it is about to be. So "Connecting…" is shown just after the truck was found: it is how Home passes the news on, and it lasts a fixed time.
   - **When.** Home is open and in front, the truck was seen not connected with no trip, and now it is seen connected, or seen to have started a trip.
   - **First, for 2.6 seconds:** the tile says "Connecting…" and "Found the truck over Bluetooth". Lime dashes run toward the truck, the Bluetooth mark pulses in a lime ring, and a ring spreads from it. The layout stays the one without a trip, also when the trip is open already. The Start tile keeps the small line it had.
   - **Then, if the connect started a trip** (the ordinary case): the tile is in its connected look with "Connected" and "Trip recording started" for 1.2 seconds, and then Home changes to the trip with a fade of 0.3 seconds. **If it started none** (End had been pressed, or the start failed): the tile shows the state it is in, from the table above.
   - **The trip does not wait for any of this.** It is recorded from its first second, with its sound and its notification. For up to 3.8 seconds Home shows "Start trip" although a trip is being recorded; a press then changes nothing about the trip and shows it at once.
   - **Shown at once, with nothing played:** Home opened beside a connected truck or during a trip; a trip started with Start; a press of Start during the arrival; a trip that starts beside a parked truck when it moves; the truck going again during "Connecting…". What happened while MilO was in the background, or another screen was showing, is never played afterwards.
   - **The movement** runs only while the tile is in view and Home is in front. With the phone's animations switched off nothing moves and nothing fades; the stages still follow one another at the same times, as in the design's own file.
7. **What is counted** is decided by `todayTrips` in `data/trip/TripTotals.kt`, the function the Android Auto screen uses, and for the month by `categoryTotals` in the same file, the function the Trips screen's month card uses: finished trips that started today, or this month. A discarded or a deleted trip is in neither, and so a trip left out by "Ignore them" is in neither. The trip in progress is not counted until it ends. **Every total is the sum of the kilometres shown for its trips,** each rounded to a tenth first (`sumOfTenths`; "How the kilometres are added up", below), so the month's figure on Home is the one on Trips and on the month's report.
8. **It follows storage.** A trip that ends, or is deleted, restored, counted, marked or edited on the Trips screen, changes the tiles by itself, and so does a report that is recorded as sent. Only the trips that started this month are read, once, and today's are picked out of them; last month's are read for the report tile.
9. **Which day is today** is worked out when the screen comes to the front (`CameToFrontEffect`), in the phone's time zone, and with it the month. A trip belongs to the day it started on. Home also asks then for the addresses that finished trips still lack, as the Trips screen does, because it now writes where the last trip went.
10. **What the drawing took off Home.** With no trip open, Home no longer lists each of today's trips, and it does not show their drive time. Both are on the list that shows while a trip is recorded, and every trip is on Trips, one tap away.

*Trips* (`feature/trips/`), the second button of the bottom bar. Shawn asked for it by name on 2026-10-05: "a trip button to view previous and current months trips". **Since 2026-10-07 it is laid out as the owner's design draws it** (the canvas's drawing of Trips), with one thing he added to his drawing that night: each day is closed until it is pressed. Since 2026-10-07 "Trips" stands at the end of the top line every screen has, and under it the month ("October 2026") is the large heading, with the two squares that step to the month before and after.
1. It opens on the current month, every time it is entered. (Coming back from the edit screen after a save, it shows the month the saved trip is in: "Editing a trip", step 13.) **The top line** is the title "Trips" and two square buttons with an arrowhead each: the month before, and the month after. The second is greyed on the current month: the screen never goes past it. Back is one month at a time, without limit.
   - **The lime tile is the month:** "October 2026 · Business", the km of the month's Business trips in the largest figures, and their number ("18 trips"). At its right stands a dark pill that says **whether the month's report has been sent to the accountant**: "Not submitted", or "Submitted". **The pill is a button. It opens the Report screen for that month** ([Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission)); the text button "Report for the accountant" that used to do this is gone. A month that was submitted has one more line under its figures, which says when ("Submitted on Oct 6, 2026", or the same with the day it was sent again and the revision's number).
   - **Two small tiles side by side under it.** "Personal": the km and the number of the month's Personal trips ("22.0 km · 3"). And "Add missed trip", a plus and the words in lime: the whole tile is the button ("Editing a trip", below). Business and Personal are never added into one figure. (A small line, "Not marked Business or Personal yet: …", stands in the Personal tile only while such a trip exists; see [Schedule and Business/Personal](#schedule-and-businesspersonal).)
   - **A submitted month whose Business trips are no longer what the report held has one more tile there,** with a red mark: "Changed since the report was sent. It listed 4 trips and 51.7 km; the month now has 3 trips and 50.7 km.", and under it a button "Report for the accountant", which also opens the Report screen ([Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission)).
2. Under them a switch that stands on the page, "Show deleted and discarded", off by default. (Until 2026-10-07 it was "Show deleted and discarded trips".) While trips are hidden, a small grey line under it says how many ("2 deleted or discarded trips are not shown.").
3. Then the trips, **one tile per day,** newest day first and newest trip first within a day. **Every day starts closed, today too.** A closed day shows two lines and a small arrowhead: the day ("Today · Tue, Oct 6", or "Mon, Oct 5"), and what it adds up to ("4 trips · 48.2 km business"). **A press on that heading opens the day and shows its trips. Another press closes it.** Shawn asked for this on 2026-10-06: "each day expand when clicked. so it shows the total day driven and when clicked expands and shows each individual trip". The number is of the day's finished trips, Business and Personal together; the km are of the Business ones only.
   - **Which days are open is remembered while Trips stays open:** through a turn of the phone, and through a visit to the edit screen or the Report screen. It is forgotten when another month is shown, and when Trips is left for another screen of the bottom bar: coming back, every day is closed again. **After a save on the edit screen the saved trip's day is open,** so that the trip is not shut away out of sight. The rules are plain functions in `feature/trips/OpenDays.kt`, and `TripsViewModel` keeps the days.
   - **The opening is animated, briefly:** the trips unfold and fade in, and the arrowhead turns to point up. With the phone's animations switched off, all of it happens at once. If the trips would stand below the edge of the screen, the list moves to show them.
   - **A trip's row, as drawn:** where it went ("Shop → Home"), under that in grey its times and what it is saved as ("5:30 – 5:41 PM · Personal", "Business · ran past schedule"), and at the right its km as a bare figure. Where both times are in the same half of the day, "AM" or "PM" is written once, after the end. The figure is white for a Business trip and grey for every other one, so the white figures are the ones that add up to the day's km. **A trip whose figures are Shawn's own says "· added by hand" or "· edited" at the end of the grey line** (see "Editing a trip" below; until 2026-10-07 it carried an asterisk and a line of its own. The report for the accountant still marks the same trips with an asterisk). Where it went: "10103 104 Avenue Northwest, Edmonton → 9321 Jasper Avenue, Edmonton". Shawn asked for this: "I just want to track from start address to end one for each trip." There is no map.
   - **A missing address is said in words,** never as coordinates and never as a gap. While neither address is known: "Looking up the addresses…". When MilO has given up on both: "No address found for this trip." With one known, the other side of the arrow says "looking up the address…" or "no address found". Words that stand in for an address are grey and lighter than the address beside them, as drawn (`placesLine` in `core/designsystem/text/TripPlaceLine.kt`).
   - An address that Shawn left empty himself, in a trip he added by hand or on the edit screen, is "no address entered", and with both empty "No address entered for this trip.": MilO never looked, so "no address found" would not be true.
   - The words are chosen by plain functions in `core/designsystem/text/TripPlaceWords.kt` (which Home uses for the same trips; until 2026-10-06 they were in the Trips screen's own `TripPlaceTexts.kt`) from what the trip's row says (`startPlace` and `endPlace` in `platform/address/AddressRetry.kt`): an address, still being looked up, not found, or left empty by hand.
   - Each time the screen comes to the front it asks for the missing addresses to be looked up (see [GPS recording and distance](#gps-recording-and-distance)). An address that arrives while the screen is open appears by itself.
4. **What is counted.** The totals and the numbers of trips are of the finished trips only (`isCounted` in `data/trip/TripTotals.kt`), added up by what each is saved as (`categoryTotals`, same file), each trip as it is printed (below). A trip discarded as too short (or as a false start), a trip left out by "Ignore them" and a trip Shawn deleted are left out of the list and the totals; a line says how many are hidden ("2 deleted or discarded trips are not shown."). With the switch on they are listed in their day with their km greyed and with what they would be saved as. No total and no count changes. The day's heading adds how many of them it lists ("3 trips · 27.9 km business · 1 not counted"), because a closed day would otherwise hide where they are. Why a trip is not counted stands at the end of its grey line:
   - a discarded trip is marked "Discarded: too short, or a start that was never confirmed. Not counted.", with no line about addresses (a discarded trip is never looked up);
   - a trip left out by "Ignore them" is a discarded trip too, and is marked "Left out: it started outside your work hours, and Settings says to ignore such trips. Not counted." Its km are the full distance that was recorded;
   - a deleted trip is marked "Deleted by you. Not counted.", with the addresses it already had when it was deleted. An address it lacked is "no address found", never "looking up": nothing is looked up for a trip while it is deleted.
5. **A trip in progress** is shown in a tile of its own above the days, "In progress", with where it started, its start time and its running distance (greyed, like every figure that is in no total), and is not in the total until it ends. The tile is always open, has no button, and says why: "Still recording. Not in the month's total until it ends. It cannot be deleted while it is recording." The running distance comes from the trip controller, because the stored row holds 0 while a trip is open; if the controller is not recording that trip, no figure is shown. Where the trip started: "Where it started is not known yet." until the first usable GPS fix, then "Looking up where it started…", then "From (address)", or "No address found for where it started."
6. **Which day and month a trip belongs to.** The day and the month it started in, in the phone's time zone. A trip that runs past midnight is listed once, under the day it started.
7. An empty month says "No trips in (month and year)." in a tile where the days would stand. A month whose only trips are deleted or discarded ones that are not shown says "No counted trips in (month and year)."; the line under the switch says how many are hidden. While a month is being read, the tile says "Reading the trips…" and each figure is a dash.
8. **How the kilometres are added up** (since 2026-10-06, phase 4). One rule for every total MilO shows, the report for the accountant included: **each trip is rounded to a tenth of a kilometre, and the rounded figures are added** (`sumOfTenths` in `core/util/DistanceFormat.kt`). So the rows of a day add up to the figure in the day's heading, the days add up to the month, and the month's Business figure is the total its report prints, to the tenth. The month card, each day's heading, Home's tiles for today and for the month, and the Android Auto screen's Today row all go through that one function, by way of `categoryTotals` and `todayTrips`. Until then the screens added up metres and rounded the sum once: on an emulator one test month read 716.3 km on Trips and 716.4 km on its report, and a day of three trips of 1.1 km read "3.4 km business". A single trip's figure was not changed; `formatDistance` (it was `formatKilometres` until 2026-10-07) is that same rounding written out. Since 2026-10-07 (evening) the rule holds in miles as well, in tenths of a mile ([Units](#units-kilometres-or-miles)).

*Marking a trip Business or Personal.* The brief: "I can switch any trip between Business and Personal."
1. Tapping a finished trip of an open day shows its buttons under it, side by side and equally wide, as drawn: "Edit" in lime (below), then "Personal" on a Business trip or "Business" on a Personal one, and "Delete" in dark red. (A trip that is not sorted yet shows both of the middle ones, and Delete then stands on a second line.) A screen reader is read what each one does: "Edit trip", "Mark as Personal", "Delete trip". Each button is drawn 44 dp high, and the place a finger can hit is 48 dp.
2. The press is made at once, with no question: the other button undoes it. The buttons are put away with the press. Left open, the same spot would at once hold the opposite button, and a double tap would undo the first.
3. **What it does.** The trip's category is written, with the note that Shawn set it by hand (`TripRepository.setCategoryByHand`), and nothing else on the row changes: it stays a finished, counted trip with its times, distance and addresses. Its row, its day's heading, the month's two totals and Home's Today follow at once.
4. **Set by hand is final for MilO.** The catch-up never touches such a trip, and no change of the schedule does. Only another press changes it.
5. **"Ran past schedule" follows the category.** It is shown only on a Business trip. A Business trip that ran past the schedule and is marked Personal stops showing it; marked Business again, it shows it again.
6. **With "Ignore them" on, marking a kept trip Personal does not discard it.** The marking writes the category and never the status.
7. **Only a finished trip can be marked.** A deleted or a discarded trip is restored or counted first, and the trip in progress has no category yet. The rule is in storage too: the update matches a finished trip that does not already have the category (`categoriesOffered` in `data/trip/TripCategories.kt` is the same rule for the screen, so it cannot offer what storage would refuse).
8. **The event log** gets one `TRIP` line per press: "Trip 12: marked Personal by hand on the Trips screen (12340 m, was Business). Nothing else about it changed, and the work schedule no longer decides what it is". A press that changed nothing gets one too ("Trip 12: mark as Personal refused on the Trips screen: it is Personal already. Nothing changed"), and a failure of storage an `ERROR` line; the screen then shows "That trip could not be changed. The Log screen says why."

*Editing a trip, restoring what was recorded, and adding a missed trip* (`feature/tripedit/`). The brief: "I can edit any trip, delete trips, and manually add a missed trip. Manually added or edited trips are marked as such."
1. **The way in.** "Edit" under a tapped finished trip opens the edit screen for that trip. "Add missed trip", the small tile beside "Personal", opens the same screen empty. The screen has a Back arrow, and the bottom bar keeps marking Trips. A trip that is being recorded, discarded or deleted has no "Edit": it is ended, counted or restored first, and storage refuses anything else.
2. **The form, laid out since 2026-10-07 as the owner's design draws it** (the canvas's drawing of "Edit trip"; built, not yet proven on the phone, device checks NL-84 to NL-91). Top to bottom:
   - **"When":** the date as a quiet button as wide as the tile, and under it "Start" and "End" side by side, each a button with its small label over the time. A press opens the calendar or the clock dial. A time that is not chosen yet says "Choose". Under them the switch "Ended the next day".
   - **"Where":** two fields, From and To.
   - **Two tiles side by side:** "Distance, km", a field with the kilometres in a larger figure (typed with a dot or a comma), and "Saved as", with "Business" and "Personal" as two buttons one above the other. The one in force is lime. Neither is, while a trip that is being added has no start time.
   - **A grey line under them says who chose:** "MilO chose this from your work hours." for a trip as it was saved; "MilO chose this from your work hours and the start time." once the start was typed or changed; "You chose this. The work hours no longer decide it." once Shawn pressed one, or for a trip he set by hand before. Two more say that a start time is needed first, and that MilO cannot tell.
   - **The Save button** ("Add this trip" on an empty form).
   - **"What MilO recorded",** a tile with a dashed outline, only for a trip that was edited (step 8).
   - **A trip that is typed in has one grey sentence under the title** that a recorded trip does not have: what adding does, or that MilO has no recording of a trip that was added by hand.
   - **What the drawing took off the form:** the sentence under the title of a recorded trip, the note under the two addresses, and the note in "What MilO recorded". What they said is still true and is in this entry.
   - **The date is the day the trip started.** The end is on the same day unless the switch is on; then it is on the day after, and a line under the switch names that day ("The end time is on Tuesday, October 6, 2026."). A stored trip that ran past midnight opens with the switch on. Switching it off brings such a trip back onto one day; switching it on is how a missed trip across midnight is typed in. **MilO never moves an end to the next day by itself:** an end that is earlier than the start is far more often a slip of the dial, so it is refused, and the refusal names the switch.
3. **Nothing is stored until Save is pressed,** and the screen leaves when it has been. It is the one screen of MilO with a Save button: a trip's figures only make sense together, and one save is one line in the log. **Leaving with something changed and not saved asks first:** "Leave without saving?", with "Keep editing" and "Discard". Every way out asks: the phone's Back, the arrow beside the title, and each button of the bottom bar; "Discard" then leads where the press was going. A form in which nothing differs from what it opened with (also one that was changed and put back) is left at once. The question is the app's and not the screen's (`app/UnsavedWork.kt`): the screen only says whether it holds unsaved work (`holdsUnsavedWork` in `TripForm.kt`).
4. **What the form refuses** (`formProblems`). The end must be later than the start. Neither may be in the future. The trip may not run into the trip that is being recorded: that one runs from its start until some moment still to come, so the rule is that the edited or added trip ends no later than the recording began. The distance must be a number, not less than zero, not more than 2,000 km, and not further than the truck gets in the time (more than 180 km/h on average, which catches a lost decimal point). Zero is taken. **Each refusal is said in red where it is put right:** what is wrong with the times in the tile "When", under the two times and the switch, one line for each thing; what is wrong with the distance directly under the distance field, which is then outlined in red. The screen moves by itself to the first of them, because the Save button is a screen or more further down. Nothing is stored, and a line goes away as soon as what it names is put right. The form is checked at the moment Save is pressed, against the clock and the recording as they are then. Trips that have finished are not compared with each other. Only a failure of storage is said at the Save button itself.
5. **A save writes only what was changed.** Every field that was not touched stays exactly as stored: a recorded time to the millisecond (the form shows whole minutes; a time that is changed and put back to the minute it had is the stored time again), a distance to the metre (typing the figure the form shows, "12.3" over a stored 12,344 m, is not a change), an address as it is. A trip that is opened and saved is not changed and not marked, and the log says so.
6. **What counts as an edit.** The trip is marked as edited when its start, its end, its distance or one of its addresses is different after the save. Business or Personal alone is not an edit: it is written with the note that Shawn set it by hand, as on the Trips screen, and the trip stays unmarked.
7. **What MilO recorded is kept.** The first edit copies the trip's start, end and distance, which are then still the recorded ones, into three columns of the row that are never written again. The trip's positions and its GPS points are not touched by any edit.
8. **Restore recorded values.** The edit screen of an edited trip has one more tile under Save, "What MilO recorded", with the recorded times and km ("7:42 – 8:06 AM · 18.4 km") and the quiet button "Restore". The line starts with the recorded day when the form shows another day. The button asks first, "Restore recorded values?", naming the day, the times and the km that come back, because it drops what Shawn typed in their place. It puts back the recorded start, end and distance exactly, removes any address he typed or emptied and hands it back to the lookup (whose attempts for the trip start afresh; an address the lookup itself had found is kept), and takes the "edited" mark away. The distance is the figure that was kept, not a new calculation from the GPS points (ARCHITECTURE section 6 says why). The trip can be edited again afterwards, and restored again.
9. **A typed address is Shawn's for good.** An address he typed, changed or emptied is never looked up and never overwritten (see [GPS recording and distance](#gps-recording-and-distance)). An emptied one reads "no address entered".
10. **Adding a missed trip.** The form opens on today's date, whichever month Trips was showing, with nothing else filled in: no time, no address, no distance, and neither Business nor Personal chosen until there is a start time to sort by. The date can be any day up to today. The trip is stored as a finished trip marked as added by hand, with no GPS points and no positions, so nothing is looked up for it: its addresses are what was typed, or "no address entered". It is counted from that moment in the month's and the day's figures, in Home's Today and on the Android Auto screen, like any finished trip, and it can be marked, edited and deleted like any other. Editing it never marks it as edited (all of it is by hand already) and it has nothing to restore.
11. **Business or Personal after an edit** follows one rule, set out under [Schedule and Business/Personal](#schedule-and-businesspersonal), step 9: Shawn's own choice wins, a trip set by hand keeps its category, and otherwise a changed start is sorted again by the work hours; "ran past schedule" follows the times.
12. **The event log** gets one `TRIP` line for every save that reaches storage, and it names each value that changed, old and new: "Trip 12: changed by hand on the edit screen: start 2026-10-06 08:14:27 → 2026-10-06 08:05:00; distance 12345 m → 13000 m; from "12 Shop Rd, Edmonton" → "14 Shop Rd, Edmonton" (times in America/Edmonton). It is now marked as edited, and "Restore recorded values" puts back what MilO recorded". After the first edit the row keeps only what MilO recorded, so a value typed in between survives nowhere but in these lines; that is why they spell addresses out, which the lookup's lines never do. A save that changed nothing, a restore ("… recorded values restored on the edit screen: distance 13000 m → 12345 m; from "Home" → none …"), an added trip ("Trip 31: added by hand on the edit screen: … to …, 23400 m, from "Shop" to none, Business (sorted by the work schedule). …") and a write that storage refused each have their line; a failure of storage is an `ERROR` line, and the screen then says "That could not be saved. The Log screen says why." A form that was refused for what is typed in it stores nothing and logs nothing.
13. **Back on Trips after a save, the month the trip is in is shown,** at the top of its list, whichever month was on screen before (`monthOfSavedTrip` in `feature/trips/TripMonth.kt`; the app carries the trip's start from the one screen to the other). **The day the trip is in is opened** (`openDaysAfterSave`, since 2026-10-07, when the days became tiles that start closed), and the days that were open stay open if the month is the same. A trip that is added for last month, or moved there, is therefore in front of Shawn when he comes back, and so is a trip that was filed under today because the date was forgotten. Without this it was stored out of sight, looked as if it had not been saved, and a second attempt stored it twice (seen by a reviewer on an emulator, 2026-10-06). "Restore recorded values" does the same for the month the trip was recorded in. Leaving without a save changes nothing on Trips.

*Deleting, restoring and counting a trip.* Shawn asked on 2026-10-05: "can we delete trips?"
1. **Delete.** Tapping a finished trip of an open day shows its buttons under it, "Delete" the last of them; tapping the trip again puts them away, and so does closing the day. (The line "Tap a trip to edit it, mark it Business or Personal, or delete it." that stood above the first day until 2026-10-07 is not in the drawing and is gone.) One trip at a time shows its buttons. If a button would come up below the edge of the screen, as it does for the lowest trip in view, the list moves up until the trip and its buttons can all be seen. Only a tap moves the list: a button that comes back into view because the list was scrolled stays where it is. A deleted or a discarded trip is not something to tap, and a screen reader does not offer it as one; its own button (below) is. The button asks first: "Delete this trip?", with the trip's times and km and how to get it back, and the buttons Cancel and Delete. Cancel, Back and a tap beside the question change nothing.
2. **What Delete does.** The trip's status becomes `DELETED`, and nothing else on its row changes: its times, distance, positions and addresses stay, and so do its raw GPS points. It leaves the list, the month's total and count, the home screen's Today and the Android Auto screen's Today at once.
3. **Restore.** With the switch on, a deleted trip has a button "Restore" under its row, as wide as the row, without being tapped first. It makes the trip a finished trip again, exactly as it was. No question is asked: nothing is taken away, and Delete undoes it.
4. **Count this trip.** With the switch on, a discarded trip has a button "Count this trip", in the same place. Its row starts with its times, because a discarded trip has no addresses. It makes the trip an ordinary finished trip, with the distance that was measured. It is there because the distance thresholds are untested on the phone and a real trip can have been discarded wrongly. The button is on every discarded trip, a false start included: on a truck whose connection no reading can confirm, a real drive ends up as one. A counted trip is deleted like any other, which is also how counting is undone.
5. **A trip that is still being recorded cannot be deleted.** Its tile has no button and says so. The rule is also in storage: every one of the three changes is one update that matches on the status it starts from (`TripCorrection` in `data/trip/`: finished to deleted, deleted to finished, discarded to finished), so a press that arrives for a trip in any other state, an open one above all, changes nothing. Which button a row shows is taken from the same three lines (`correctionOffered`: the change that starts from the row's status), so the screen cannot offer a change that storage would refuse.
6. **Addresses.** A deleted or discarded trip is never looked up. When a trip becomes a finished one again, by Restore or by Count this trip, the screen asks for a lookup pass at once, and its missing addresses arrive like any other trip's.
7. **The event log** gets one `TRIP` line for every press that reaches storage: "Trip 12: deleted on the Trips screen (12340 m, was finished). It is no longer counted. Its row and its GPS points are kept, and Restore puts it back", "Trip 12: restored on the Trips screen (12340 m, was deleted). It is counted again", "Trip 9: counted on the Trips screen (250 m, was discarded). It is now a finished trip". A press that changed nothing gets one too ("Trip 12: delete refused on the Trips screen: it is still being recorded. Nothing changed"), and a failure of storage an `ERROR` line with the exception. After either of those the screen shows "That trip could not be changed. The Log screen says why." until the next change.
8. **Reading.** Only the month on screen is read: `TripRepository.observeTripsStartedBetween` takes a span of time, and `monthSpan` in `core/util/TimeSpan.kt` gives the span of a month in a time zone (from local midnight on the 1st up to, not including, local midnight on the next 1st, so a month in which the clocks change is an hour longer or shorter). The list follows the database: a trip that closes while the screen is open appears by itself. Which month is the current one is worked out again each time the screen comes to the front.
9. The sums and the grouping are pure functions in `feature/trips/TripMonth.kt` (`monthSummary`, `stepMonth`, `canStepForward`). Which words a row uses for what a trip is saved as, for why it is left out, and on its buttons, which buttons a trip has (`actions`), what a day's heading says, the pill's words and the sentence of an empty month are chosen in `TripNoteTexts.kt`.

**Where the code lives**
- `feature/home/`: `HomeScreen.kt` (the top line and the two warnings), `HomeIdleTiles.kt` and `HomeRecordingTiles.kt` (the two layouts, tile by tile), `HomeTodayTile.kt` (today's trips, in both since 2026-10-09), `HomeOdometerTile.kt` (the odometers, 2026-10-09), `HomeTexts.kt` (the tile and the words both layouts use), `HomeScreenPreview.kt`, `HomeViewModel.kt` (what is read, put together in one step), `HomeUi.kt` (what the screen draws, as plain values), `HomeFigures.kt` (the month's share, today picked out of the month, today's trips newest first, when a report is waiting, where a trip in progress started), `TruckState.kt` (every state of the truck's tile, the one function that chooses, and what stands where the vehicle is named), `ShownArrival.kt` (the stages of the truck's arrival, as plain values) and `HomeShown.kt` (what is drawn at one moment, and the arrival played over what the view model publishes).
- `feature/tripedit/`: `TripEditScreen.kt`, `TripEditTiles.kt` and `RecordedTile.kt` (the screen, with `TripEditScreenPreview.kt`), `TripEditViewModel.kt` (holds the form until it is saved), `TripForm.kt` (the form as plain values: what it starts with, what picked and typed values mean as stored times, metres and addresses, which day it ends on, and whether it holds something that is not saved), `TripFormProblems.kt` (what the form refuses, and which card each refusal belongs to), `TripEditUiState.kt` (what the screen shows, and the Business or Personal a save would store), `TripEditing.kt` (checks, saves, adds and restores, and writes the log line), `TripEditTexts.kt` (the words for each refusal).
- `feature/trips/`: `TripsScreen.kt` (the list, with `TripsScreenPreview.kt`), `TripsTop.kt` (what stands above the trips: the title and its two steps, the switch), `MonthTiles.kt` (the lime tile with the pill that leads to the Report screen and the line that says when the month was submitted, from `monthSubmission` in `data/report/`; "Personal"; "Add missed trip"; the warning for a month that changed), `DayTiles.kt` (a day's tile, and the trip in progress), `TripRows.kt` (a trip's row and its buttons) and `DeleteQuestion.kt`, `TripsViewModel.kt` (which also keeps which days are open, and which day is today), `OpenDays.kt` (the rules for which days are open), `TripMonth.kt` (what is counted, how it is grouped, each day's figures, and the kinds of row, each with the stored status it stands for), `TripPlaceTexts.kt` (which kind of row says where its trip went; the words themselves are in `core/designsystem/text/TripPlaceWords.kt`, and which of them are grey in `TripPlaceLine.kt` beside it), `TripNoteTexts.kt` (the words for what a trip is saved as, why it is left out, its buttons, a day's heading, the pill and an empty month), `TripCorrections.kt` (makes a delete, a restore, a "count this trip" or a marking as Business or Personal, and writes its line to the event log). `core/util/TimeSpanFormat.kt` writes a trip's two times the short way.
- `data/trip/`: `TripEdit.kt` (the rules as pure functions: `editedTrip`, what counts as an edit and what is kept; `restoredTrip`; which trips can be edited; the mark a trip carries), `TripAddedByHand.kt` (`tripAddedByHand`: the row of a trip that is typed in), `TripEditLogText.kt` (the log lines, with every changed value before and after), `TripCorrection.kt` (the three changes as changes of status, and which one a trip of each status is offered), `TripCategories.kt` (what a trip may be marked as, and when "ran past schedule" is said), `TripTotals.kt` (`isCounted`, `categoryTotals`, `todayTrips`), `TripDao.kt` and `TripRepository.kt` (the query by span of time; `correct`, the one update behind all three changes; `setCategoryByHand`; `editByHand`, `restoreRecorded` and `addByHand`, with `rewriteFinished`, the one transaction behind the first two). `data/MiloMigrations.kt` has the step from version 3 to 4.
- `core/schedule/TripRefiling.kt` (`refileTrip`) and `platform/address/AddressRetry.kt` (an address that is Shawn's own is never due for a lookup).
- `app/MiloBackStack.kt` (`TripEditKey`) and `app/MiloNavigation.kt` open the edit screen from Trips, and carry the start of a saved trip back to it. `app/UnsavedWork.kt` and `app/MiloApp.kt` ask "Leave without saving?" before any way out of a screen that holds unsaved work.
- `core/designsystem/text/TripCategoryWords.kt`: which words say Business or Personal, shared by Home and Trips so that a trip reads the same on both. `TripPlaceWords.kt` beside it: which words say where a trip went, shared for the same reason.
- `platform/reminder/ReminderRules.kt`: `reminderMoment`, which gathers what the monthly reminder's rule goes by. The notification and Home's report tile both go through it.
- `core/util/TimeSpan.kt` (a month and a day as a span of time; the day and month of a stored time), `TimeFormat.kt` (the month heading, the day heading, the time of day), `DurationFormat.kt` (hours and minutes) and `DistanceFormat.kt` (`tenthsOf`, the rounding of one trip; `sumOfTenths`, the one rule for a total; `formatTenths` and `formatDistance`, which write them; since 2026-10-07 (evening) each takes the unit, `DistanceUnit`). `core/designsystem/component/TwentyFourHourClock.kt`: whether the phone is set to 24 hours, which every time of day on Home and Trips is written by.
- Tests for Home: `app/src/test/.../feature/home/TruckStateTest.kt` (every state of the truck's tile, where its facts come from, and when the vehicle is named), `HomeFiguresTest.kt` (the month's share at its edges, today picked out of the month, today's trips newest first, the start address, the minute clock) `HomeUiTest.kt` (when the report tile shows, how the screen is put together, and which of two vehicles the truck's tile names), `ShownArrivalTest.kt` (when the arrival plays, for how long, and everything that must not play it) and `HomeShownTest.kt` (the same from moment to moment, on a clock of the test's own); `app/src/test/.../core/designsystem/component/TruckLinkMotionTest.kt` (each movement of the truck's tile against the design's times and sizes); `app/src/test/.../platform/reminder/ReminderMomentTest.kt` (what the reminder's rule is fed).
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../feature/trips/TripMonthTest.kt`, `TripMonthLeftOutTest.kt` (the month once trips can be deleted, and that a row's kind stands for the status its trip has), `TripPlaceTextsTest.kt` and `TripCorrectionsTest.kt` (what is stored after each change, every change refused from every status but its own, the log lines; against the stand-in trips table, through the repository the screen uses); `app/src/test/.../data/trip/TripCorrectionTest.kt` (where each change starts and leads, and which one each status is offered) and `TripTotalsTest.kt` (what counts, today's totals and drive time) and `TripCategoryTotalsTest.kt` (the totals with Business, Personal and unsorted trips mixed; what a trip may be marked as); `app/src/test/.../feature/trips/TripMonthCategoryTest.kt` (the month's two totals, a day's heading, what a row says) and `TripMarkingTest.kt` (what a marking stores, what is refused, the log lines); `app/src/test/.../core/util/TimeSpanTest.kt`, `TimeFormatTest.kt` and `DurationFormatTest.kt`. For editing and adding: `app/src/test/.../data/trip/TripEditTest.kt` (what counts as an edit, what is kept, addresses, a trip added by hand), `TripRestoreTest.kt` (what a restore puts back, and for which trips) and `TripEditLogTextTest.kt` (each line, with old and new); `app/src/test/.../feature/tripedit/TripFormTest.kt` (the empty form, a stored trip's form, that an untouched form asks for nothing, typed distances and addresses, both clock changes), `TripFormProblemsTest.kt` (every refusal at its edge), `TripEditUiStateTest.kt` (what the screen shows, and that the category it shows is the one a save stores), `TripEditingTest.kt`, `TripAddingTest.kt` and `TripRestoringTest.kt` (what is stored, refused and logged, against the stand-in trips table through the repository the screen uses), `TripFormEndDayTest.kt` (a trip past midnight typed in and brought back onto one day; the hour that happens twice when the clocks go back), `TripFormUnsavedTest.kt` (when the form holds something to lose) and `TripEditProblemPlacesTest.kt` (which card each refusal is said in, and where the screen moves); `app/src/test/.../app/UnsavedWorkTest.kt` (which ways out ask, and where each leads); `app/src/test/.../feature/trips/TripMonthByHandTest.kt` (the mark on a row, that such trips are in every total, and the month shown after a save); `app/src/test/.../platform/address/TripAddressesByHandTest.kt` (the lookup keeps away from an address of Shawn's own, and the query agrees with the rule); `app/src/test/.../core/schedule/TripRefilingTest.kt`; `app/src/test/.../core/designsystem/component/DateDialogTest.kt`; and `data/MiloMigrationsTest.kt` for the step to version 4. For the totals: `app/src/test/.../core/util/DistanceFormatTest.kt` (the rule, and that the two ways of adding up do drift apart over a month) and `app/src/test/.../feature/trips/TripMonthReportTotalsTest.kt` (the month card, each day's heading and Today against the report's own figures for the same trips, in a month made to round badly; and what "changed since it was sent" notices and does not). For the layout of 2026-10-07: `app/src/test/.../feature/trips/OpenDaysTest.kt` (a press opens and closes a day, another month starts closed, a saved trip's day is opened) and `TripsWordsTest.kt` (a day's heading, the pill, an empty month, the order of a trip's notes, and which buttons each kind of trip has); `app/src/test/.../core/util/TimeSpanFormatTest.kt` (when "AM" or "PM" is written once and when twice); `app/src/test/.../core/designsystem/text/TripPlaceLineTest.kt` (which part of "from → to" is grey); `app/src/test/.../core/designsystem/component/ChevronIconsTest.kt`.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **Editing and adding have never run on the phone.** They ran on an emulator on 2026-10-06 (FINDINGS_LOG), where the keyboard, the clock dial and the calendar are Google's and not HyperOS's. Device checks 153 to 176.
- **What is typed and not saved is lost only after "Discard"** (step 3), and in one other way that asks nothing: if Android ends MilO while it is in the background. MilO then opens on the edit screen again, with the trip as it is stored (seen on the emulator), and with no question left over. The form is held by the screen's ViewModel and not in the saved state. Turning the phone keeps it, and keeps the question if it was open.
- **The keyboard takes room from the form and does not push it away.** MilO draws behind the phone's status bar, so a window that Android slides up for the keyboard put the form's text under the clock (seen by a reviewer on an emulator, 2026-10-06). The activity is now declared `adjustResize` and `MiloApp` ends the screens where the keyboard begins: the field that is typed into stays whole above it and the form scrolls down to Save with the keyboard open. The bottom bar is under the keyboard meanwhile. Seen on the emulator's keyboard, upright and on its side; HyperOS's own is device check 173.
- **An edit is not checked against other finished trips.** Two trips can be made to overlap, or a missed trip added over a recorded one, and both are then counted. Only the trip that is being recorded is protected.
- **A recorded trip whose stored times make no sense** (the phone corrected its clock during it, so that it ends before it starts) cannot be saved as it is from the edit screen: the form asks for the times to be put right first, even if only an address was to be changed. Marking it Business or Personal on its row still works.
- **The switch says "the next day" and no more.** A trip can end on the day it started or on the day after. A stored trip that ran over more than one midnight (never seen; it would take a truck that stayed connected for over a day) opens as it is stored and names its real end day, but once the switch has been touched it ends on the start day or the next.
- **The hour that happens twice.** On the night the clocks go back (Sunday 1 November 2026, 1:00 to 2:00), a dial cannot say which of the two hours is meant. A time typed over a stored one is read on the side of the change the stored time is on: a trip that ended at the second 1:30 and is given the end 1:40 ends ten minutes later, not fifty minutes earlier (before the review it was saved fifty minutes short, with no warning). For a trip that is being added there is no stored time, and it is the first of the two; a trip across that hour cannot be typed in exactly. On the night the clocks go forward a time in the hour that does not exist is moved on by that hour.
- **A typed time is on the whole minute,** while a recorded one is exact to the second. A trip whose start was edited therefore starts at :00 seconds.
- **The edited mark does not go away by itself.** A trip that is edited back, by hand, to the figures it was recorded with is still marked; "Restore recorded values" is what takes the mark away.
- **A trip's positions are never edited.** A trip whose address was typed over still has the coordinates where the truck was, and they stay the record. A trip added by hand has none.
- **The Trips screen has never been drawn on the phone.** Device checks 76 to 86, and NL-40 to NL-49 for its layout of 2026-10-07. The older checks were written for the layout before it: where one of them names a button or a line that is worded differently now ("Edit trip", "Mark as Personal", "Add a missed trip", the month's card), NL-40 to NL-49 say what is there. It was drawn on an emulator on 2026-10-05, with addresses (FINDINGS_LOG). Deleting, restoring, counting and the home screen's list of today's trips, as it was then, were used on an emulator on 2026-10-06, and are device checks 101 to 110 and 125 on the phone.
- **A trip is two taps away:** one opens its day, one brings up its buttons. That is the price of days that start closed, which Shawn asked for. A day that is open stays open while Trips does.
- **Every trip typed in on the edit screen says "· added by hand",** so its grey line is longer than the drawing's and wraps sooner. A trip the truck recorded has the short line that is drawn.
- **"Next month" is the design's faint grey on the current month.** On the page that grey stands out less than Android's guideline asks of a mark that carries meaning (2.5 to 1, where 3 to 1 is asked). The button is switched off as well, and a screen reader says so. If it cannot be told apart in sunlight (device check NL-40), it gets the lighter grey that `SquareIconButton` uses everywhere else.
- **The arrow in "Shop → Home" is thinner than drawn.** Sora has no arrow, so the phone's own font draws it (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, the same on Home).
- **A deleted trip is never destroyed,** and nothing in MilO can destroy one: there is no "empty the bin". The row and its raw points stay on the phone for good, like a discarded trip's.
- "Count this trip" counts the distance as measured. If the trip was discarded because GPS was poor and the distance came out too short, the counted trip carries that too-short distance; nothing recalculates it from the raw points. Once it is counted it can be edited like any finished trip, and the distance typed in.
- Restore and Count this trip ask no question, Delete does. A trip counted by mistake is put right by deleting it; it then shows as "Deleted by you", not as "Discarded" again.
- Which finished trip shows its buttons, which days are open, and whether the switch is on, belong to the screen: leaving Trips for another screen of the bottom bar and coming back starts with every day closed, the switch off and no button showing. A visit to the edit screen or the Report screen, which are opened on top of Trips, changes none of the three, except that a trip's buttons are put away when "Edit" is pressed.
- The home screen's day and month are worked out when Home comes to the front. Left open across midnight, it keeps showing the day it was opened on, date included, until Home is left and opened again, or MilO is. The Android Auto screen looks at the clock every 10 seconds and does not have this.
- **Home has never shown a real truck.** No truck can be paired on an emulator, so the real app draws "No truck paired" there. The other states are looked at as previews: `HomeScreenPreview.kt` has one for every state and one that plays the arrival, and the debug build can show a preview by its name. On 2026-10-07 a throwaway copy with a stand-in for the truck (not committed) also ran the arrival, End with the truck connected, and the wait beside a parked truck through the real trip controller. That shows that MilO reaches those states on an emulator, not what the truck does. Device checks NL-22 to NL-26 and NL-31 to NL-39.
- **"Connecting…" comes after the connection, not before.** It is played for 2.6 seconds from the moment MilO learns that the truck is there (step 6). Until 2026-10-07 it was not built, for that reason; Shawn asked for it as drawn. If the truck's link is reported late, the tile has said "Connecting…" over a truck that was connected all along.
- **A trip is being recorded while Home still says "Start trip",** for up to 3.8 seconds after the truck arrived with Home open. Pressing it does no harm: there is still one trip, and Home shows it at once.
- **Opening MilO beside a truck it had not noticed plays the arrival too.** Home shows "Not connected" for a moment, MilO reads the truck, and from there it is an arrival like any other.
- **"Truck connected. MilO has stopped watching it" takes two lines** as the tile's title. It is the notification's wording, kept so that both say the same. With its sentence of two lines the tile is then 139 dp high, the highest it gets at the usual font size.
- **With two vehicles paired, the truck's tile still speaks of one.** It names the vehicle that is connected, or the first one paired while neither is, and its sentences say "the truck" for whichever it is. Settings writes "Work truck + 1 more" for the same thing; Home does not.
- **Home prints one decimal where the drawing has none.** The drawing's month reads "214 km". MilO prints "214.0 km", as on Trips and on the report, so that the three never differ.
- **A long pair of addresses takes two or three lines on "Last trip".** The drawing's sample is two short names. Nothing is cut off; the tile grows.
- **The minutes of a trip in progress move on once a minute,** counted from the trip's start, and only while Home is showing.
- **The odometer can step back when a trip ends** (since 2026-10-09, when it began to count up during the trip). The trip's final distance is worked out again from the stored points, without what was recorded after the truck disconnected, and can be lower than the running one; a trip that is discarded as too short, or deleted as a drive in another vehicle, takes all of its kilometres off again. How often the odometer is seen to step back on a real drive is device check HM-10.
- **The odometer counts up only on the phone's Home and in Settings.** The home-screen widget and the Android Auto screen show no odometer (Shawn chose phone only), and the report for the accountant is made from finished trips alone, so a report made during a trip does not hold that trip.
- **A total is the sum of the figures shown, not of the metres behind them** (step 8 of Trips). Three trips of 1,149 m are 1.1 km each and 3.3 km together, although their metres come to 3.447 km. The difference from the real distance is at most 50 m a trip, either way, and evens out over a month; what was gained is that every figure Shawn can add up by hand adds up, and that the phone and the report never disagree.
- **Sending is not done on the Trips screen itself.** Its month tile says whether the month was submitted, and the pill that says so leads to the Report screen, where a month or a date range is made into a PDF, sent, and exported as CSV ([Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission)). The pill and the line under the figures follow the list of sent reports, so it changes by itself when Shawn answers "I sent it" there.
- There is no separate day view: a day is its tile on the Trips screen, which opens under its heading. (Deleting was phase 2 in the brief and was built on 2026-10-06, when Shawn asked for it; Business/Personal, the figures of each day, editing and adding followed the same day.)
- **Every finished trip is listed and counted, Business or Personal.** What keeps a Personal trip off the report is its category, not its absence. Only "Ignore them" takes a trip out of the list, and then as a discarded trip that can be counted.
- A day's heading counts sessions of both kinds and adds up Business km only, as the brief asks. A day of Personal trips only therefore reads "2 trips · 0.0 km business".
- The Android Auto screen's Today row still adds Business and Personal together (see [Android Auto screen](#android-auto-screen)).
- Marking a trip has never been tried with a screen reader, like the other buttons of a row (device check 127).
- The query by span of time is read through an index on the start time, added by the first migration (database version 2).
- "From → to" wraps onto a second line when the two addresses are long; a trip's row is then taller than its neighbours. A finished trip's row is pressed over its whole height, the room above and below its words included: about 60 dp, more than the 48 dp a finger needs.
- The delete, restore and count buttons of a row have never been used with a screen reader (device check 127).
- A trip still recording at the turn of the month is shown in the month it started in, not in the new current month.
- **The month's Business figure is the report's total,** to the tenth of a kilometre, as long as both are worked out in the same time zone: the two read the same trips (finished, Business, started in the month) and add them up by the same function. Seen on an emulator on 2026-10-06: 716.4 km on both, where the build before showed 716.3 km on Trips.
- If the phone's time zone changes, the days and months are worked out again in the new zone the next time the screen comes to the front. A trip near midnight can then move to the neighbouring day.
- **A trip's times have 24 hours or AM and PM as the phone itself is set** ("Use 24-hour format" in Android's date and time settings; until that switch has been touched, Android goes by the language). "08:14 – 08:39" on a phone set to 24 hours, "8:14 a.m. – 8:39 a.m." on one that is not, on Home, on Trips and in the question before a delete alike. Until the review of 2026-10-06 the language alone decided. The switch is read when a screen is drawn and again each time it comes back, so a change shows without a restart (seen on an emulator, device check 152). The Log's times are always 24 hours, to the second.
- A trip whose stored start is later than its end (the clock was corrected during it, see [GPS recording and distance](#gps-recording-and-distance)) is listed as stored.

## Trip labels

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone, and **never run anywhere**: built and unit tested in CI only. Its checks are LB-1 to LB-8 · **Surfaces:** Phone (the Android Auto screen is not touched), and the report · **Last updated:** 2026-10-08

**What it does**
A finished trip can be given a label, such as "Work" or a client's name, picked from the labels used before or typed new. The label used where the trip ended is listed first and already chosen, so a regular stop takes one press of Save. The report for the accountant prints the label as the trip's purpose. Shawn's request of 2026-10-08: "add the ability to add a label for each trip pick from a list of previously used ones. have them from a gps quaridinate so that if im at work and labeled work before that is the one that is choosen from the list".

**Required behaviour** (his answers that day)
- **The place is where the trip ends,** within **200 m** of where an earlier labelled trip ended.
- **Only suggested,** never set by itself: a trip has no label until Save is pressed.
- **Printed on the report,** as the trip's purpose (he chose "Yes, as a column").
- **The phone only.**

**How it works (step by step)**
1. **The button.** A finished trip pressed on the Trips screen shows Edit, **Label**, the category it does not have, and Delete (`TripAction.Label`, `actions`): four or five buttons, so they take two lines. A deleted, discarded or open trip has no Label button.
2. **The choices** (`TripLabelViewModel.onLabel`, a view model of its own beside `TripsViewModel`, which is at its size limit). Every labelled trip is read (`TripRepository.labelledTrips`, `TripDao.findLabelled`), and `labelChoices` (`core/trip/TripLabels.kt`, pure) makes the list: every label once, newest first, labels that differ only in capitals as one, written as last used; and the **suggestion**, the label of the newest labelled trip whose end is within `LABEL_PLACE_RADIUS_METRES` (200) of this trip's end, by the haversine distance, listed first. A trip with no end position (one added by hand) gets no suggestion.
3. **The dialog** (`LabelDialog`): "Trip label", a sentence (with a suggestion: "Work was used where this trip ended. …"), the labels as a column of choice buttons, "No label" for a trip that has one, and a field "New label". It starts on the trip's own label, or else on the suggestion. Typing a label clears the choice; choosing a button empties the field. **Save** saves the typed label if there is one, or else the one chosen, and is greyed while that is what the trip has; Cancel, Back and a press outside change nothing. It is the dialog of `ConfirmDialog` with the edit screen's choice buttons and text field in it: the design's chips are the colour of a tile and would not be seen on one.
4. **Saving** (`TripLabelViewModel.onSave`, `TripRepository.setLabel`): the label is cleaned (`cleanLabel`: spaces trimmed and runs of them made one, at most `MAX_LABEL_LENGTH`, 40, characters; empty is none) and, if it is one of the list's but for its capitals, written as the list writes it. Nothing is written when the trip has it already. It is written to `trips.label` (database version 7) for a trip that is not open; the trip does not count as edited by hand for it. One event-log line for each: "Trip 12: labelled "Work" on the Trips screen (was none, the label used where it ended before)", or taken away, or refused. A label that could not be read or saved shows the Trips screen's line for a change that was not made.
5. **On the list,** a trip's grey line has its label after the times: "7:42 – 8:06 AM · Work · Business".
6. **On the report** ([Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission)): a grey line under the trip's addresses, and the CSV's Purpose column. An export file (format 3) carries each trip's label.

**Where the code lives**
- `core/trip/TripLabels.kt` (`labelChoices`, `cleanLabel`, `LABEL_PLACE_RADIUS_METRES`, `MAX_LABEL_LENGTH`).
- `data/trip/TripDao.kt` (`setLabel`, `findLabelled`), `data/trip/TripRepository.kt` (`setLabel`, `labelledTrips`); the column in `Trip.kt`, `MIGRATION_6_7`.
- `feature/trips/TripLabelViewModel.kt` (`LabelPick`, `TripLabelUiState`, `labelledText`), `feature/trips/LabelDialog.kt`; the button in `TripNoteTexts.kt` and `TripRows.kt`; `TripLine.label` in `TripMonth.kt`. Words in `res/values/strings_trip_labels.xml`.
- `app/TripsEntry.kt` (the Trips screen's two view models, moved out of `MiloNavigation` for its size).
- Tests: `core/trip/TripLabelsTest.kt` (the suggestion within 200 m and not past it, the newest first, capitals, no position, cleaning), `feature/trips/TripLabelViewModelTest.kt` (the dialog's start, save, a typed label as the list writes it, No label, nothing written for the same label, an open trip refused, Cancel), `feature/trips/TripsWordsTest.kt` (the buttons).

**Depends on**
The trips table ([Trip log](#trip-log-home-day-and-month-views)); the end positions recorded by [GPS recording and distance](#gps-recording-and-distance); the report ([Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission)).

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **Every labelled trip is read each time the dialog opens.** A few labelled trips a day are a few thousand in some years: one query, done once per press.
- **Places are compared by where trips ended, not by their addresses,** so a place whose address was never found still suggests its label.
- **The suggestion is the newest label used there,** not the most used: a place that changed name gets its new one after one trip.
- **A trip added by hand has no position,** so it gets no suggestion, and its label suggests nothing for later trips.
- **The CRA and the IRS ask a logbook for each business trip's purpose;** MilO prints one only where the trip was labelled.

**Related**
[Trip log](#trip-log-home-day-and-month-views), [Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission), [Backup, export and import](#backup-export-and-import).

## Monthly PDF and submission

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone. Built on 2026-10-06 (phase 3), with removing a sent report and the two warnings added the same day (phase 4, part A). The Report screen is laid out since 2026-10-07 as the owner's design draws it, with "Preview PDF", "Save PDF and CSV" and "Mark as sent". Ran on an emulator, the migration to database version 5 included. **No email draft has been seen anywhere:** Gmail on the emulator had no account. Device checks 177 to 207, 208 to 231 for part A, and NL-75 to NL-83 for the layout · **Platforms:** Android (phone) · **Last updated:** 2026-10-07

**Kilometres or miles (2026-10-07, evening).** A report is made in the unit chosen in Settings at that moment. In miles the PDF says "Distances are in miles.", titles the column "mi", writes "mi" after the two figures at the top and prints "Total business miles for …"; the CSV's column is titled "mi", still with a dot. Rows, subtotals and the total add up in miles as they do in kilometres. **A sent report keeps the unit it was printed in** (`sent_reports.distanceUnit`, database version 6): it is listed in that unit for good, and "changed since the report was sent" is judged in that unit, so choosing the other unit never raises it. See [Units](#units-kilometres-or-miles).

**What it does**
Makes the report for the accountant, for a month or for a date range, as a PDF and as a CSV file, hands the PDF to the email app, and remembers which reports Shawn says he sent.

**Required behaviour**
- Shawn picks a specific month or a custom date range, not only the current month, and generates a PDF of the Business trips in it. The date range was added on 2026-10-05 ("be able to pick specific month or date range"). Built with Android's own `PdfDocument`, no third-party PDF library.
- Header: name, company, vehicle, month, generated date. Since 2026-10-07 also the app's mark and name, and the period's Business and Personal kilometres, each by itself (Shawn's request of that day).
- One table per day with columns **Start, End, From, To, km**, then a daily subtotal. A month total km at the end.
- **No rate and no amount owed.** Accounts works the money out, so the report shows km only. The home-screen widget's dollars at the rate set in Settings (2026-10-07) are for reference and stay off the report ([Home-screen widget](#home-screen-widget)).
- **The trip's purpose, since 2026-10-08:** under a trip's addresses, in grey across the address columns, its label ([Trip labels](#trip-labels)) and, where the period had several vehicles, the vehicle's name, joined by " · ". A trip without a label has no such line. The CSV has two columns before the distance, **Purpose** and **Vehicle** (the vehicle's name, or its address when it is no longer paired). **With several vehicles each has its two odometer figures, named** (`VehicleOdometer`), since the same day. **The odometer at the start and the end of the period, since 2026-10-07** (Shawn: "this should also show monthly start and finish mileage on the vehicles report to the accountant"), which reverses the kickoff's "no odometer readings": two figures on a grey tile under the sender's, "Odometer, Oct 1, 2026: 120,000 km" and "Odometer, Oct 31, 2026: 121,234 km est.". A figure is printed as typed only when it is a reading typed that day with no truck trip between it and the start or end of the day; every other figure MilO worked out, and it carries "est."
