*If the truck connects and a trip fails to start.* The brief: "If the truck connects during scheduled hours and a trip fails to start, notify me." Nothing was built or changed for this in phase 4. This is what the "MilO could not start this trip. Tap to start" notification of phase 1 ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection)) covers and what it cannot, checked against the code on 2026-10-06.

**It is posted whenever MilO has decided that recording must begin and could not make it begin,** whatever prompted the start (the truck, the companion service, a reconcile, a button) and at any hour: it does not ask the work schedule, so it speaks outside the scheduled hours as well. The failures it covers:
- **the preflight found a reason** (`TripPreflight`): precise location not granted, location not set to "Allow all the time", location switched off for the whole phone, battery use set to Restricted, or Nearby devices not allowed;
- **Android refused to start the trip service** from the background (`TripServiceStarter`);
- **the service started and Android refused to let it enter the foreground** (`TripService`);
- **the service was lost in mid-trip and Android would not start it again.**

For each of them no trip is opened (or the open one is dropped from memory), a `SERVICE` line names the reason, Home and the Android Auto screen show it, and a tap on the notification starts the trip.

**It cannot cover a start that MilO never attempted, or a notification that is not seen:**
- **MilO was never woken.** If HyperOS does not start MilO's process when the truck connects (Autostart off, MilO force-stopped, or the phone not unlocked since a reboot), no code of MilO's runs, and a MilO that is not running cannot notify anyone. This is the failure the brief's sentence is most about, and no notification of MilO's own can reach it. What stands beside it: the Setup screen's rows; the driving alert, which itself needs the phone's driving detection to wake MilO; the reconcile the next time MilO is opened, which starts the trip late if the truck is still connected; and, since 2026-10-06 (evening), the daily "nothing recorded" check, which says at the time set that a work day has no trip yet, provided its own alarm gets MilO to run (above).
- **The Bluetooth permission is missing.** Android then delivers no Bluetooth broadcast to MilO at all. Only the companion service's callback can still arrive, and that start does end in the preflight and the notification.
- **The truck connected and MilO decided not to start.** The hold-off after End trip is set; the reading of the truck came back "unknown" or "not connected" (a truck connected on neither profile MilO can ask); or the device that connected is not the paired truck. These are decisions with a `TRIGGER` line each, not failures, and bring no notification.
- **The trip started and came to nothing:** location was lost after the start, a companion start was never confirmed and was discarded as a false start, or the trip stayed under the minimum distance.
- **The notification was posted and not seen:** notifications switched off for MilO (the `SERVICE` line then says "the warning was not shown"), the "Trip problems" channel silenced, or Do Not Disturb.
- **MilO crashed while starting.** The crash is in the log at the next start; nothing was posted.


Checked on an emulator (Android 16) on 2026-10-03: an induced crash, a force stop and a background kill each appeared in the log at the next start, once. On 2026-10-05 the same emulator showed the trigger lines of the companion service, the boot and update reconcile and the pairing check (FINDINGS_LOG). On the POCO X5 the log has been written and read back over adb twice (2026-10-05: a start refused with its reasons, the trip and sound lines of a trip started with the button, no crash and no error line; 2026-10-06: all 65 earlier lines unchanged after the update, and the line "MilO was updated" written before anyone opened MilO); an induced crash and a kill have not been tried there (device checks 1 to 7). The Log screen was drawn and used on an emulator on 2026-10-06 (phase 4, part A: the chips, the share, the lines down to the oldest), and again on 2026-10-07 in its new layout: each chip, a chip with no line, a line opened, three days of lines with their labels, "Show older entries", the share's question and Android's share sheet, and a font size of 1.3. The two things the share says under the title were drawn in a throwaway copy of the build only. Nothing records the screen being drawn on the phone.

**Where the code lives**
- `feature/eventlog/`: `EventLogScreen.kt`, `LogLine.kt` (one line), `EventLogTools.kt` (the chips, where a share stands, the tile for an empty list), `EventLogViewModel.kt` (the list, the filter and the share), `LogGroups.kt` (`logGroup`, `tagWord`: which chip and which tag), `LogList.kt` (`logDays`, and how the screen writes a time and a day). The text file's time format is `formatLogTime` in `core/util/TimeFormat.kt`. The chips are `ChipChoice`, the tag is `Tag` and the tiles are made of `TileRow`, all in `core/designsystem/component/`. Tests: `LogGroupsTest`, `LogListTest`, `EventLogPageTest`.
- `platform/diagnostics/`: `CrashHandler.kt`, `ProcessExitReader.kt`, `ProcessExit.kt`, `StartupDiagnostics.kt`.
- `data/crash/`: `CrashFileStore.kt` (the crash files and where they are kept).
- `data/eventlog/`: `EventLogEntry.kt` (the table and the categories), `EventLogDao.kt`, `EventLogRepository.kt` (with `trim` and its rule `trimBeforeMs`, the filter, and `readAll`, which reads the whole log in pieces), `EventLogFiles.kt` (the log as a text file: its name, its first lines, a line for each entry, and the writing).
- `platform/reminder/`: `ReminderRules.kt` (`judgeReminder`, `reminderMoment`, `reminderStartsOn`, `monthToRemindOf`, `nextDailyLookMs`: pure), `MonthlyReminder.kt` (looks, shows, withdraws, asks for the alarm, keeps its failures to itself), `ReminderAlarm.kt` (the inexact alarm), `ReminderReceiver.kt` (where the alarm arrives), `ReminderNotification.kt` (the channel, the notification, and `reportMonthToOpen`, which reads the month back out of a tap), `ReminderLogText.kt`. Its settings: `data/settings/ReminderStorage.kt`. Its Settings card: `feature/settings/ReminderTile.kt`.
- `platform/nothingrecorded/`: `NothingRecordedRules.kt` (`judgeNothingRecorded`, `checkedFrom`, `checkedFromMs`, `countsAsRecordedToday`, `nextNothingRecordedLookMs`: pure), `NothingRecordedCheck.kt` (when it looks, the alarm, the watch on the work schedule, a trip that begins taking the notification away), `NothingRecordedLook.kt` (one look: the wait for the trip controller, the second look before a notification, one look at a time, showing and withdrawing), `NothingRecordedFailures.kt` (keeps its failures to itself), `NothingRecordedAlarm.kt` (the inexact alarm, and taking it back), `NothingRecordedReceiver.kt` (where the alarm arrives), `NothingRecordedNotification.kt` (the channel, the notification, and `homeAskedFor`, which reads a tap), `NothingRecordedLogText.kt`. Its settings: `data/settings/NothingRecordedStorage.kt`. Its Settings tile: `feature/settings/NothingRecordedTile.kt` and `NothingRecordedViewModel.kt`. Its words are in a resource file of their own, `res/values/strings_nothing_recorded.xml`. `app/CheckObjects.kt` builds it (`container.checks.nothingRecorded`).
- `platform/car/`: `AndroidAutoLog.kt` (the app-wide watch: what it is handed, the one coroutine that writes its lines, how a failure is kept inside; `AndroidAutoSource`, where its reports come from, and `buildAndroidAutoLog`), `AndroidAutoLogRules.kt` (`judgeAndroidAutoReport`, a line or silence, and the wording: pure). `app/CarObjects.kt` builds it (`container.car.connectionLog`).
- `app/MiloApplication.kt` starts the capture, the trimming, the reminder, the daily check and the watch on Android Auto; `app/MainActivity.kt` has the reminder and the daily check looked at when MilO comes to the front, and passes on what a tapped notification asked for (the reminder's month, or Home for the daily check); `app/MiloApp.kt` opens the Report screen for the one, with `showReport` in `app/MiloBackStack.kt`, and Home for the other, with `showTopLevel`; `app/AppContainer.kt`, `app/ReportObjects.kt` and `app/CheckObjects.kt` build them.
- The trip lines are written by `platform/trip/TripWorker.kt` and worded in `platform/trip/TripLogText.kt` and `TripLedger.kt`. The lines for a trip changed by hand are written and worded in `feature/trips/TripCorrections.kt`, and the lines about the choice of sound in `platform/trip/OwnTripSound.kt`. What the schedule made of a trip is worded in `data/trip/TripCategoryLogText.kt`, for the closing line and for the catch-up's line, which `data/trip/TripCategoryCatchUp.kt` writes. The address lines are written by `platform/address/TripAddresses.kt` and worded in `AddressLogText.kt`. The driving alert's lines are written by `platform/driving/DrivingAlert.kt` and worded in `DrivingLogText.kt`; the line for a failure inside it is written by `DrivingFailures.kt`. The lines about a report, a removed one included, are written and worded in `feature/report/ReportRecords.kt`. The reminder's lines are written by `platform/reminder/MonthlyReminder.kt` and worded in `ReminderLogText.kt`. The lines of the watch on Android Auto outside trips are written by `platform/car/AndroidAutoLog.kt` and worded in `AndroidAutoLogRules.kt`. The daily check's lines are written by `platform/nothingrecorded/NothingRecordedCheck.kt` and worded in `NothingRecordedLogText.kt`. The source of a Bluetooth or companion trigger is worded in `platform/bluetooth/TruckSignals.kt`, and the pairing lines in `platform/bluetooth/TruckPairing.kt` and `TruckPairingCheck.kt`.
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../platform/diagnostics/`, `app/src/test/.../data/crash/` and `app/src/test/.../feature/eventlog/EventLogPageTest.kt`. For phase 4, part A: `app/src/test/.../platform/reminder/ReminderRulesTest.kt` (each condition at its edge, the day in a shorter month and in a leap February, the turn of the year, the time zone, the daily alarm across both clock changes), `MonthlyReminderTest.kt` and `MonthlyReminderFailureTest.kt` (one reminder a day however often MilO looks, withdrawal, the log lines, unreadable settings, a failure that stays inside); `app/src/test/.../data/settings/ReminderSettingsStoreTest.kt`; `app/src/test/.../data/eventlog/EventLogRepositoryTest.kt` (what is trimmed and what never is, the whole log read in pieces, the filter) and `EventLogFilesTest.kt` (the file's name, text and writing); `StartupDiagnosticsTest.kt` for the trimming step; `app/src/test/.../app/MiloNavigationTest.kt` for the back stack a tapped reminder makes. For the watch on Android Auto: `app/src/test/.../platform/car/AndroidAutoLogRulesTest.kt` (a line or silence for every combination of what was known, what is reported and whether a trip is being recorded; the wording) and `AndroidAutoLogTest.kt` (the watch on stand-ins: the first reading and each change written and dated, nothing during a trip, beside a real trip controller only the controller's own line, no trigger and no service start from a report, and each kind of failure kept inside). For the daily check: `app/src/test/.../platform/nothingrecorded/NothingRecordedRulesTest.kt` (a day that is not tracked, the stroke of the set time, a set time before the day's start, each kind of trip, a trip added by hand, an edited trip, already shown today, the switch off, the order of the conditions, midnight), `NothingRecordedClockTest.kt` (both days on which the clocks change, and the moment the daily alarm is asked for, across them too), `NothingRecordedCheckTest.kt` (the check on stand-ins: one notification a day however often MilO looks, a line for every asking, a trip that begins taking the notification away, the alarm taken back with the switch) `NothingRecordedCheckFailureTest.kt` (an unseen notification, unreadable settings, a day that cannot be stored, a trip controller that does not answer, and each kind of failure kept inside), `NothingRecordedCheckWaitTest.kt` (nothing said or written before the second look, a trip stored during the wait, two looks at the same moment bringing one notification, a changed schedule moving the alarm and taking a notification away) and `NothingRecordedWithControllerTest.kt` (beside a real trip controller: MilO opened beside the connected truck, a process the truck started, a trip service that reports in as the wait ends, and one that takes longer than the wait); `app/src/test/.../data/settings/NothingRecordedStorageTest.kt` (the three values read back, their key names, nonsense in the file); `app/src/test/.../feature/settings/NothingRecordedViewModelTest.kt` (the card: stored first, then the check told; a change that cannot be stored).
- What can only be checked on the phone is in `docs/DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST.md`.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- At most 20 crash files wait at once, so an app that crashes at every start cannot fill the phone. The first 20 are kept.
- Android keeps only a small number of exit records per app. If MilO is not started for a long time while being killed repeatedly, the oldest records are gone before they are imported.
- A crash produces two entries: the `CRASH` entry with the stack trace, and the system's `PROCESS` entry saying the process ended by crashing.
- **Lines older than 90 days are gone,** except the newest 1,000 ("Trimming"). A question about a trip from four months ago can no longer be answered from the log; the trip itself is untouched. The two numbers are constants in `EventLogRepository.kt`.
- The lines are in English and are not translated, on the Log screen too: the log is evidence.
- The Log screen has no search within a line. It has a filter by category, and the whole log can be shared as a text file and searched there. The filter shows one category or all of them, never two.
- **A shared log is out of MilO's hands.** It is a file in whatever app Shawn picked. It names the truck, the phone's other paired devices and every address typed or replaced on the edit screen; until 2026-10-06 the log never left the phone at all (ENGINEERING_STANDARDS section 12 says what leaves and how).
- **The watch on Android Auto outside trips has never run on the phone, and never beside the real Android Auto app.** The emulator has only a stub of that app, and the reading there was "not connected" at every start. So on 2026-10-06 a small stand-in was installed on the emulator that answers the Car App Library's question with what the test sets (FINDINGS_LOG). With it: a connection and a disconnection outside a trip were each written within a second, also with MilO three minutes in the background; during a trip started with the button the trip controller's own lines were the only ones; a connection made during a trip and ended after it was written when it ended; one made and ended while MilO was not running left no line, and one still there at the next start was written as "First reading … so MilO did not see it connect". What that cannot show: what the real Android Auto reports and when, and what HyperOS lets through to a MilO that is idle. Device checks 260 to 264.
- **Every process start now writes one more line,** the watch's first reading, unless a trip is already being recorded by then. It is what shows that the watch is running, and what a connection made while MilO was closed is noticed by.
- **In the instant a trip starts or ends, a change can be written by both watches or by neither.** Whether a trip is being recorded is asked when a report arrives, and the trip service's watch begins and ends a moment after the trip controller has published the trip. The gap is milliseconds. At a process start that the truck caused, the watch's first reading can arrive before the trip is open: it is then written, and the trip service's own first reading follows it a moment later.
- **A line is dated when MilO hears of a change, which can be later than the change.** Android 14 may hold a broadcast back from an app whose process is cached in the background until that process is active again, and nothing is known about what HyperOS does with Android Auto's announcement to an idle MilO (device check 264).
- **The watch outside trips never asks again by itself.** The trip service makes the library ask Android Auto once a minute while it believes it connected, because a missed "disconnected" would hold a trip open. Outside a trip a missed announcement costs a line of the log, and nothing else.
- **It asks the Android Auto app once at every process start** (the Car App Library's own query), as the trip service does at every trip start. On a phone without Android Auto the answer is "not connected". **"Type 0" does not say that the Android Auto app answered.** The Car App Library (read from its code in 1.7.0, and again in 1.8.0-rc01 on 2026-10-06: the class is unchanged) hands on 0 when the answer is missing, empty or cannot be read, exactly as for a real "not connected", so a line with "type 0" is written either way and "CarConnection reports no type" is a wording MilO has but this library never causes. Only a line with "type 2" shows that the watch reaches the Android Auto app (device check 261).
- **The daily "nothing recorded" check has never run on the phone.** On an emulator (Android 16, 2026-10-06, with the days made by setting the emulator's clock and time zone): the alarm started a MilO whose process had been ended and the notification was posted; the tap opened Home, from a MilO that Android had to start again (it had been left on Settings) and from the Trips screen; with something typed on the edit screen, "Leave without saving?" came first, and both answers did what they say; a trip started with the button took the notification away at once, and the same trip, discarded for being too short, still counted as that day's trip; switched off, the alarm was gone from Android's list, and back when switched on; after a restart of the emulator the alarm was asked for again without MilO being opened. Whether HyperOS lets the alarm start MilO with Autostart off, whether it delivers it at all to an app it has put to sleep, and whether the notification makes a sound there are device checks NR-3 and NR-9 to NR-11. **The emulator runs were made before the review's two changes** (the second look before a notification, and the schedule followed): those rest on unit tests, beside a real trip controller for the first, and have run on no emulator and no phone (NR-15, NR-16).
- **The daily check can only speak if MilO runs.** It is meant for the day detection has quietly stopped, and the commonest reason for that on this phone is that HyperOS does not start MilO. If HyperOS does not deliver the check's alarm either, nothing is said until MilO is opened, by which time Shawn has seen for himself. The alarm names MilO's receiver directly, like the reminder's; nothing is known about what HyperOS does with it (NR-9 to NR-11).
- **A work day without a drive before the time set brings the notification, every such day.** The check asks only whether a trip was started, not whether the truck stood still. That holds also while MilO waits beside a truck that is connected and parked ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), rule 15): a wait is not a trip, so on a work day with no trip the notification comes then too, beside "Truck connected and parked". A unit test has the two together since the verification of 2026-10-06 (evening) (`NothingRecordedWithControllerTest`): the notification comes during the wait, and the trip that the truck's movement starts takes it away. On the phone the two together have not run. And a trip that the parked rule discarded beside a standing truck counts as a trip started today, so on such a day the check is silent. The text says that this is fine, and the cure is the time: set it to when the first drive of a day is normally over.
- **It asks whether a trip was started, not whether it came to anything.** A trip that was started and discarded for being under the minimum distance counts as that day's trip, so a day on which MilO started a trip and recorded nothing useful is silent. "MilO could not start this trip" and the driving alert are what speak then, if anything does.
- **The trips are found by their stored start.** A trip MilO recorded today whose start was then moved to another day on the edit screen is no longer found, so that day can bring the notification after all. The other way round is caught: a trip of another day that was moved into today does not count, because the start MilO recorded is what is judged.
- **Every asking writes a line,** so opening MilO ten times on one day is ten lines, and a start of MilO from its icon is two (one for the process start, one for MilO coming to the front). That is on purpose (step 11); the log's trimming keeps the newest 1,000 lines whatever happens.
- **Moving the time later after the day's notification takes that notification away, and it does not come back that day:** it was shown today. The same goes for switching the check off and on again.
- **The check wakes the phone once a day** (`RTC_WAKEUP`), for a second or two. The monthly reminder does not; that is the one way in which its alarm differs. Switched off, the check asks for no alarm at all.
- **The day is the phone's day.** "Today" and the time are read in the phone's time zone at the moment of the look, so a work day spent in another zone is checked by that zone's clock.
- **Android removes MilO's notifications when MilO has just been installed or updated.** Seen on a loaded emulator on 2026-10-06: the notification was posted five seconds after `adb install`, and Android cancelled it a second later (its reason code says "the package changed"), while MilO had already stored that the day's notification was shown. On the phone this needs an update installed on a work day after the time set, with no trip that day; the "shown" line is in the Log all the same (device check NR-13). The monthly reminder is open to the same.
- **The check's switch and time are not in an export file,** although they mean the same on any phone ([Backup, export and import](#backup-export-and-import)). An import leaves them as this phone has them; Android's own backup carries them, with the rest of the settings file (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, `[DEBT]`).
- **The tap's request names no trip and no screen but Home.** Like the reminder's, it is a request to MilO's one activity that any app on the phone could send; all it can do is show Home.
- **The reminder has never been seen on the phone.** On an emulator (2026-10-06) the alarm woke a MilO whose process had been killed, the notification was posted and its tap opened the Report screen. Whether HyperOS lets an alarm start MilO with Autostart off, whether it delivers the alarm at all to an app it has put to sleep, and whether the notification makes a sound there (MIUI is reported to switch channel sounds off) are device checks 215 to 219. If the alarm never arrives, the reminder still comes whenever MilO is opened or woken for anything else.
- **The daily alarm is another thing that starts MilO once a day.** A process started by it does what every process start does: the reconcile, the pairing check, the catch-ups, the driving alert's request.
- **The reminder is about last month only.** A month that was never sent stops being reminded of when the next month ends; nothing lists the months that are still open, except the Trips screen, month by month.
- **The reminder does not know that a sent month has changed.** A month with a report is "submitted" for it, however its trips have changed since; the Trips card is what says that ([Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission)).
- **A reminder set to a day late in the month is short-lived:** set to the 28th it is shown for three or four days and then moves on to the next month.
- **The reminder comes while MilO is open, too.** Opening MilO on the reminder day posts the notification a moment later, while the screen is being looked at. It is the same look as every other.
- **A tap on the reminder while the edit screen holds something unsaved** asks "Leave without saving?" first, like every way out of that screen. "Discard" then opens the report; "Keep editing" stays, and the tap is forgotten.
- **The reminder's tap is a request to MilO's one activity** that names a month. Any app on the phone could send the same request; all it can do is open the Report screen on a month.
- Entries are ordered by the time they describe. A crash or a kill is written at the next start but dated when it happened, so it appears further down than the lines around its writing.
- During the hour that is repeated when the clocks go back, two lines an hour apart show the same time. Their order is still right.
- If the event log cannot be written while the trip controller is handling a failure, the process crashes on purpose, which leaves a crash file. A failure must not vanish.
- An unreadable settings file is never reset: a silent reset would lose the truck pairing without a trace. It also no longer crashes the start (that was a crash at every start, with no way out but clearing the app's data, trips included). The start-up import logs an `ERROR` entry and carries on, and the exit records are not imported while the file stays unreadable. Whatever reads the settings next has to deal with the failure itself, and each one that exists does. The trip controller logs it once and runs with the defaults. The pairing check reports `FAILED` with the reason, in its `PAIRING` line. The setup checklist logs an `ERROR` line and shows its rows without the confirmations. The Bluetooth receiver cannot tell whose broadcast it is, so it asks for a reading of the truck in place of trusting the event; that reading comes back unknown for the same reason. The companion service acts on its callback all the same, because Android only calls it for MilO's own association.
- If neither the event log nor the crash folder can be written, start-up does crash: there is nowhere left to record the failure.

---

## MilO's clock

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone. It ran on a stock-Android emulator on 2026-10-09 with the date set a day ahead 18 times, and no trip, wait, hold-off or stored point was changed (FINDINGS_LOG 2026-10-09, "MilO's clock ran on an emulator"). What was changed after that run runs in unit tests only: no notification by a clock on probation, the alarm asked for two minutes ahead, and the alarms asked for when the clock is set back. Device checks CJ-1 to CJ-15 · **Surfaces:** Both. It has no screen of its own; every time on the phone UI and on the Android Auto screen comes from it · **Last updated:** 2026-10-09 (after its run on an emulator; before that, the same day, after its verification: the daily alarms count from boot while the clocks disagree, and an anchor taken with nothing to check it against is on probation; built on 2026-10-07)

**What it does**
MilO keeps the time of day itself. A phone whose date is set a day ahead for a few seconds, which Shawn does to get lives in a game, no longer reaches a trip, a wait, a check or a line of the log. While nobody touches the phone's clock, MilO's time is exactly the phone's. The decision and its price are in `docs/adr/ADR-006-trusted-clock.md`.

**How it works (step by step)**
1. **One clock for everything.** `MiloApplication` makes it before anything else (`miloClock`), and the `AppContainer` hands `container.clock` to every object that needs the time: the trip controller and through it the trip rules, the trip service and its timers, the daily check, the monthly reminder, the driving alert, everything that writes to the event log, the screens' ViewModels (the first start's, What's new's and the trip label's among them), the widget and the Android Auto screen. The header's date and the Log screen's "today" get it through `LocalMiloClock`, which `MiloApp` provides once. Android's backup agent runs without the container and asks `miloClock` itself. **Nothing else reads the phone's clock:** `WallClockReadersTest` reads the source code and fails if something does. The time zone is still the phone's (`ZoneId.systemDefault()`).
2. **The rule** (`core/clock/TrustedClock`, pure). The clock holds an anchor: a time of day, and what the clock that counts from the phone's boot read at that moment. That second clock cannot be set. "Now" is the anchor's time plus what has been counted since. At every reading this is compared with the phone's clock:
   - **Within 2 minutes:** the phone's clock becomes the new anchor and is the answer. A network time correction looks like this.
   - **Further apart:** the phone is not believed, and the answer is MilO's own time. If the phone's clock keeps the same difference for 10 minutes, it is followed: it becomes the new anchor, from that reading on.
   - **"Keeps it" means seen to keep it.** While MilO does not believe the phone, it looks at the clock again every 30 seconds (`ClockWatch`). If more than 2 minutes pass between two looks, the 10 minutes start again. Two looks that both fell into one of Shawn's jumps, with nothing in between, do not make a change that held.
   - It never asks who changed the clock, and does not read the "automatic time" setting.
   - **The two clocks are read as one moment.** A reading asks the phone twice: for the time since boot, and for the time of day. A process that Android stops exactly between the two would see a change that nobody made. So the time since boot is read once more afterwards, and if it moved by more than a second the pair is read again, three times at most.
3. **An anchor on probation** (since 2026-10-09). A MilO process that starts with no usable anchor takes the phone's clock as it is: the first start after an install or an update, the first MilO process after a restart of the phone, an anchor file that is missing or cannot be read. That can be in the very seconds the date is set ahead. So such an anchor is on probation:
   - **It is proven** when the phone's clock agrees with it, to within 2 minutes, at a reading at least 2 minutes (`PROBATION_MS`) after it was taken. That is longer than any of Shawn's jumps. Time alone proves nothing, and neither does a reading that disagrees.
   - **Until then, a phone's clock found more than 2 minutes BEHIND MilO's time is taken at once,** as a new anchor that is on probation itself. The trick goes ahead and then back, so a clock that goes back says that the anchor was taken inside a jump. A phone's clock that goes AHEAD is set aside as always.
   - **A proven anchor is never taken back at once.** A step back is then held for ten minutes like any change.
   - **For the seconds until the date is back, such a MilO goes by the date.** What it stamps then stays stamped.
   - **It shows no notification while its anchor is on probation.** A "No trip recorded today" or a monthly reminder that would be due is not shown and not stored; the Log says "…no notification yet: no trip has been started today, Thu 2026-10-08, but MilO's time is not confirmed yet. It was taken from the phone's clock at a start with nothing to check it against, and the phone's date may be set ahead. MilO looks again once its time is confirmed." The rules that decide whether one is due are not changed.
   - **It is woken two minutes on** (6 below), so that the clock is confirmed, and a notification that is truly due is shown, also when Android has frozen MilO in between.
4. **The anchor is kept in a file,** `no_backup/clock/anchor`: one line with the number of the phone's boot, the time of day, the time since boot, and the probation mark ("proven", or the time since boot at which the probation began). It is read at the start of a process before anything asks the time. So a MilO that Android starts while the date is ahead, as the daily alarm does, has the right time from its first reading. It is written when it is first set, when it is proven, when a change is followed or a date taken at the start is given up, and otherwise at most every 5 minutes, at a reading; on a thread of its own, into a second file that then takes the first one's name in one step. An anchor is used only in the boot it was made in. After a restart of the phone, or with no file, the phone's clock is taken as it is, on probation. A line that does not end with a line end was cut short and reads as no anchor. The folder is never in Android's backup, and the file is not in an export.
5. **One line in the event log for each change MilO saw and did not follow at once** (`ClockWatch`, a `PROCESS` line), written when the change is over:
   - "The phone's date was set 24 h 0 min ahead. MilO saw it back 9 s later and kept its own time."
   - "The phone's clock is 3 h 0 min behind where it was and has stayed so for 10 min. MilO now follows it."
   - "The phone's date was set 24 h 0 min ahead. MilO saw it changed again 5 min later and kept its own time."
   - "MilO started while the phone's date was set 24 h 0 min ahead, and took the phone's time when it came back." (3 above. It gives no length of time: the process that took the date may be gone, and the next one writes the line.)
   A change of a day or more is called "date", a smaller one "clock". "Saw it back … later" is the time between MilO's first look that saw the change and the look that saw it gone. **The first three are written by the process that saw the change begin.** A change that no MilO process saw, or whose process Android ended before the date came back, leaves no line.
6. **The two daily alarms.** Android's alarm service goes by the phone's clock, so it delivers both alarms the moment the date goes ahead. The looks they trigger run on MilO's clock and decide by the real day. Asked for the real next time of day while the phone reads tomorrow, the next alarm would be delivered at once, again and again. So **while the two clocks disagree, the reminder and the daily check ask for the same alarm on the clock that counts from boot** (since 2026-10-09): due after the time that is really left, `ELAPSED_REALTIME_WAKEUP` for the daily check and `ELAPSED_REALTIME` for the reminder, inexact like the normal ones. Setting the date does not move that clock, so the alarm cannot go off at the jump. Each writes a line that says so: "…the next daily look is asked for at 2026-10-08 12:00:00, or soon after it (the daily alarm). The phone's clock is set differently from MilO's own, so Android was asked to count 17 h 27 min from now and not to go by the phone's clock. It is asked in the normal way as soon as the two agree." When the clocks agree again, `ClockWatch` has both ask in the normal way and look; that request takes the other's place, because it is the same request to Android. If no MilO process is there by then, the alarm that counts from boot remains and comes at the real time.
   - **Three ways in all,** decided in one place for both alarms (`askForDailyAlarm`): the clocks disagree, and the alarm counts from boot to the real time; they agree and the anchor is on probation, and the alarm is asked for 2 minutes ahead, counted from boot too; otherwise it is asked for at its time of day.
   - **On probation, 2 minutes ahead.** The line: "Nothing-recorded check: MilO's time is not confirmed yet: it was taken from the phone's clock at a start with nothing to check it against (process start). So Android was asked to count 2 min from now. MilO then asks for the next daily look, and shows no notification while its time is not confirmed." When that alarm comes, the reading confirms the clock, or sees the date back and starts two more minutes; then the alarm is asked for in the normal way. If the phone's clock goes ahead of an anchor on probation and stays, the alarm counts to the real time, so that MilO is not woken every two minutes.
   - **A clock that is set back** makes Android deliver nothing, so nothing would ask. `ClockWatch` has both ask when it sets such a change aside ("the phone's clock was set back and MilO keeps its own time"), and they count from boot.
7. **How MilO hears of a change.** `ClockChangeReceiver` is in the manifest for Android's `TIME_SET` broadcast, which Android may start an app for. It has the clock read once and holds the broadcast open until what follows is done. A change that no broadcast told of is seen at the next reading of the time by anything. While MilO does not believe the phone, and while its anchor is on probation, it looks every 30 seconds by itself; those looks are waits inside MilO's process and do not run while Android has the process frozen. Seen on an emulator: Android froze a MilO that it had started and left alone within seconds. Such a MilO is woken by its daily alarms (6 above).
8. **What Android dates itself.** Android stamps the record of a process's end with the phone's clock. A record dated later than MilO's clock reads is written at the time MilO reads it, with "It ended while the phone's clock was set ahead; this line stands at the time MilO read the record, not at the time the process ended", and the "imported up to" mark is not moved past the present. Each record is looked for in the newest 500 process lines before it is written, so it is written once.

**Where the code lives**
- `core/clock/TrustedClock.kt`: the rule and its five numbers (`FOLLOW_AT_ONCE_MS`, `HOLD_MS`, `SEEN_AGAIN_WITHIN_MS`, `PROBATION_MS`, `ANCHOR_SAVE_EVERY_MS`). `ClockAnchor.kt`: the anchor with its probation mark. `ClockNews.kt`: what the clock tells of the phone's clock.
- `data/clock/ClockAnchorStore.kt`: the anchor's file.
- `platform/clock/`: `PhoneClock.kt` (`miloClock`: one clock per process, the one place that reads the phone's clock, the boot's number, the writing of the anchor), `ClockWatch.kt` (the line, the looking again, the alarms asked for again), `ClockChangeReceiver.kt` (`TIME_SET`), `ClockLogText.kt` (the words).
- `app/`: `MiloApplication.kt` makes the clock and starts the watch; `AppContainer.clock`; `ClockObjects.kt`; `MiloApp.kt` provides `LocalMiloClock` (`core/designsystem/component/LocalMiloClock.kt`).
- The alarms: `platform/clock/DailyAlarmAsked.kt` (`askForDailyAlarm`: which of the three ways, for both), called by `MonthlyReminder.askForAlarm` and `NothingRecordedCheck.askForAlarm`, and the two requests each alarm takes (`setFor` a time of day, `setAfter` a wait counted from boot) in `ReminderAlarm.kt` and `NothingRecordedAlarm.kt`. The notification held back: `NothingRecordedLook.carryOut` and `MonthlyReminder.judge`. The process records: `platform/diagnostics/StartupDiagnostics.kt`.
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../core/clock/` (`TrustedClockTest`, `TrustedClockProcessTest`, `TrustedClockProbationTest`, `TrustedClockReadingTest`, `WallClockReadersTest`, and `JumpingPhone`, the phone whose date a test sets), `.../data/clock/`, `.../platform/clock/` (`ClockWatchProbationTest` among them), and the regression suite of a jump: `.../clockjump/` (a trip, a wait, the hold-off, the grace period, the screens, and `ClockJumpFirstStartTest` for a start inside a jump) and the `…ClockJumpTest`, `…AlarmInAJumpTest` and `…OnProbationTest` files beside the daily check, the reminder, the driving alert, the widget, the Log and the start-up diagnostics; `DailyAlarmAskedTest` for the three ways an alarm is asked for.
- **`WallClockReadersTest` also knows the readers that do not say so:** Android's `DateUtils`, `measureTimeMillis`, a file's date (`lastModified`) and a notification's `setWhen`. Three files may use one of them, each once and each with its reason in the test: `CrashFileStore.kt`, `ReportFileStore.kt` and `TripNotifications.kt`. It reads the code inside a string's `${…}` as code.

**Depends on**
Android only: `SystemClock.elapsedRealtime`, `Settings.Global.BOOT_COUNT` and the `TIME_SET` broadcast. No library and no permission.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **A date that is really changed by hand is followed ten minutes late,** and only by a MilO that has watched it for ten minutes in a row: during a trip, during a wait beside the truck, or with MilO open. A MilO that Android has put to sleep watches nothing (seen on an emulator: idle in the background it had not followed after 10.5 minutes, and followed ten minutes after it was opened). When it follows with its screen open, the header and Home show the old day until MilO is brought to the front again, as at midnight. Until the change is followed, MilO's times differ from the phone's, and the daily alarms count from boot and come by MilO's time.
- **When a change is followed, MilO's time moves by the whole change at once.** A trip that is open at that moment is hit as trips were before MilO kept a clock: the parked rule can close it, a grace period can end.
- **A date held ahead for more than ten minutes is believed.** So is its way back, ten minutes late. MilO cannot tell it from a date that was meant.
- **The first start after the update that brought the clock, and the first MilO process after a restart of the phone, take the phone's clock as it is.** With the date ahead in that moment MilO goes by the date until the phone's clock comes back, and then takes the phone's time at once (3 above). In those seconds it is as it was before MilO kept a clock: log lines are dated tomorrow and stay so, a trip that starts in exactly those seconds is dated tomorrow, and a trip that is open can be closed by the parked rule. It shows no notification and asks for no alarm by that date (3 and 6 above). **Seen on an emulator, before the notification was held back:** 14 lines dated tomorrow, which stand at the top of the Log, above today's newer lines and above the line that explains them, until that time tomorrow has passed. If the process is gone when the date is back, the next MilO process takes the phone's time at its first reading.
- **After a restart of the phone, a notification that is due at MilO's first start comes two to four minutes later,** and the phone is woken once more, two minutes after that start: MilO's clock is on probation until then. The Log has the lines for it. The same happens once, at the first start after the update that brings the clock.
- **A date left ahead for 2 minutes or more after such a start is believed,** and its way back then takes ten watched minutes. His jumps last 5 to 16 seconds.
- **One gap in the probation is known.** MilO starts with no anchor inside one jump, no MilO process reads the clock until a later jump, and a reading falls into that one. The two readings agree and lie more than 2 minutes apart, so the anchor counts as proven with tomorrow's date, and MilO is a day ahead until ten watched minutes. It needs Android to start MilO for both jumps and for neither return, and not to deliver the alarm that is asked for two minutes after the first start, which would be a reading between the two. A unit test pins it; it is logged as debt (FINDINGS_LOG 2026-10-09).
- **While an anchor is on probation a clock that is really set back by more than 2 minutes is followed at once,** with the line "MilO started while the phone's clock was set … ahead…". That is what MilO did before it kept a clock.
- **A phone that keeps no boot number** gets the phone's clock at every start of MilO, on probation, and no anchor is stored. A MilO that starts while the date is ahead then goes by the date until the phone's clock comes back, at every such start. Android has kept this number since Android 7; that the POCO X5 gives it has not been seen. If it does not, every start of MilO writes an `ERROR` line that begins "MilO's clock: this phone gives no boot number" (CJ-1).
- **Whether HyperOS starts MilO for `TIME_SET` has not been seen (CJ-8).** If it does not, a jump that happens while no MilO process runs leaves no line in the Log. The daily alarms do not depend on it any more (6 above).
- **The trip notification's running time is worked out by Android,** from the phone's clock. Posted while the date is ahead, it reads a day long until it is next posted.
- **Lines that the earlier build dated a day ahead stay as they are.** They stand at the top of the Log until their time has come.
- **An anchor that cannot be written** leaves one crash file per process, which becomes a line of the log at the next start, and nothing else: the trip service runs in the same process.
- The clock is read from any thread. A reading takes a lock for a moment.

**Related**
[Diagnostics and reminders](#diagnostics-and-reminders) (the event log, the monthly reminder, the daily check), [Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), `docs/adr/ADR-006-trusted-clock.md`, FINDINGS_LOG 2026-10-07 (night) and 2026-10-09 ("MilO's clock put onto today's main", "What the verification of MilO's clock found" with its two decisions, and "MilO's clock ran on an emulator").

---

## Backup, export and import

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone (phase 4, part B, 2026-10-06). Android's backup is switched on with rules of MilO's own, and the Settings screen has "Export all data" and "Import data". **Since 2026-10-07 the two buttons are drawn as "Export" and "Import",** side by side on the last tile of Settings as Shawn drew it; a screen reader still says the longer names, and this entry keeps them. Where it says "the card", that tile is meant ([Settings](#settings), tile 10). All of it ran on an emulator (Android 16), **never on the phone**: whether HyperOS ever backs MilO up, what its file picker looks like, and a move to a real second phone are open. Device checks 232 to 259, and NL-68 for the tile · **Platforms:** Android · **Last updated:** 2026-10-07

**Format 2 (2026-10-07, evening).** The export file is now format 2. The one difference from format 1: each sent report says which unit it was printed in (`distanceUnit`, "KILOMETRES" or "MILES"). A file of format 1 is still read, and its sent reports are in kilometres. A file of format 2 whose sent report names no unit, or one MilO does not know, is refused as damaged. The main database is version 6 (one new column on `sent_reports`). The choice of unit itself is a setting and is not in the file: an import leaves this phone's choice as it is (see the debt in FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-07 (evening)).

**Format 3 (2026-10-08).** Each trip carries the vehicle it was in (`vehicleAddress`) and its label (`label`), both null where it has none (`FORMAT_WITH_TRIP_VEHICLE`). Files of formats 1 and 2 are still read, and their trips have neither; a file of format 1 or 2 whose trips have either is refused as damaged. The export writes every field, empty ones included (`ExportJson`, `encodeDefaults`). After an import the trips without a vehicle are given the first vehicle's address once more ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), "Several vehicles"). **The settings in the file still name the first vehicle only** (`[DEBT]`).

**Required behaviour**
- The main database (trips, submission status, settings, event log) is included in Android Auto Backup. Raw GPS points live in a second database file that stays out of cloud backup, because Auto Backup is capped at 25 MB and backs up nothing at all once that is exceeded.
- Manual export and import of all data to a file.

**The three copies, and what each depends on**

| Copy | Made by | Holds | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|
| Android's cloud backup | Android, by itself, about once a day when the phone is idle, charging and on Wi-Fi | The trips, the sent reports, the event log, the settings. **Not the raw GPS points** | Backup being switched on in the phone's settings, Shawn's Google account, MilO staying under 25 MB, and **the signing key**: Android restores a backup only into a build signed with the same key |
| A transfer to another phone | Android, when a new phone is set up from the old one | The same, **and the raw GPS points** | Both phones in hand, the signing key |
| An export file | Shawn, with "Export all data" | Every closed trip, every sent report, the settings that mean the same on another phone (all but three: the daily "nothing recorded" check's switch and time, and the parked limit, all added on 2026-10-06, are not in the file yet), and the raw GPS points unless he switches them off. Not the event log | Nothing but the file. It is the one copy that is still good when the key, the account or the phone is gone |

*Android's backup* (`res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml`, `platform/transfer/MiloBackupAgent`).
1. **What is taken** is decided by the rules file, with exclude lines only: Android takes everything of MilO's that is not excluded. So `milo.db` is never named there, and is taken together with the `-wal` file Room keeps beside it. A rule that named `milo.db` alone would leave that second file behind, and on an emulator every trip of a fresh install was in it (the main file was 4,096 bytes, the `-wal` file 716,912).
2. **Taken, in both cases:** `databases/milo.db` with `milo.db-wal`, and `files/datastore/settings.preferences_pb`, the settings.
3. **The raw GPS points** (`databases/points.db` with its three side files) are excluded from the cloud backup and included in a transfer to another phone. On the emulator MilO held a 28.7 MB points database and its cloud backup was 113,664 bytes.
4. **Never taken:** everything under `no_backup/` (crash files, MilO's copies of the chosen sounds, the backup agent's notes, the safety copies, a file that is being imported) and the cache (report files, the log as a text file), both of which Android leaves out by itself; the `-shm` and `.lck` files beside each database; `files/profileInstalled`.
5. **Before the files are collected, each database is brought into one file.** The backup agent opens each database with SQLite itself (not through Room, so no migration runs and no table is touched) and moves the write-ahead log into the main file ("checkpoint, truncate"; `data/transfer/DatabaseSettling.kt`). What is copied is then one file that is whole by itself. On the emulator the backup of a MilO whose `milo.db` was 86,016 bytes with 1,660,392 bytes beside it in the log copied a `milo.db` of 110,592 bytes and an empty log.
6. **Android does not always end MilO before a backup.** The documentation says it does. On the emulator, `bmgr backupnow` with MilO alive ran the agent inside MilO's own process (the same process id), with Room's connections open beside it, and ended that process afterwards; with MilO not running it started a process for the backup. Step 5 is what makes the copy whole in the first case: once the log is empty nothing moves into the main file again before a thousand pages have been written, so the main file does not change in the moment the copy takes.
7. **A backup that is over 25 MB is written down.** Android then backs up nothing and tells nobody. The agent's `onQuotaExceeded` leaves a note, and the next start of MilO writes an `ERROR` line: "Android did not back MilO up: MilO's files are 30.1 MB and the limit is 25.0 MB. Nothing at all is backed up while MilO is over the limit, and the last backup that worked stays as it was. "Export all data" in Settings writes a copy that has no limit". Seen on the emulator with 30 MB of filler in `files/`.
8. **A backup that was collected is one line too:** a `PROCESS` line "Android collected MilO's data for a cloud backup: … Whether the copy arrived is Android's to say (Settings, Google, Backup)". It is the only sign inside MilO that Android backs it up at all. A backup that was refused for its size does not get this line.
9. **A backup Android makes by itself does not end a recording:** `backupInForeground` is left off, so Android's own schedule passes over an app with a foreground service. **A backup that is asked for does not look.** `adb shell bmgr backupnow` during a recording ended MilO's process on the emulator ("has died: fg FGS"); Android started the trip service again ten seconds later, and the trip's points have a gap of 16 seconds. Never run it during a trip. Whether "Back up now" in the phone's own Google settings takes the same path is not known, and HyperOS may not start the service again: do not press it during a trip either (device check 259 finds out, on a trip that does not matter).

*After a restore* (`platform/transfer/BackupAftermath`, at every process start).
1. Android restores when MilO is installed, before it first runs, in a process where MilO's own code is not set up. The agent's `onRestoreFinished` leaves a note; nothing else happens then.
2. **The first ordinary start deals with it.** The restored settings are those of another installation, and four things in them are not true here:
   - **The truck's companion association.** Its number is Android's, from the other phone. MilO asks Android which associations it holds here (`truckOnArrival`, a pure function). With none for the truck, **the truck's address and name are kept and the association's number is removed**, and the pairing check that follows reports "paired before, pair it again": Setup's row says "Work truck was paired, but Android no longer watches for it. Pair it again.", Home says "Setup needs attention", and the pairing screen offers the truck. MilO never believes it is paired because a file says so. If Android does hold an association for that truck here (a restore over an installation that was not removed), the truck stays paired.
   - **The confirmations of the setup checklist** (Autostart, Battery saver, Other permissions, the recents lock). They were Shawn's word about switches of the other phone, and an uninstall resets the same switches on this one. All four are removed, so Setup asks again.
   - **The chosen sounds,** if the file of one in use is not here. It never is: the files are under `no_backup/`. The list of his own sounds and what each of the two sounds plays are removed, and both play their built-in sound (since 2026-10-08 for two sounds; before, for the one).
   - **A wait beside the parked truck** ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), rule 15), if the backup was made during one. It was true of that moment and of nothing else, so it is removed in the same write as the confirmations; the log lines below do not name it. **One gap:** this clean-up runs on a background thread, and the trip controller's first look at storage is asked for in the same moment, so nothing orders the two. If the controller looks first and the backup held a wait less than three days old, it picks the wait up. On a freshly restored phone Bluetooth is not allowed yet, so the truck cannot be read and the wait carries on; the trip service is asked for and refused, and Home shows that a start failed, until the next trip or the next restart of MilO. Read from the code, the last step shown in a test; never seen, and logged as `[DEBT]` (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06 (evening)).
3. Everything else is left as restored: the trips, the sent reports, the event log, the report's four settings, the schedule, the two numbers, the reminder, the date of the last export, a report that waits for "Did you send it?".
4. **Two lines in the log,** dated when the restore happened: `PROCESS` "MilO's data was put back by Android, from a backup or from another phone: … Taken out of the settings, because it was only true of the installation they came from: the confirmations of the setup checklist, and the chosen sounds, whose files are in no backup. Go through Setup again on this phone", and `PAIRING` "After the restore: the truck (AA:BB:…) is known by address and name only. Android on this phone holds no association for it, so it has to be paired again. The association from the other installation was not kept".
5. **After a cloud restore there are no raw GPS points.** Every trip has its times, distance, positions and addresses on its own row, and "Restore recorded values" works from the row too (ARCHITECTURE, section 6), so nothing on a screen or a report changes.
6. **A restored database is opened like any other.** One from an older build is taken through the migrations, step by step; there is no destructive fallback. Seen on the emulator: a version 4 file restored by Android became version 5 at the first start with all 251 trips equal. One from a newer build is not opened: MilO dies at its start ("A migration from 6 to 5 was required but not found"), the file is left with every row, and the newer build reads it.

*Export all data* (the Settings screen's last card; `platform/transfer/DataTransfer`, `data/transfer/`).
1. "Export all data" opens Android's file picker with the name `MilO-export-2026-10-06.json`. Shawn chooses where it goes: Downloads, Drive. MilO needs no permission for that one file.
2. **One JSON document** (`data/transfer/ExportFormat.kt` describes it): what it is and which version of the form (`"format": "milo-export"`, `"formatVersion": 3` since 2026-10-08; 2 since 2026-10-07 (evening), and 1 before), when and by which MilO it was written, a sentence for whoever opens it, **how many of each thing it holds**, the settings, every sent report, every closed trip, and the raw points as rows of seven numbers. One thing on a line, so it can be read and compared.
3. **Every closed trip:** finished, discarded and deleted, with every column of the table, so a trip that is edited keeps what MilO recorded, a deleted one can still be restored on the Trips screen, and a trip sorted by hand stays so. A unit test holds the file against the tables' own column lists (`app/schemas`): a column that is added to a table and not to the file fails the build.
4. **The settings that mean the same on another phone:** the truck's address and name, the grace period and the minimum trip distance, the connect sound's switch, the schedule and the choice for trips outside it, the driving alert's switch, the report's four, the reminder's two. Not the association, the sounds' files, the confirmations, or any of the small pieces of state. Not the trip-start sound's switch either (since 2026-10-08): a gap, like the parked limit's, until a new form of the file takes them all.
5. **"Include the GPS points"** is a switch on the card, on each time the screen is opened. Off, the file says `"points": null`. On the emulator 250 trips with 415,860 points were a file of 32.9 MB, written in about two seconds; without the points, 177 KB.
6. **Not while a trip is being recorded:** the buttons wait and the card says why. A trip in progress has no end and no distance yet.
7. **The file is whole or it is not there.** If the writing fails, or a trip starts while it is written, the file is removed again (or, if the app that holds it will not remove it, the card says that it is not a complete export). The same if a trip has started by the time Save is pressed: the file picker has made the file by then, empty, and MilO removes it.
8. **"Last export: Oct 6, 2026 at 1:29 PM"** stands on the card, with "without the GPS points" where that applies.
9. **A raw point that is stored for no closed trip is passed over, counted and said.** There should be none: they are what is left when the two databases have come apart (the trips put back by Android over an installation that kept its points; an import that could not be put right). The file holds closed trips only and an import refuses a point of a trip the file does not hold, so such a point cannot be in it; and an export that stopped at one could never be made, nor the safety copy every import begins with. The card says in red "Left out of the file: 2,700 GPS points that are stored for no trip this phone holds. The Log screen says which.", and the log's line names the trip ids, the first ten of them. What the file says it holds is what it holds. Seen on the emulator.

*Import data.*
1. "Import data" opens the file picker. **The picked file is first copied into MilO** (`no_backup/import/`), so that what is checked is what is imported.
2. **The whole file is read and checked before anything is touched** (`readExport`, `ExportChecks.kt`): that it says it is an export, in a form this MilO reads; every trip, sent report, setting and point, part by part; and the file against itself: as many of each thing as it says, no id twice, no point of a trip it does not hold. The file is walked through one value at a time, so its size does not matter, and its layout does not either: a file another program saved again is read the same (tried on the emulator with one written by Python, keys in another order).
3. **What is refused, and what the card says:**
   - not an export at all: "That file is not an export of MilO's. Nothing was changed."
   - cut short, or something in it that cannot be (a trip that is still being recorded, a position off the earth, an id twice): "That file is damaged or incomplete, so it was not imported. Nothing was changed. The Log screen says what is wrong with it.", and the log names it: "… Trip 7: its status is "OPEN", and only a closed trip can be in an export".
   - **from a newer MilO:** "That file was written by a newer MilO (export format 2). This MilO reads export formats up to 1. Update MilO on this phone, then import the file again. Nothing was changed."
4. **Then the question,** "Replace everything on this phone?": what the file holds and when it was exported, that importing REPLACES this phone's trips and sent reports (by number) with all their GPS points and merges nothing, what becomes of the settings and the truck, and that a copy of what the phone holds is kept first. "Replace everything" or "Cancel"; a press beside it is Cancel.
5. **A safety copy first.** Everything the phone holds is written to `no_backup/safety_copies/MilO-before-import-<time>.json`, a whole export with every point. If that fails, nothing is replaced. After an import of a picked file the newest three are kept.
6. **Then, in this order** (`platform/transfer/ImportRun`): a note that the import begins (`no_backup/import/begun.txt`); the trips and the sent reports in one transaction of the main database, which also writes a line into the event log; the settings in one write; the raw points in one transaction of the points database; the note taken away. A failure in the transaction of the trips leaves the phone exactly as it was. A failure in a later step does not undo the ones before: the card and the log say what could not be done, and the same file can be imported again. If the points cannot be stored, the points of the trips that are gone are removed, so that no imported trip is ever left with another trip's track.
7. **Not while a trip is being recorded.** Asked when the file is picked, when the question is answered, and once more inside the transaction that replaces the trips. A trip the truck starts after that is a new row with a new id, and its points are not touched.
8. **The truck after an import** (`truckOnArrival`): a phone that is paired with its truck stays paired with it, whatever the file names. Otherwise the file's truck is stored by address and name, **without an association**, and the card says "The truck has to be paired again on this phone before a trip can start by itself."
9. **What this phone knows about itself stays:** the association, the sounds and the trip-start sound's switch, the confirmations, the event log. The report that waited for "Did you send it?" is forgotten: its trips were replaced.
10. **"Put that data back"** stands on the card under each safety copy there is, newest first, each with the day and time it was kept ("Before an import MilO kept a copy of what this phone held then, on Oct 6, 2026 at 3:54 PM."). It offers that copy like a picked file, with the same question, which begins "This is the copy MilO kept before an import, on …" and says what the copy holds. So after two wrong imports the copy from before the first can be put back at once.
11. **Putting a copy back never costs another copy.** MilO keeps a copy of what the phone holds first, as before any import. Then, once the phone holds all of the copy that was put back, that copy is removed (the question says so), and no other copy is: the rule of the newest three is not applied. Pressed for the newest copy again and again, the button goes back and forth between two states and the number of copies stays what it was; the copy of what the phone held before the first wrong import is never pushed out by it. A copy whose points could not be stored when it was put back is kept.
12. **An import that MilO's process is ended in the middle of is finished at the next start** (`platform/transfer/ImportResume`, called last in the start-up work of `MiloApplication`). The trips and the raw points are in two database files and are replaced one after the other, the points taking some ten seconds for a year of driving. A process that Android ends in between (MilO swiped away, an update, a reboot) has stored the file's trips and still holds the raw points of the trips that are gone, under the same trip ids, and can say nothing. The note of step 6 says it for it:
    - **Were the trips replaced?** The note is written before they are, so it cannot say. The line the event log gets in the transaction that replaces them can: it is in the same database file and is there exactly if they were. The note holds that line's time and text.
    - **Not replaced:** everything is as it was. The note, MilO's copy of the file and the safety copy of that import are removed; the card says "MilO was closed just as the last import began, before anything was replaced. Everything on this phone is as it was. Import the file again.", and the log has a `REPORT` line. Nothing is done to the raw points: a trip that started since may have an id the file uses.
    - **Replaced:** the steps after the trips are taken again from MilO's copy of the file, which is still there: the settings, the raw points, the lines in the log, and the rest of the app told. Each can be taken twice. The card shows "Replacing the data on this phone. This can take a minute…" while it does, then "MilO was closed in the middle of the last import. It finished the import when it started again." above the usual "Imported: …"; the log has "MilO was ended in the middle of an import, and has finished it at this start from its copy of the file …". Seen on the emulator: 54 trips and 5,400 points, an import of 47 trips and 405,187 points, `kill -9` three seconds after "Replace everything"; after the next start every trip and every point compared equal to the file, eleven seconds after the process started.
    - **Replaced, and the copy cannot be used** (it is gone or no longer reads as the export it was, or a start has tried it before): the raw points of the trips that are gone are removed and the card says "… The trips and the sent reports are the file's. Its GPS points are not stored, and its settings may not be either. Import the same file again to make it whole. …". Seen on the emulator with the copy removed by hand.
    - **It touches no trip,** and only raw points up to the highest trip id the import knew: a trip that started since has a higher id. So it runs also while a trip is being recorded, as the second half of an import always could.
    - **A start that is itself ended while it finishes is counted** in the note. The start after it only removes the points; after two, the import is left as it is and an `ERROR` line says so ("… two later starts could not finish it. It is left as it is …"). Whatever ended two processes in the middle of this must not go on ending every process that follows: the trip service runs in each of them.
    - **Every safety copy is kept by it,** also beyond three; the next import of a picked file makes room.
11. On the emulator an import of 250 trips and 415,860 points took about ten seconds, and the trips, the sent reports and every point compared equal to what was exported.

**Where the code lives**
- `res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml` and the `<application>` element of the manifest: what Android's backup takes.
- `platform/transfer/`: `MiloBackupAgent.kt` (what Android calls around a backup and after a restore), `BackupAftermath.kt` (the first start afterwards), `TruckArrival.kt` (`truckOnArrival`, the pairing decision, pure), `DataTransfer.kt` (export, the reading of a picked file, the question, and where each stands), `ImportRun.kt` (the steps of one import), `ImportResume.kt` (the start after an import that was cut off), `TransferStatus.kt` (what the screen is told), `TransferLogText.kt` (the log's lines), `TransferFailures.kt` (a failure written down and kept from the process), `PickedDocuments.kt` (the picked file through Android's content resolver), `TransferBuilding.kt` (how the two long-lived objects are made).
- `data/transfer/`: `ExportFormat.kt` (the document and its version), `ExportMapping.kt` (between the tables and the document), `ExportChecks.kt` (what is refused), `ExportWriter.kt`, `ExportReader.kt` and `JsonScanner.kt` (writing and reading it without holding it in memory), `TransferDaos.kt` (the only SQL that can empty a table), `DataExport.kt`, `DataImport.kt`, `TransferFiles.kt` (the copy of a picked file and the note beside it, the safety copies), `ImportBegun.kt` (that note), `BackupNotes.kt` (the agent's notes), `DatabaseSettling.kt` (each database into one file before a backup).
- `data/settings/TransferStorage.kt`: the settings that travel, the date of the last export, what an import writes and what a restore takes out.
- `feature/settings/`: `DataTile.kt`, `DataCardTexts.kt`, `DataUiState.kt`, `DataViewModel.kt`.
- `app/TransferObjects.kt` builds them; `app/MiloApplication.kt` runs the aftermath at every start, and has an import that was cut off finished.
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../data/transfer/` (the round trip of every kind of trip, of the sent reports, the settings and the points; the file against the tables' columns; every rule of the checks; damaged, cut-short and wrong-version files; the scanner; the files MilO keeps), `.../data/settings/TransferStorageTest.kt`, `.../platform/transfer/` (`TruckArrivalTest.kt`, the pairing decision; `BackupAftermathTest.kt`; `DataExportingTest.kt` and `DataImportingTest.kt`, an export and an import from start to end on stand-ins; `ImportCutOffTest.kt` and `ImportCutOffLimitsTest.kt`, an import whose process is ended where the stand-ins say, and the starts after it; `PuttingBackTest.kt`, one copy after another put back; `TransferLogTextTest.kt`), `.../feature/settings/DataUiStateTest.kt` and `DataViewModelTest.kt`.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **Never run on the phone, and a restore cannot be tried there:** it needs an uninstall, which deletes the trips. A restore is first met on the day the phone is replaced. Until then the export file is the copy that is known to work; make one before that day (device checks 235 and 250).
- **Android applies the rules again when it restores, and for a backup made on the same phone it applies the cloud rules.** On the emulator a backup made in transfer mode held `points.db`, and the restore at install on the same emulator left it out; an explicit restore through the same transport in transfer mode (`bmgr restore`) brought back all 83,172 points. So the points arrive only by a restore that Android itself treats as a move from another phone. Google's own phone-to-phone transfer has not been run.
- **The emulator's test transport refuses a transfer over 25 MB as well,** which a real transfer (limit 2 GB) does not. That is how the refused-for-size line was first seen: a 28.7 MB points database in transfer mode.
- **Xiaomi's own phone-clone tool is not Android's transfer.** If it copies MilO's data, MilO's backup agent is not called and MilO does not know that anything happened. The truck's row on Setup still reports the missing association (it asks Android at every start), but the checklist's confirmations would come along as if they were true. After any move to another phone: go through Setup again.
- **A cloud backup needs the same signing key.** A build signed with another key is refused the restore (ARCHITECTURE, section 10). The export file is not tied to the key.
- **A backup may never happen.** Android backs up only with backup switched on, an account set, the phone idle, charging and on Wi-Fi, and whether HyperOS lets it is not known. The `PROCESS` line of step 8 is how to tell.
- **Android ends MilO's process after it has collected a backup,** also when MilO was alive in the background. The next trigger starts it again.
- **The safety copies are inside MilO and go with it.** An uninstall removes them, and no backup or transfer carries them. They undo a wrong import on this phone; they are not a backup.
- **A safety copy is as large as an export with every point,** and three are kept: on a phone with a year of driving that is on the order of 100 MB under `no_backup/`. There can be more than three for a while: putting a copy back and finishing an import that was cut off remove none, and the next import of a picked file keeps the newest three again.
- **Two safety copies kept within the same minute read alike on the card:** it writes the day and the time to the minute. The question that follows the button says how many trips, sent reports and GPS points the copy holds.
- **Finishing an import that was cut off happens at a process start, and takes as long as the points did** (about ten seconds on the emulator for 405,187). For that time it holds the points database for writing, so the fixes of a trip that begins in the same seconds are stored when it is done, later than they arrived and unchanged. An import that is under way when a trip starts always did the same.
- **Not covered: a power cut.** The two databases keep a change safe from the end of a process, not from the phone losing power within seconds of it (SQLite's write-ahead log, as Room sets it up, like every other write MilO makes). A phone that dies at the very moment an import ends could still come back with trips and points that do not belong together. An export would then say "Left out of the file: …"; importing the file again puts it right.
- **Raw points that belong to no stored trip stay where they are.** An export and a safety copy pass over them and count them; nothing removes them but the next import, which replaces every point up to the highest trip id.
- **An import leaves a large log file beside the points database** (`points.db-wal`, as large as the imported points) until Android next collects a backup, which empties it. It holds no data that is not also in the database.
- **An import replaces; it never merges.** A trip recorded on this phone since the file was exported is gone from the lists after the import, and is in the safety copy.
- **The event log is neither exported nor replaced.** It is the record of this phone. It is in Android's backup, so after a restore it is the other installation's log, followed by the lines of this one.
- **A restore over an installation that is still there** (Android Studio's "restore app state", `bmgr restore`) replaces `milo.db` and leaves the `points.db` that was there: the points are then those of the trips before the restore. A restore at install has no such leftovers.
- **Ids in a file are capped at two thousand million** (`MAX_IMPORTED_ID`): the tables number upwards from the highest id they ever held, and one absurd id would leave none for the next trip.
- **A file with an accuracy or a speed that is not a number has it written as "none"**; a position that is not a number stops the export, with the points switched off as the way round. Neither has been seen.
- **`"formatVersion"` must be raised with any change to the document,** and every earlier version kept readable. A new column of `trips`, `sent_reports` or `raw_points` is such a change.
- **No new category in the event log:** the lines of an export and an import are `REPORT` lines, a restore's and a collected backup's are `PROCESS` and `PAIRING`, a refused backup's is `ERROR`. A build of phase 4, part A, installed over this one reads everything (seen on the emulator).
