## Units: kilometres or miles

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone. Built on 2026-10-07 (evening). It rests on unit tests (2,264 in the gate, 88 of them new) and on an emulator (Android 16) that day: Home, Trips, the edit screen, Settings, the Report screen, the widget and the trip's notification in both units; the PDF and the CSV in both units; and the database going from version 5 to 6 over a build of `main` that held a sent report. After a review that evening, the odometer's tile in both units and the form for a missed trip in miles were looked at there again. The Android Auto screen has run nowhere. Device checks UN-1 to UN-12 · **Surfaces:** Both (phone and Android Auto), and the widget, the notification and the report · **Last updated:** 2026-10-07

**What it does**
Shawn's request of 2026-10-07: "also add the ability in settings to change the unitys from km to mile". Asked where, he answered "Everywhere". Asked about the accountant's report, he answered "Follow the setting". So Settings has one choice, Kilometres or Miles, and everything that shows a distance shows it in the unit chosen. Kilometres until he picks Miles.

**It only changes what is shown.** Every trip, every GPS point, the export file and the backup stay in metres. Switching back and forth changes no stored trip and loses nothing.

**How it works (step by step)**
1. **The choice.** Settings, the tile "Units" under "Trips": "Kilometres / Miles" (`feature/settings/UnitsTile.kt`). A press calls `SettingsViewModel.onDistanceUnit`, which stores one value in the settings file (`distance_unit`, "km" or "mi"; `data/settings/UnitStorage.kt`). Nothing is stored until he chooses.
2. **How it reaches every surface.** `ShownUnit` (same file) keeps the unit in memory for the life of the process. `MiloApplication` starts it at process start. Home and Trips get it from the `AppContainer`; so do the Android Auto screen, the widget and the trip's notification. The Report screen, Settings, the odometer's tile and the widget's tile read it from the settings they read anyway. Each follows a change at once. The edit form reads it once from the settings file, when it opens, and keeps it (step 7). The trip service writes its notification again when the unit changes (`TripService.keepNotificationInTheChosenUnit`); it touches no trip.
3. **One rounding rule, in either unit** (`core/util/DistanceFormat.kt`, `DistanceUnit.kt`). `tenthsOf(metres, unit)` rounds one trip once to a tenth of the unit, half a tenth upwards. `sumOfTenths(metres, unit)` adds those rounded figures: that is every total. So a column of trips added up by hand gives the total printed under it, in miles as in kilometres. A mile is 1,609.344 m exactly.
4. **Kilometres are untouched.** In kilometres `tenthsOf` runs the arithmetic it always ran. On the emulator the kilometre PDF and CSV of this build were byte for byte the files `main` made from the same trips, and every screen said the same words.
5. **Money is not converted.** The widget's dollars are Business kilometres at the rate per kilometre, whichever unit is shown (`HomeWidgetContent.kt` always asks for kilometres). The rate is typed and written per kilometre in both units. With Miles chosen the tile adds "$0.70 per km is $1.13 per mile", for reading only (`centsPerMileOf`); nothing is priced at that figure.
6. **The odometer.** A reading is typed in the unit chosen and stored with it ("time:km" as before, or "time:miles:mi"). A reading typed in miles comes back exactly as typed. It is turned into the other unit only when it is shown in it. **The number typed is taken in the unit chosen, whatever the dashboard shows,** so with Miles chosen the tile says so where a reading is typed: the field is "Reading on the dashboard now, in miles", and before the first reading the tile says "Type the miles on the truck's dashboard once." In kilometres both are worded as before (`odometerFieldRes`, `odometerFirstReadingRes`). The figure is the reading plus the truck trips since, each as printed in the unit shown. Which reading is used is always decided in kilometres, so it does not depend on the unit.
7. **The edit screen.** The field is "Distance, km" or "Distance, mi" and shows the stored distance in that unit. A form keeps the unit it was opened in. It reads that unit from the settings file when it opens, in the read that gives it the work schedule (`TripEditing.formSettings`), so a form opened in the first moment of a process is in the chosen unit too. An untouched distance, or the shown figure typed again, stands for the stored metres exactly, in both units. A distance typed in miles is stored in metres (12.3 mi is 19,794.9312 m).
8. **The report.** `mileageReport` gives the report the unit the settings hold at that moment (`MileageReport.unit`). The PDF's words that name a unit, and the CSV's column title, are handed in for that unit (`ReportTexts`). The file's name does not change.
9. **What remembers a report.** A sent report stores the total it printed, in metres, and the unit it was printed in (`SentReport.distanceUnit`; `printedTenths` reads the figure back exactly). The report waiting for "Did you send it?" carries its unit too (`ReportHandOver.unit`), so the answer records what was printed even if the setting changed in between. `changedSinceSent` adds the month's trips up in the newest report's unit and holds them against that report's figure. The unit MilO is set to is not asked.
10. **The event log is left alone.** Old lines are not rewritten. A new line about a report names the unit of its own figure ("3 trips, 70.1 mi").

**What each place shows**
| Place | Kilometres | Miles |
|---|---|---|
| Home: Today, the month, the last trip, the trip being recorded, today's rows | as before | the same figures in miles, "mi" |
| Trips: month tile, Personal tile, day headings, rows, the delete question | as before | miles |
| Trips: "changed since the report was sent" | in the unit of the newest report | in the unit of the newest report |
| Edit screen: the field, its label, "What MilO recorded", the refusals | "Distance, km"; limits 2000 km and 180 km/h | "Distance, mi"; the same limits said as 1242 mi and 111 mph |
| Report screen: Business tile, "…to and km", the questions | as before | miles, "…to and mi" |
| Report screen: the list of sent reports | each in the unit it was printed in | each in the unit it was printed in |
| PDF and CSV | as before, byte for byte | miles; "mi", "Distances are in miles.", "Total business miles for …" |
| The email's first line | "…: 112.8 km in 3 business trips." | "…: 70.1 mi in 3 business trips." |
| Settings: "Shortest trip that counts" | "0.3 km" | the same stored distance, "0.19 mi" |
| Settings: the odometer | whole kilometres; the field is "Reading on the dashboard now" | whole miles; the field is "Reading on the dashboard now, in miles" |
| Settings: the widget's rate | "$0.70/km" | "$0.70/km", and "$0.70 per km is $1.13 per mile" |
| Widget: the trip's distance | "1.5 km" | "0.9 mi" |
| Widget: the dollars | "Oct $79 · 2026 $79 at $0.70/km" | the same, to the dollar |
| The trip's notification | "1.5 km" | "0.9 mi" |
| Android Auto: "This trip", "Today" | as before | miles |
| The event log | not converted | not converted |

**Where the code lives**
- The rule: `core/util/DistanceUnit.kt`, `core/util/DistanceFormat.kt`. The words: `core/designsystem/text/DistanceWords.kt`.
- The setting and the unit in memory: `data/settings/UnitStorage.kt`; `MiloSettings.distanceUnit`.
- Totals: `data/trip/TripTotals.kt` (`categoryTotals(trips, unit)`, `TodayTrips.totals(unit)`).
- The report: `core/report/` (`MileageReport.unit`), `platform/report/ReportTexts.kt`, `feature/report/`.
- Sent reports: `data/report/SentReport.kt`, `Submission.kt`; `data/MiloMigrations.kt` (5 to 6); `data/transfer/` (format 2).
- The odometer: `core/odometer/Odometer.kt`, `data/settings/OdometerStorage.kt`.
- Surfaces: `feature/home`, `feature/trips`, `feature/tripedit`, `feature/settings`, `platform/car`, `platform/widget`, `platform/trip/TripNotifications.kt`.
- Tests: `DistanceUnitTest`, `UnitStorageTest`, `ReportInMilesTest`, and new cases in `SubmissionTest`, `TripFormTest`, `TripFormProblemsTest`, `TripEditViewModelTest`, `OdometerTest`, `OdometerStorageTest`, `HomeWidgetContentTest`, `CarScreenContentTest`, `CarScreenRefreshTest`, `TripMonthReportTotalsTest`, `HomeFiguresTest`, `HomeUiTest`, `ReportUiStateTest`, `ReportRecordsTest`, `ReportSettingsStoreTest`, `ExportReaderTest`, `ExportRoundTripTest`, `MiloMigrationsTest`, `SettingsUiStateTest`, `SettingsViewModelTest`, `HomeWidgetViewModelTest`, `OdometerCardStateTest`, `BusinessAllowanceTest`.

**Depends on**
The settings file; the main database (version 6); nothing new from outside. No library was added.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **A total in miles is not the kilometre total converted.** It is the sum of the miles printed. Seven test trips are 75.2 km and 46.8 mi; 75.2 km converted would be 46.7. Never convert a figure that was already rounded: ask `tenthsOf` or `sumOfTenths` again with the metres.
- **Why miles are divided in decimal arithmetic.** A tenth of a mile is 160.9344 m. Divided as binary fractions, four in ten distances typed in miles that end on half a tenth would round down (0.35 mi would show as 0.3). Kilometres keep the arithmetic they had.
- **A sent report stays in its own unit.** A report sent in kilometres is listed as "112.8 km" also while MilO shows miles. The warning "changed since the report was sent" writes both its figures in the newest report's unit, which can be the other unit than the screen's.
- **"Shortest trip that counts" in miles has two decimals** because its steps are 100 m apart: with one decimal, 0.1 km and 0.2 km would both read "0.1 mi". The trip rule compares metres and did not change.
- **The parked rule's speeds and distances are metric inside** and are shown nowhere.
- **The edit form's two limits did not move.** They are 2,000 km and 180 km/h in metres; in miles the refusal names them as 1242 mi and 111 mph, rounded down so the sentence is true.
- **In the first moment of a process the unit is kilometres,** until the settings file has been read. A notification or a widget drawn in that moment is drawn again when the unit arrives. The edit form does not go by it: it reads the unit from the settings file when it opens. Only if that file cannot be read is the form in the unit the other screens show.
- **The odometer does not check the number against the dashboard.** With Miles chosen and a dashboard in kilometres, 123456 is stored as 123,456 miles, and the report would print it. The tile names miles where the number is typed. The unit a reading is stored in is the one the tile shows at the press of "Save reading".
- **A kilometre report and a mile report of the same month have the same file name and the same email subject.** The unit is said inside: in the PDF after the figures at its top, in "Distances are in miles.", in the column's title and in the total line; in the CSV only in the column's title, "km" or "mi". A revision in the other unit does not say that the unit changed.
- **The choice is not in the export file.** An import leaves this phone's choice as it is. The sent reports' units are in the file (format 2).
- **A screen reader** hears "kilometres" and "miles" for a figure that stands by itself. Inside a sentence ("4 trips · 48.2 mi business") it reads the short word as written.
- **Names in the code still say kilometres in places** where the value is now in the chosen unit (`TripForm.kilometres`, `PrintedRow.km`, `ReportWords.columnKm`, `TripFigures.kilometres`). Each says so in its comment. Renaming them is a tidy-up of its own (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-07 (evening), debt).

**Related**
[Settings](#settings), [Trip log](#trip-log-home-day-and-month-views), [Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission), [Home-screen widget](#home-screen-widget), [Android Auto screen](#android-auto-screen), [Backup, export and import](#backup-export-and-import), [Design system](#design-system)

## Settings

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone. The settings of every phase are stored and have a screen of eleven tiles: the truck, the driving alert, the report's four settings, the monthly reminder's two, the three numbers of the trip rules, the work schedule, the choice for trips outside it, the daily check's two, the connect sound and the trip-start sound (two tiles since 2026-10-08), the home-screen widget's switch (since 2026-10-07), and backup, export and import; and under them, since 2026-10-08, the Version tile, which opens What's new ([Version and What's new](#version-and-whats-new)). Since 2026-10-07 the screen is laid out as Shawn drew it. It ran on an emulator only (device checks NL-60 to NL-74) · **Platforms:** Android · **Last updated:** 2026-10-09

**Kilometres or miles (2026-10-07, evening).** One more tile, "Units", stands under the "Trips" tile: the two-part control "Kilometres / Miles" and a grey sentence that says what follows the choice and that stored trips are not changed. "Shortest trip that counts" keeps its stored metres and its steps of 100 m; with Miles chosen it is written in miles with two decimals (0.3 km is "0.19 mi"). The odometer is typed and shown in the chosen unit, and a reading is kept with the unit it was typed in. The widget's rate stays a rate per kilometre. See [Units](#units-kilometres-or-miles).

**Required behaviour**
- Truck: the paired device, with a way to change it.
- Trip: disconnect grace period (default 2 minutes), minimum trip distance (default 0.3 km), the connect sound (bundled chirp or a chosen audio file) and, since 2026-10-08, the trip-start sound (bundled chime or a chosen audio file).
- Schedule: tracked days and hours, and whether out-of-schedule trips are saved as Personal or ignored.
- Report: name, company, vehicle description, accounts email. There is no rate-per-km setting.
- Reminder: on or off, and the day of the month.
- Daily "nothing recorded" check (not in the brief; Shawn's decision of 2026-10-06): on or off, on by default, and the time, noon by default.

**How it works today**
*The screen* (`feature/settings/`), **the third button of the bottom bar since 2026-10-07**, in Setup's place (Shawn: "the bottom button Setup I want to now be Settings. I want to remove the top Settings button on the home page and integrate the two buttons"). Until then it was opened with a square button with three sliders at the end of Home's top line, and was not in the bar. Its top line ends in "Settings"; Back leads to Home. (Opened with "Open Settings" on the Report screen, it stands on top of that screen: the bar keeps Trips marked, an arrowhead stands before "Settings" at the top, and it, or Back, leads back to the report.)

**The Setup tile, first, above the ten** (since 2026-10-07; Shawn chose "Setup tile in Settings" over the checklist on the same page and over a two-part switch). It is the tile from the top of [Setup](#permission-checklist): "11 of 14 ready" with what the others need ("2 to fix · 1 to confirm") and the bar filled by the share that is ready, with "Setup" and an arrowhead over it. The whole tile opens the checklist. **"2 to fix" is red here,** not grey as on Setup itself, because the rows that say what is wrong are on another screen. While the phone is read for the first time it says "Reading the phone's settings…". It is shown whatever the settings file says: the checklist does not come from it. The count is read again each time Settings comes to the front, as on Setup. It is the Setup feature's tile (`SetupLinkTile`), handed to Settings by the app with Setup's own view model, so it can never count differently from the screen it opens.

**Since 2026-10-07 it is laid out as Shawn drew it** (the canvas's Settings): the same tiles in the same order, with the drawing's sizes, spacing, type, colours and words. What MilO has and the drawing does not show is kept, in the same style: a third number in "Trips", the day of the reminder, a slider for each work day, the daily check, and everything the last tile did. **The sentences that say what a tile's settings are for are put away** behind one small grey line with an arrowhead at the end of the tile ("About the reminder"). A press shows them, another puts them away; they are the sentences the cards had before. What has happened is never put away: a refused time or file, a change that was not stored, the last export. Ten tiles, top to bottom:
1. **Truck**, the left of two tiles side by side. The truck's name as the phone gave it ("The truck" if it has none) and a grey button "Change truck"; with no truck stored, "None paired" and a lime button "Pair truck". Either opens the pairing screen (see [Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection)); Back from there leads back to Settings. The tile shows the name only; whether Android is still watching for the truck is the Setup screen's and the pairing screen's to say.
   - **Since 2026-10-08 the tile is titled "Vehicles"** and its button "Pair or remove": with several paired, "Work truck + 1 more". The pairing screen it opens adds and removes vehicles ([Truck pairing and trip detection](#truck-pairing-and-trip-detection), "Several vehicles"). **The odometer tile is one per vehicle,** titled "Odometer · <name>" when there are several, and a reading is saved for the tile's vehicle; a vehicle's trips are those that name it, and for the first vehicle also those that name none.
   **Odometer**, a full tile right under the two (since 2026-10-07; Shawn chose "Settings, truck tile"; the truck's own tile is a half tile beside the driving alert's switch, so the odometer has the width under the pair). Before the first reading, "Type the number on the truck's dashboard once. MilO adds every truck trip to it from then on." and a lime "Enter reading". After it, the odometer in large figures ("123,802 km") and under it what it is made of: "Your reading of 123,456 km on Oct 3, 2026, plus 345.6 km of truck trips since", or "…No truck trip since.", or "Your reading of 123,456 km, typed today". "Adjust" opens a field, "Reading on the dashboard now" (with Miles chosen: "Reading on the dashboard now, in miles", and the sentence before the first reading is "Type the miles on the truck's dashboard once. …"), with Cancel and a lime "Save reading"; a reading is whole kilometres, seven digits at most, and spaces and commas between the digits are allowed. What is not a reading is said under the field in red, and nothing is stored. **Every reading is kept, with the moment it was typed;** a reading is never changed or taken back, and a correction is a new reading beside it ("each correction is kept with its date"). "About the odometer" says what is counted.
   - *How the figure is worked out* (`core/odometer/Odometer.kt`, Shawn's choices "I enter it, MilO adds trips" and "Every truck trip"). **A truck trip** is a counted trip (finished; not one in progress, discarded or deleted), Business, Personal or not sorted yet, that was recorded with the truck connected or typed in by hand (`movesOdometer`); a trip started with the button that the truck never joined is left out, and a drive in another vehicle is gone from the table anyway. The odometer at a moment is a reading plus the truck trips that started between the reading and that moment, or minus them for a moment before the reading. A trip counts by when it **started**, so a reading typed just after parking, with the trip still open for its ten minutes, already holds that trip. **Which reading:** a reading followed by a later one with no truck trip between is taken for a typing slip that the later one corrects, and is not used; of the rest, the one with the fewest truck kilometres between it and the moment, and of two as close, the later. Kilometres are added as every total in MilO is (each trip to a tenth, `sumOfTenths`), and the figure is rounded to the whole kilometre the dashboard shows.
   - *While a trip is recorded* (since 2026-10-09, Shawn: "can we have the mileage on the home screen change as we drive"; he chose the odometer, on Home and in Settings alike). **The tile counts the trip in progress as it is driven,** once a paired vehicle has been seen connected in it: the figure goes up a whole kilometre at a time, and the line under it names the trip's kilometres with the others ("…plus 357.9 km of truck trips since"). It is the figure Home shows at the same moment ([Trip log](#trip-log-home-day-and-month-views), "Home", step 4, which has the rules). The report for the accountant still counts finished trips only.
   - The readings are kept in the settings file (`OdometerStorage.kt`, one string set entry "time:km" each), which Android's backup carries; an export file does not (`[DEBT]`, FINDINGS_LOG 2026-10-07). The tile has a ViewModel of its own (`OdometerViewModel`), which reads every finished trip and follows the trip controller for the one in progress. Tests: `OdometerTest` (the rules), `OdometerStorageTest`, `TruckTripsTest`, `OdometerCardStateTest`, `VehicleOdometersTest` (the trip in progress) and `ReportOdometerTest` for the report. None of it has run on a phone or an emulator: device checks OD-1 to OD-6, and HM-8 to HM-12 for the counting during a trip.
2. **Driving alert**, the right one (since 2026-10-06; what it does is under [Safety net](#safety-net-manual-button-and-driving-alert)). "Tell me when I drive without a trip" and a switch under it, on out of the box; the whole tile is what is pressed. Under the two tiles, on the page, the line **"About the truck and the driving alert"** opens three sentences: that a trip starts by itself when the truck connects (or, with none paired, that none does); what the alert does and that MilO never starts a trip from it by itself, "the phone cannot tell the truck from any other vehicle"; and that it needs the Physical activity permission, which the Setup screen asks for.
3. **Report for the accountant** (since 2026-10-06; what the report is, is under [Monthly PDF and submission](#monthly-pdf-and-submission)). Right under the two small tiles, because the Report screen sends Shawn here for it. Four fields, each under its name, each with grey example words while it is empty ("[Your name]", "[Company]", "Make, model and plate", "name@example.com"):
   - **Your name**, **Company** and **Vehicle**. Each is stored as it is typed, without the spaces around it, 80 characters at most; an emptied field forgets the value. There is no Save button, as everywhere on this screen.
   - **Accountant's email address**, with the keyboard for an email address ("@" on it, no capitals, no corrections). **An address is stored only once it is one:** something before one "@", and after it a domain of at least two parts that ends in letters, with nothing else in the field. Until then the field is outlined in red and says under it "Not saved: this is not a complete email address. It has to look like name@example.com, with nothing else in the field." The address stored before stays in force until a whole new one is typed. An emptied field forgets the address. **The red always stands around the text it is about.** The Settings screen's ViewModel keeps the text that was refused, not only that something was, and a field that is built again while that ViewModel lives (the phone was turned, or the pairing screen was opened from Settings and closed) starts from that text, in red. It is never drawn around the stored address.
   - A report cannot be made without the name, or sent without the address. Company and vehicle can be left empty, and are then left off the report. **"About these details"** opens the three sentences that say so.
4. **Monthly reminder** (since 2026-10-06, phase 4; what the reminder does is under [Diagnostics and reminders](#diagnostics-and-reminders)). Its title, the line **"Daily from the 1st until the report is sent"** and a switch, on out of the box; the whole row is what is pressed. The line names the stored day ("22nd", "3rd"). While the switch is on, **"Start on this day of the month"** stands under it, with "Day 1" between a minus and a plus button, from 1 to 31. Switched off, that row is not shown; the day is kept, and comes back with the switch. **"About the reminder"** opens: "A notification once a day, from the day set above until the report for last month is recorded as sent. Tap it to open that report. A month without a Business trip brings no reminder." and "In a month that has fewer days, the reminder starts on its last day." Each press is stored at once and has the reminder look again straight away, so a reminder that is showing goes the moment it is switched off or its day is moved past today.
5. **Trips · applies from the next trip.** Three numbers, each with its name at the left and, at the right, its value between a minus and a plus button:
   - **Wait after the truck disconnects** (the grace period): 0.5 to 10 minutes in half minutes, default 2. Shown as "2 min" or "2.5 min".
   - **End a trip after the truck has not moved for** (the parked limit, since 2026-10-06 (evening)): 5 to 30 minutes in steps of 5, default 10. Shown as "10 min". Under 5 minutes a long red light or a level crossing would cut a drive in two; over 30, a truck parked at home with Bluetooth still connected records for most of an hour. The drawing shows two numbers; this is the third.
   - **Shortest trip that counts** (the minimum trip distance): 0.1 to 2.0 km in tenths, default 0.3. Shown as "0.3 km".
   A button is greyed out at its end of the range. Each press is stored at once; there is no Save. The two buttons do not move while the value between them changes (see `StepperRow` under [Design system](#design-system)), so a thumb can stay where it is and press again. **"About these three"** opens "A change applies from the next trip. A trip that is being recorded may already use it." and what each number does.
6. **Work schedule · trips in these hours are Business** (since 2026-10-06; what it is for is under [Schedule and Business/Personal](#schedule-and-businesspersonal)):
   - **Seven day buttons,** "Mon" to "Sun". A work day is lime with dark words; any other day is grey. A press switches the day; its hours are kept either way. A screen reader says the day's full name and "Work day" or "Not a work day".
   - **The hours, in large figures:** "8:00 AM – 4:30 PM" (or "08:00 – 16:30": the phone's own "Use 24-hour format" switch decides, as for every time MilO writes). Under them "5 days · 42.5 h a week", or "No days tracked: every trip is outside the work hours".
   - **The slider with two handles,** a start and an end, on a line from 4 AM to 10 PM with five of its hours named under it. A handle is dragged; a tap on the line does nothing. It moves in quarters of an hour, and the two handles stay at least half an hour apart. Each move is stored as it is made. **Moving a handle sets the hours of all seven days,** the days that are off included, so a day that is switched on later has the hours the slider shows.
   - **Each time can also be set to the minute:** a press on the start or on the end opens the **time picker**, Android's clock dial under a title such as "Every work day: start", with Cancel and OK. Nothing changes until OK. The dial has AM and PM exactly when the times on the screen do.
   - **The schedule is still stored day by day,** and a day can have hours of its own. The line **"Set each day by itself"** under the slider opens a slider for every work day, each with the day's name and its hours (which open the picker for that day, titled "Monday: start"). As soon as two work days differ, the tile shows these sliders by itself, under the caption "Each work day by itself", and no longer the one for the week: one pair of times could not say them. Under a day stands **"Use these hours on every tracked day"** while another work day has other hours; it gives that day's hours to every work day and asks no question. When the work days agree again, the line can be pressed to go back to one slider. A day's own start and end each take up 48 dp, because that day's slider stands right under them; the week's large hours have free room around them and are exactly as high as drawn.
   - **Hours outside 4 AM to 10 PM,** which only the time picker can set, are shown on a line of the whole day, midnight to midnight ("12 AM", "6 AM", "12 PM", "6 PM", "12 AM"). Every slider of the tile then stands on that line. A handle at its very end is 23:59, the latest end there is. The line does not change while a handle is held.
   - **An end must be later than its start.** A picked time that would break that is not stored, and a red line says so directly under the hours it was picked for: "A day's end must be later than its start. Nothing was changed. To move a whole day later, change its end first." If the line would come up below the edge of the screen, the screen moves until it can be read. It stays until the next press. A slider cannot be moved that far, so it is never refused.
   - **"About the work schedule"** opens what the schedule does, when a change applies, and how the slider and the picker differ.
7. **Trips outside the work hours.** One choice of two, side by side in a dark frame, the one in force lime: **"Save as Personal"** (the default) and **"Ignore them"**. Under them stands what the one in force does. With "Ignore them" a trip is still recorded, then left out of the list and the totals; the sentence says where to find it ("on the Trips screen, switch on "Show deleted and discarded" to see one and count it"). The other choice's sentence comes up when it is pressed.
8. **Daily check** (since 2026-10-06, evening; what the check does is under [Diagnostics and reminders](#diagnostics-and-reminders), "The "nothing recorded" check"). Not in the drawing; drawn like the reminder's tile, and placed under the schedule it goes by. Its title, the line "Tell me when no trip has been recorded" and a switch, on out of the box. While it is on, **"Check at"** with a grey button that shows the time ("12:00 PM", written as the phone writes times) and opens the time picker under the title "Daily check: time"; nothing changes until OK. Switched off, the time is not shown; it is kept, and comes back with the switch. **"About the daily check"** opens what the check is for and what a work day is. Each press is stored at once and has the check ask for its alarm again and look straight away, so a notification that is showing goes the moment the check is switched off. **A press that could not be stored is said on this tile,** in the screen's own words ("That change could not be saved. The Log screen says why."), and not at the top of the screen.
9. **Connect sound** and **Trip-start sound,** two tiles one under the other (since 2026-10-08; until then one tile, "Trip-start sound", which is now the connect sound). Each: its title, under it the sound in use ("Built-in chirp" or "Built-in chime", or the name of the file Shawn chose) and a switch; a grey line on when it plays; once he has added a sound, the list to choose from, the same in both; then "Play" and "Add a sound" side by side, and under them "Remove this sound" while a sound of his own is in use on that tile. Under the second tile one grey line: "Both sounds play like an alarm, at the alarm volume: also when the phone is on silent, on vibrate, in Do Not Disturb or in Bedtime mode." See [Connect and trip-start sounds](#connect-and-trip-start-sounds).
10. **Home-screen widget** (since 2026-10-07; what the widget does is under [Home-screen widget](#home-screen-widget)). Not in the drawing; drawn like the sound tiles above it: the title "Home-screen widget", the line "The trip, Start and End, and business kilometres in dollars" and a switch, on out of the box. While it is on, the rate (since the evening of 2026-10-07): a quiet button with "Rate" over "$0.70/km", as the edit screen draws a date. A press opens, in its place, the field "Dollars a kilometre" with the grey example "0.70" and two buttons, "Cancel" and "Save rate", as the odometer's tile does; what is not a rate in whole cents from $0.01 to $5.00 is refused under the field ("Type the dollars a kilometre, such as 0.70: from $0.01 to $5.00."), and one that cannot be stored says so on the tile with an `ERROR` line. Then a button **"Add to home screen"** (only if the home screen takes such a request; the home screen then asks by itself) and the line "To add it yourself: touch and hold an empty spot on the home screen, then Widgets, then MilO." **"About the widget"** opens what the dollars are (the Business kilometres at the rate above, the same for every kilometre), that they are for reference only, and what switching off does. A press of the switch is stored first and then applied to the widget; one that cannot be stored says "That change could not be saved. The Log screen says why." on the tile itself, with an `ERROR` line.
11. **Backup, export and import** (since 2026-10-06, phase 4, part B; what each does is under [Backup, export and import](#backup-export-and-import)). Last on the screen: it is used a few times a year, and it is the one tile that can replace everything. Its title, the line "Keep a copy off the phone a few times a year", and **"Export"** and **"Import"** side by side (a screen reader says "Export all data" and "Import data"). Under them, what the drawing does not show: the switch **"Include the GPS points"**, on each time the screen is opened; **"Last export: Oct 6, 2026 at 1:29 PM"** (or "…, without the GPS points", or "No export has been made on this phone yet."); and, once an import has been made on this phone, for each safety copy MilO holds, newest first, "Before an import MilO kept a copy of what this phone held then, on (date) at (time)." with a button **"Put that data back"** under it. While a trip is being recorded the buttons are greyed out and the tile says "A trip is being recorded. Export and import wait until it has ended." While something is under way it says what ("Writing the file…", "Reading and checking the file…", "Replacing the data on this phone. This can take a minute…"), and the buttons wait. What the last export or import came to stands directly under the two buttons until the next one, one sentence each: with the tick what was done ("Exported: 250 trips, 4 sent reports and 415,860 GPS points."), with the empty ring what is left to do ("The truck has to be paired again on this phone …"), with the red exclamation mark what was not done. The screen moves so that those sentences can be read, once for each thing that changes there while the screen is open. Opened again with an outcome still standing, Settings starts at its top like any screen: the tile draws nothing until its first real state is known (`DataViewModel.state` is null until then), so that what stands there from before is not taken for news. **"About backup, export and import"** opens what Android's own backup takes (the trips, the sent reports, the settings and the event log, with what the event log names), what an export is, what the GPS points are, and what an import does. The tile is shown also when the settings file cannot be read: an export then holds the trips without the settings and says so.
   **Under it, Buy me a coffee** (since 2026-10-08, Shawn's request: "can we add a buy me a coffee link in app and on the website. buymeacoffee.com/SeaWingman"), above the Version tile, drawn as it is: the grey label with its arrowhead, "buymeacoffee.com/SeaWingman" and a grey sentence; the whole tile is one button that opens the page in the phone's browser (`feature/settings/CoffeeTile.kt`). MilO needs no internet permission for it and sends nothing with the press. A phone with nothing that opens a web page says so on the tile. **Not shown in a copy installed from Google Play** (since 2026-10-09, ADR-004): Google Play's payments policy does not allow a link that pays the developer outside it. The app asks Android which app installed it (`platform/system/InstallSource.kt`, read once by `MiloNavigation`); for Google Play (`com.android.vending`) Settings is handed `showCoffee = false`. The APK from GitHub, or a build from the Mac, shows it. A copy from GitHub that Google Play later updated counts as Google Play's.
12. **When a change takes effect.** Nothing tells the trip engine. The trip controller reads the grace period, the parked limit and the minimum distance at every trigger, and the trip service reads the sound settings each time it plays a sound, so the stored value is the one in force from the next event on, with no restart. For a trip that is being recorded this means: a grace period that starts after the change uses the new length, one that is already running keeps its deadline, and the minimum distance that counts is the one stored when the trip ends. The schedule and the choice for trips outside it are read the same way and used at one moment only, the end of a trip: a trip is sorted by what is stored when it ends. No trip that is already saved changes when the schedule does. The driving alert reads the schedule as well, each time the phone reports driving, to keep quiet outside the work hours; it starts nothing. **The driving alert's switch, the reminder's card and the daily check's card are the settings that do tell something.** the alert has no trigger of its own to read it at, so a press has it ask the phone to report driving, or to stop, at once. The reminder is told for a like reason: one that is showing has to go when it is switched off, not at tomorrow's look. The daily check is told so that it asks for its alarm at the new time, or takes the alarm back, and looks again at once. **The daily check also follows the work schedule, and that needs no telling:** it watches the stored schedule itself, so a change on the schedule's card (a day switched on or off, a start or an end moved, the hours copied) has it ask for its alarm again and look, with nothing in the screen's ViewModel for it. The report's four settings are read when a report is made: the next PDF has the new name, and a report already sent is not changed.
13. **If the settings file cannot be read,** the screen says so in place of every tile but the last ("MilO cannot read its settings, so they cannot be shown or changed here. Trips are still recorded, with the usual values."), and an `ERROR` line goes to the event log. If a change cannot be stored, the screen says "That change could not be saved. The Log screen says why."
14. What eight of the tiles show is decided by one pure function, `settingsUiState`, from the stored settings. Some tiles have a state and a ViewModel of their own beside the screen's, because the screen's is at its size limit and none of them shares anything with the others: the daily check's (`nothingRecordedCardState`, `NothingRecordedViewModel`), the odometer's (`OdometerViewModel`), the home-screen widget's (`HomeWidgetCardState`, `HomeWidgetViewModel`, since 2026-10-07) and the last one (`dataCardState`, `DataViewModel`). The ranges and the stepping are `SteppedChoice` in `data/settings/SettingChoices.kt` (the three trip numbers, and the reminder's day); what hours a day may have, and the copy, are `WorkSchedule` in `core/schedule/`. What the week's one slider shows and sets is `core/schedule/WorkWeek.kt` (`weekHours`, `workDaysAgree`, `withHoursOnEveryDay`). Which line the slider draws, and how a place on it becomes a time of day, is `feature/settings/ScheduleSlider.kt` (`hoursScale`, `timeAtMinute`); the slider's own arithmetic is `SpanScale` in the design system. All three are pure functions with unit tests.

*The store.* `SettingsStore` in `data/settings/` keeps the settings in a DataStore Preferences file and is the only way to read or write them. `settings` is a flow of `MiloSettings` that delivers the current values and every change; `current()` reads them once. A value never written reads as its default. Each setter refuses a value that cannot be right: a negative duration, distance or time, a blank address, name or URI, a report text that is blank, untrimmed or over 80 characters, and an accountant's address that is not one email address. The schedule's setter needs no check of its own: a `WorkSchedule` cannot hold a day that ends before it starts. "No truck name" and "no sound name" are passed as null. If the file cannot be read, every read throws: the store never replaces it with empty settings.

Stored now: the truck's Bluetooth address, name and companion association id; the grace period (default 120 s); the parked limit (default 600 s) and, while MilO waits beside a parked truck, when the wait began and the place the truck is parked at (`ParkedStorage.kt`; not a setting, like the hold-off; the place is the closed trip's end position, or its newest usable fix if it never moved); the minimum trip distance (default 300 m); the connect sound and, since 2026-10-08, the trip-start sound, each on or off (default on) and with an optional sound of Shawn's own, as the place of MilO's copy of the file and the name of the file that was picked; the list of his own sounds both choose from; and values that are not Shawn's to choose but must outlive the process: the hold-off after a manual end, stored as the time End was pressed; how far the process-exit records have been imported; and the time at which he confirmed each step of the setup checklist that MilO cannot read (`ConfirmedStep`: HyperOS Autostart, Battery saver, Other permissions, the recents lock). A step that is not stored is not confirmed. Since 2026-10-06 also the work schedule (for each day of the week whether it is tracked, and its start and end as minutes since midnight; default Monday to Friday, 08:00 to 16:30) and whether trips outside it are ignored (default no: they are saved as Personal). A day whose stored times make no sense, which no setter can write, reads with the default hours. Also since 2026-10-06, whether the driving alert is switched on (default yes), and one more value that is not Shawn's to choose: the time the driving alert was last posted (none before the first alert). Since 2026-10-06 also the report's four settings (the name, the company, the vehicle and the accountant's email address; each absent until it is set), and one more value that is not Shawn's to choose: the report that was handed to the email app and waits for "Did you send it?" (`ReportHandOver`: its period, number of trips, total and the time), written before the email app is opened and removed when he answers. Since 2026-10-06 (phase 4) also the monthly reminder: whether it is switched on (default yes), its day of the month (default 1; a stored number that is no day of a month reads as 1), and one more value that is not Shawn's to choose: the month the reminder was last shown for and the day it was shown on (`ReminderShown`), written each time it is shown. Since phase 4, part B, one more of that kind: when the last export was written and whether the GPS points were in it (`LastExport`). Since 2026-10-06 (evening) also the daily "nothing recorded" check, three values read as one (`MiloSettings.nothingRecorded`, a `NothingRecordedStored`): whether it is switched on (default yes), the time of day from which a work day is checked (default 12:00; stored as whole minutes since midnight, and a stored number that is no minute of a day reads as noon), and one more value that is not Shawn's to choose: the day its notification was last shown. Their setters are extension functions beside their keys, in `NothingRecordedStorage.kt`, like the last export's in `TransferStorage.kt`; the time's setter refuses a time with seconds in it. **Which of all these travel in an export is one type,** `TransferredSettings` in `data/settings/TransferStorage.kt`: the truck's address and name, the grace period and the minimum trip distance, the connect sound's switch, the schedule and the choice for trips outside it, the driving alert's switch, the report's four and the reminder's two. Everything else is about one phone or one moment and stays behind, **with two exceptions that are gaps and not rules: the daily check's switch and time, and the parked limit, mean the same on any phone and are not in the type yet** (Edge cases). Since 2026-10-07 also the home-screen widget's switch (`home_widget_enabled`, default on, `WidgetStorage.kt`); it stays behind on purpose, because the widget it offers is this phone's home screen's. Android's backup carries it with the file, and it is applied again at the next process start. Since 2026-10-08 also the vehicles beside the first (`more_vehicles`, `VehicleStorage.kt`), each with its address, name, association id and time of pairing; the vehicle an odometer reading was typed for, after a bar in its entry ("time:km|AA:BB:…"); and one more value that is not Shawn's to choose: whether the trips recorded before several vehicles have been given the first one (`trip_vehicles_filled`). An export carries the first vehicle only (`[DEBT]`).

Who reads and writes: the trip controller reads the grace period, the parked limit and the minimum trip distance at every trigger, and writes the wait beside a parked truck when it begins and ends. It reads the stored hold-off only when it picks the state up from storage (after a process start, or after it dropped what it held in memory); from then on it keeps the hold-off in memory and writes every change. The trip service reads the sound settings each time it plays a sound. The Settings screen writes the grace period, the parked limit, the minimum distance, the two sound switches, the schedule and the choice for trips outside it, and `OwnTripSound` writes the custom sound. The schedule and that choice are read by the trip controller with the other settings at every trigger and used only when a trip closes, and by the catch-up at process start. The driving alert reads its own switch, the schedule, whether a truck is stored and the time of its last alert each time the phone reports driving, and the switch again whenever it looks at what to ask of the phone; it writes the time of an alert when it posts one. The Settings screen writes the switch, and the setup checklist reads it for the Physical activity row. The report's four settings are written by the Settings screen and read by the Report screen each time it shows or makes a report; the waiting report is written and removed by the Report screen (`ReportRecords`). The monthly reminder reads its switch, its day and the record of when it was last shown at every look, and writes that record when it shows one; the Settings screen writes the switch and the day. The daily check reads its switch, its time, the schedule and the day its notification was last shown at every look, and the first three again whenever it asks for its alarm; it writes that day when it shows the notification, and the card of the Settings screen writes the switch and the time. The truck's three values are written by `TruckPairing` (from the pairing screen, or when it adopts an association), and the confirmations by the setup checklist. Three more writes come from backup, export and import, all in `TransferStorage.kt`: the date of an export (`setLastExport`); the settings an import brought, in one write, with the truck stored without an association unless this phone's own truck is kept (`replaceTransferred`); and, after a restore, the removal of the association's number, the four confirmations, a stored wait beside the parked truck and a sound whose file is not here (`forgetOtherInstallation`). The truck's values are read by the Bluetooth receiver and the companion service at every event (to tell whether it is about the truck) and by every reading of the truck's connection.

**Where the code lives**
- `feature/settings/`: `SettingsScreen.kt` (the tiles in their order, the sound's file picker) and `SettingsScreenPreview.kt` (four states of the whole screen, for Android Studio and for an emulator); `SettingsTiles.kt` (the truck beside the driving alert, the three numbers), `ReportDetailsTile.kt` (the report's four fields), `ReminderTile.kt` (the monthly reminder's switch and day), `SoundTile.kt` (one tile for either sound); the work schedule in four files, `ScheduleTile.kt` (the tile), `ScheduleParts.kt` (the hours written out, one day's slider, a refused time, and the tile for trips outside the hours), `ScheduleTileState.kt` (what the tile keeps for itself: the span a slider shows while its handle moves, which time the picker is open for) and `ScheduleSlider.kt` (the line and the times, pure); `SettingsViewModel.kt`, `SettingsUiState.kt` (what the screen shows and the function that decides it). The daily check's tile: `NothingRecordedTile.kt` and `NothingRecordedViewModel.kt` (with `nothingRecordedCardState`); its words are in `res/values/strings_nothing_recorded.xml`. The last tile: `DataTile.kt` (with the two file pickers and the question before an import), `DataCardTexts.kt`, `DataUiState.kt`, `DataViewModel.kt`. The words that came with the new layout are in `res/values/strings_settings_layout.xml`.
- `data/settings/`: `SettingsStore.kt`, `MiloSettings.kt` (the values and their defaults), `SettingChoices.kt` (the values the screen offers), `ScheduleStorage.kt` (the schedule's keys in the settings file), `ReportDetails.kt` (what a typed report setting is stored as, and what an email address must look like), `ReportHandOver.kt` (the report that waits for its answer, and its keys), `ReminderStorage.kt` (the monthly reminder's three values and their four keys), `NothingRecordedStorage.kt` (the daily check's three values, their three keys and their setters), `TransferStorage.kt` (the settings that travel in an export, the date of the last export and its two keys, and what an import and a restore write).
- `core/schedule/WorkSchedule.kt`: the schedule itself and its rules. `core/schedule/WorkWeek.kt`: the schedule read as one week.
- `core/util/DurationFormat.kt`: `formatMinutes`, the "2.5" of "2.5 min". `core/util/TimeFormat.kt`: `formatClockTime` (a day's start and end) and `formatDayOfWeek`. `core/util/WorkHoursFormat.kt`: `formatShortDayOfWeek` ("Mon"), `formatHourMark` ("4 AM") and `formatHours` (the "42.5" of "42.5 h a week"). `core/designsystem/component/TwentyFourHourClock.kt`: `rememberTwentyFourHourClock`, the phone's own 24-hour switch, which the two times and the time picker's dial go by.
- `app/MiloNavigation.kt` opens the screen from Home and the pairing screen from it. The two cards with a ViewModel of their own get it from `app/TransferObjects.kt` and `app/CheckObjects.kt`, and the home-screen widget's tile from `app/WidgetObjects.kt`.
- Tests: `app/src/test/.../data/settings/SettingsStoreTest.kt`, `ReportSettingsStoreTest.kt` (the four settings and the waiting report read back, their key names, a file with nonsense in it), `ReminderSettingsStoreTest.kt` (the same for the reminder's values), `ReportDetailsTest.kt` (which addresses are taken and which slips are caught), `ScheduleStorageTest.kt` (the schedule read back, its key names, a file with nonsense in it) and `SettingChoicesTest.kt` (the ranges, the steps, what each value looks like on the screen), `app/src/test/.../core/schedule/WorkScheduleTest.kt` (what hours a day may have, the copy), `app/src/test/.../feature/settings/SettingsUiStateTest.kt` (with which day a refusal stands under), `ReportSettingsUiStateTest.kt` (the report's four fields as stored, and that "not saved" stands with the refused address and never with the stored one), `ScheduleWeekStateTest.kt` (the week's hours, whether the work days agree, where a time refused for the whole week is said, and the ending of the reminder's day: 1st, 22nd, 3rd, 11th), `ScheduleSliderTest.kt` (the drawn line and the whole day's, every storable time on one of them, the end of the day as 23:59, what a moving handle shows) and `SettingsViewModelTest.kt` (the driving alert's switch: stored, then the alert told; the reminder's switch and day likewise, and the day's two ends; the week's slider storing its hours on all seven days, and a refused time for the week), `app/src/test/.../core/schedule/WorkWeekTest.kt` (the schedule read as one week), `app/src/test/.../core/designsystem/component/SpanScaleTest.kt` (the slider's arithmetic), `app/src/test/.../core/util/WorkHoursFormatTest.kt`, `DataUiStateTest.kt` (the last card: when its buttons wait, and which sentences stand for which outcome), `NothingRecordedViewModelTest.kt` (the daily check's card: what it shows, stored first and then the check told, a change that cannot be stored), `app/src/test/.../data/settings/NothingRecordedStorageTest.kt` (its three values read back, their key names, a file with nonsense in it), `app/src/test/.../data/settings/TransferStorageTest.kt` (what travels, what an import writes and leaves, what a restore takes out), `app/src/test/.../core/util/DurationFormatTest.kt` and `TimeFormatTest.kt` (with the phone's 24-hour switch against the language's own habit), and `app/src/test/.../app/MiloNavigationTest.kt` for the way in and out.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **The home-screen widget's switch is not in the export file,** on purpose (it is about this phone's home screen). An import leaves it as it was.
- **The parked limit is not in the export file.** The grace period and the minimum distance travel with an export; the parked limit, added later, does not, because the file's form is fixed and versioned (`ExportFormat.kt`) and adding to it needs a new version with a reader for both. An import leaves the phone's own value in place. Android's backup restores the settings file whole, so there it does come along. Logged as `[DEBT]` in FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06 (evening).
- **The row "End a trip after the truck has not moved for" was seen on an emulator only** (2026-10-06: "10 min" out of the box, "5 min" after one press, and the minus greyed out there). Device check 265.
- **Never run on the phone.** The new layout is device checks NL-60 to NL-74. The older checks still hold for what the settings do: 111 to 124 and 126, for the schedule 130 to 135, 151 and 152, for the driving alert DA-2, for the report's four settings 179 to 181, for the reminder 213 and 221, for the daily check NR-1 and NR-2, and for the last tile 234 to 245. **They were written for the cards the screen had before,** so where one names a card, a day's switch or a button "Start: 08:00", the tile, the day's button and the hours of NL-60 to NL-69 are meant (the checklist lists what is called what now). The sound heard in the phone's quiet modes is NL-70 to NL-74. On an emulator on 2026-10-06 the screen was drawn, the two numbers were stepped, a trip recorded straight afterwards was discarded by the new minimum without a restart, and the screen came back after Android had killed MilO in the background. On 2026-10-07 the new layout was drawn there beside the drawing, each handle was dragged, a day was given hours of its own and the days were made to agree again, a time was picked outside the drawn hours, a file was chosen as the sound, and an export was made (FINDINGS_LOG).
- **What differs from the drawing, each on purpose.** One small grey line with an arrowhead stands at the end of most tiles, and one under the first two: it opens the sentences the drawing has no room for. "Trips" has three numbers, not two, and the reminder has its day. The schedule's tile has the line "Set each day by itself". The sound tiles have their grey sentences. The last tile has its switch and the date of the last export. The names of the hours under the slider stand where their hours are on the line, so "9 AM" and "6 PM" are a few dp to the right of where the drawing spaces them evenly. The outline of a text field is a lighter grey than drawn, and a field is 48 dp high, not 46 (both as on every screen). The daily check's tile is not in the drawing at all. And the bottom bar stays on screen, as on every screen opened from Home.
- **A slider cannot set a time between two quarters of an hour, and the picker can.** A time set with the picker stays as it is, to the minute, until its own handle is moved; the handle then goes to a quarter of an hour. Beside a time between two quarters the other handle stops on the last quarter that leaves half an hour.
- **The one slider writes all seven days.** That is what makes a day that is switched on later agree with the others. It also means: hours that a day was given by itself, while it was switched off, are replaced the next time the week's slider is moved. A day that still has hours of its own when it is switched on brings them back, and the tile then shows a slider for each day.
- **Shown each by itself, the days stay so until the line is pressed,** also after "Use these hours on every tracked day" has made them agree. Otherwise the slider that makes the last day agree would be put away while a finger is on it. Leaving Settings and coming back shows one slider again, if the days agree.
- **While a handle is held, nothing under a finger moves:** the line the sliders are drawn on is kept (hours dragged back inside 4 AM to 10 PM go to the drawn line when the handle is let go), and the button "Use these hours on every tracked day" comes and goes only half a second after it.
- **Each move of a handle is a write to the settings file,** a quarter of an hour at a time. The daily check follows every one of them (it watches the schedule), so one drag leaves several pairs of its lines in the Log.
- The ranges (0.5 to 10 minutes for the grace period, 5 to 30 minutes for the parked limit, 0.1 to 2 km) were chosen while building and are Shawn's to change: three constants in `SettingChoices.kt`.
- The store accepts values the screen does not offer (a grace period of 0, for one). Such a value is shown as it is, and the first press of minus or plus brings it to the nearest value the screen does offer.
- There is no minimum distance of zero. With none, a start that never left the yard would count as a trip.
- A longer grace period keeps a trip open, and the phone recording, for longer after Shawn leaves the truck. The trip is still cut where the truck was found gone, so the walk is not counted.
- Changing a number is not written to the event log: each press would be a line. A trip's own lines show the values it ran with ("… is under the minimum of 300 m").
- **The switch "Include the GPS points" is not stored.** It is on whenever the screen is opened, so that an export is whole unless Shawn says otherwise that time.
- **An export or an import is not stopped by leaving the screen,** and what it came to is still on the card when the screen is opened again: both run outside the screen, and where they stand is kept by `DataTransfer`, not by the card. Ending MilO's process loses what the card said. An import that was under way then is finished at the next start, and the card says so ([Backup, export and import](#backup-export-and-import)); a file whose question was never answered is removed at the next start.
- The screen has no "reset to defaults". The defaults are 2 minutes, 10 minutes of standing still and 0.3 km, Monday to Friday 08:00 to 16:30, "Save as Personal", the driving alert switched on, the monthly reminder switched on for the 1st, and the daily check switched on for 12:00.
- **The daily check is on out of the box,** by Shawn's decision of 2026-10-06, at noon. It says nothing until a work day has reached that time without a trip, and needs the notification permission the Setup screen asks for.
- **The daily check's time is picked on a clock dial, to the minute,** like a day's hours, and has no range of its own: any time of the day can be set. A time before a day's start is not refused; that day is then checked from its start, and the card says so.
- **The daily check's switch and time are not in an export file** (`TransferredSettings`), although the rule is that a setting which means the same on any phone travels. Adding them changes the form of the export file, which needs a new version of that form that still reads the old one; it was left for a change of its own. An import leaves the two as this phone has them (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, `[DEBT]`; tagged `TODO(debt)` in `NothingRecordedStorage.kt`).
- **A change of the daily check's card is written to the event log** as the check's own two lines: the alarm asked for (or taken back), and what the check then decided. **So is every change of the work schedule,** with "(the work schedule was changed)" in the two lines: a start and an end picked one after the other leave two pairs.
- **The daily check's card was drawn and used on an emulator** on 2026-10-06 (the switch, the time picker in its 12-hour form, the alarm following both), never on the phone.
- **The reminder's day is stepped a day at a time,** so from the 1st to the 15th is fourteen presses. It is set once; a list of thirty-one days to pick from would have been a new kind of control for it.
- **The reminder is on out of the box,** as the brief has it ("on the 1st of each month"). It shows nothing until last month has a Business trip, and needs the notification permission the Setup screen asks for.
- A change of the reminder's card is written to the event log only as what the reminder then decided (a `REPORT` line, when the decision is not the one last written).
- **The driving alert is on out of the box,** a judgement made while building: the brief asks for the alert, and it does nothing until the Physical activity permission is granted on the Setup screen and a truck is paired. A press of its switch is written to the event log (a `DRIVING` line), unlike the two numbers and the schedule: it changes what MilO asks of the phone.
- **A half-typed address is not stored, and is gone when the screen is left.** The field says so in red while it stands there. Turning the phone keeps it, in red. Leaving Settings with Back does not: coming back, the field shows the address that is stored, which can be the one from before, without red.
- **The check of the address catches slips, not wrong addresses.** A missing "@", a space, a comma for the dot, two addresses in one field are refused; `acounts@example.ca` is taken. Only the email bouncing shows that one.
- **With the keyboard open, the red line under the address field can be hidden by the keyboard** while the field itself, outlined in red, stays in view (seen on an emulator, 2026-10-06). Putting the keyboard away shows the line.
- A name is stored at every keystroke, so a name that is half typed when MilO is ended is stored half typed.
- **With the time picker, to move hours past their present end, the end is changed first** (and to move them before their present start, the start first): each time is checked against the other as it stands. The red line says so. A slider needs no such order: its handles stop before they meet.
- **The copy asks no question** and replaces the hours of every other tracked day in one press. It is undone by setting the days again, which is why the button is only shown while it would change something.
- A change of the schedule is not written to the event log, like the two numbers. Each trip's closing line says which hours it was judged by.
- The time picker cannot choose seconds, and the schedule stores whole minutes. The latest end a day can have is 23:59; a trip that starts in the last minute of such a day is Personal.
- The day names follow the phone's language. **Whether a time has 24 hours or AM and PM follows the phone's own "Use 24-hour format" switch:** the dial, the two times beside it, and every trip's times on Home and Trips, so that they agree with each other and with the phone's clock. Until the review of 2026-10-06 the language alone decided, and on a phone set to 24 hours in English the dial had AM and PM, where one missed tap stores a time that is valid and wrong. Where the phone's switch and the language's own habit disagree, the time is written plainly, "16:30" or "4:30 PM", and not in the language's own short form. Seen on an emulator, never on the phone (device check 152).
- **"That change could not be saved" still stands at the top of the screen,** in a tile above the first two, whichever tile the press was in. For a press far down the screen it is out of view, the fault the schedule's own red line had until the review of 2026-10-06. It needs a settings file that cannot be written, and was left for a change of its own.
- **The time picker is Material 3's, which that library still marks as experimental** in the release the build pins (1.4.0). It is used through one component (`TimeDialog`), so a change to it in a later release is one file's repair; the weekly update PR would fail to compile, not misbehave.

## Website

**Status:** Built, live at `https://milotriplog.top` since the evening of 2026-10-08: Shawn set up Hosting, the deploy key and the domain (README, "The website"), the merges of #27 and #28 deployed it, and the sitemap he opened in his browser came from the domain over HTTPS. The pages were drawn in Chromium at a phone's width and a desktop's each time they changed (no line wider than the screen, the font loaded, no file missing), the What's new page and the name "MilO Trip Log" last, on 2026-10-08 · **Surfaces:** Web (not one of the app's two surfaces; the app is not changed) · **Last updated:** 2026-10-10

**What it does**
`https://milotriplog.top` tells a visitor what MilO is and lets them download it, and holds MilO's privacy policy (Shawn, 2026-10-08: "i created a web project in firebase for another project reactimate. i want to do the same for milo. i allready purchased a domain name milotriplog.top and created the project in firebase"). Asked, Shawn chose "Landing + privacy page", "Auto on merge, like Reactimate" and "Plain HTML and CSS" (ADR-003).

**How it works (step by step)**
1. **The pages** are plain files in `website/`: `index.html` (the lime tile with "Business mileage that logs itself." and the button "Download the latest APK", which opens the newest GitHub Release; the four phone screenshots; "By itself" and "With you"; the sample report page; the install steps; a privacy summary; "A personal project"), `privacy.html` (the policy), `compare.html` (below), `changes.html` (What's new, since 2026-10-08: every version and what changed in it, written from the app's own list by `tools/changes_page.py`; see [Version and What's new](#version-and-whats-new)) and `404.html`. No JavaScript, no cookies, no analytics, nothing loaded from another site.
2. **The look is the app's:** the page, tile and text colours, the lime, the corner radii and the Sora typeface are copied from the design system (`website/styles.css` says from where), the mark is the launcher icon's (`website/mark.svg`, also the browser's tab icon; and since 2026-10-09 `website/icon-512.png`, the same M on a full-bleed lime square of 512 × 512, drawn from the launcher icon's path, for the Google Play listing, which rounds the corners itself), and the font is a copy of the app's file. Dark only, like the app. One page at a phone's width: the screenshots two across, four on a wide screen.
3. **Firebase Hosting** serves `website/` (`firebase.json`): `/privacy`, `/compare` and `/changes` without `.html` (three rewrites; until 2026-10-08 the `cleanUrls` setting, which also sent every `….html` address to its short form with a redirect), the not-found page for any other address, `google995357ceb40b715c.html` for Google Search Console's ownership check, served as it is, a Content-Security-Policy that allows only the site's own images, styles and font, and cache times of a week for images and the font and an hour for the stylesheet.
4. **For search engines and link previews** (Shawn, the same evening: "can you also make some SEO content for the site. I would like a comparison to other similiar products triplog, mileIQ, driversnote,everlance"): `robots.txt` and `sitemap.xml` (the four pages); on the landing page a title and description with what people search for ("free automatic mileage tracker for Android"), structured data for Google (schema.org `SoftwareApplication`, and `FAQPage` for the questions), questions that open in place, nine since the widget's was added the same evening, ten since the labels' of 2026-10-08 ("Is the report enough for the CRA or the IRS?" says that the report prints a trip's purpose only where the trip has a label), and a preview image for shared links (`og-image.png`, 1200 x 630, drawn from the app's colours, font and Home screenshot); and `compare.html`, the comparison with TripLog, MileIQ, Driversnote and Everlance (below). **Since 2026-10-10 the structured data links to the app's Wikidata item and its GitHub repository** (`sameAs`): Q141683130 (`https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Q141683130`) and `https://github.com/shawnkowalchuk/MilO`. A search engine can then tell that the three pages describe one app. Shawn made the item himself on wikidata.org that day and keeps it there; the repository holds only the link (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-10).
5. **A merge to `main` that changes the site deploys it** (`.github/workflows/website.yml`): the job checks that the deploy key is set (it fails with instructions if not) and that the What's new page is the one the app's list makes, then uploads `website/` to the live site with Firebase's own action. "Run workflow" on the Actions tab does the same by hand.

6. **The name on the site is "MilO Trip Log"** (since 2026-10-08, evening; MilO for short in running text): in every page's top line and title, the link previews and the structured data (`"alternateName": "MilO"`). Every footer says "MilO Trip Log is an independent project, not affiliated with any other app or company named Milo." Shawn had found another mileage app called Milo on Google Play, and there are more; he chose the two words over "TripLog" as one, which is the brand of a competitor on `compare.html`. The app's own name on the phone stays "MilO".

**Since 2026-10-08 (later):** the landing page, its questions and structured data (with a `featureList`), the comparison and the privacy policy describe several vehicles, trip labels as the report's purpose, and What's new. Every page's footer and the landing page's last tile have **Buy me a coffee**, a button drawn in the site's own CSS in Buy Me a Coffee's yellow (`--coffee`), so the page still loads nothing from another site; Shawn's pasted button was an image from img.buymeacoffee.com, which the site's Content-Security-Policy (`img-src 'self'`) blocks and the privacy policy's "loads nothing from other sites" rules out. A long word, such as a web address, breaks on a phone rather than run off the page.

**Where the code lives**
`website/` (the pages, the stylesheet, the mark, `screenshots/`, `fonts/`); `tools/changes_page.py` (writes `changes.html`); `firebase.json` and `.firebaserc` at the root; `.github/workflows/website.yml`. ADR-003 has the decisions; README, "The website", the setup.

**Depends on**
Firebase Hosting in the project `milotriplog`; the deploy key in GitHub's Actions secrets (`FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_MILOTRIPLOG`); the domain's DNS records at its registrar; GitHub Releases for the download.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- **The privacy page makes promises about the app** (what it keeps, what leaves the phone and how, every permission and why). A change to any of that changes `website/privacy.html` in the same pull request.
- **The download button opens the newest GitHub Release** (README, "Making a release"): since 2026-10-09, 0.1.0.
- **`changes.html` is written, not typed:** edit `app/src/main/assets/changelog.json` and run `python3 tools/changes_page.py`. CI fails a pull request whose page is not the one the list makes.
- **The project ID is `milotriplog`** (display name "MilOtriplog"), read from the console's Project settings on 2026-10-08. `.firebaserc` and the secret's name in the workflow depend on it; on the free Spark plan.
- **The screenshots are the README's too:** they moved from `docs/screenshots/` to `website/screenshots/`, one copy for both. The home screenshot's street addresses are blurred; the Settings one shows Shawn's name, as the README does.
- The font is a copy of `app/src/main/res/font/sora.ttf`; the two are changed together, and its licence goes with it (`website/fonts/Sora-OFL.txt`).
- The Firebase command-line tool the deploy runs is pinned in the workflow (15.33.0), where Dependabot cannot see it (`[DEBT]`).
- **The comparison goes out of date.** `compare.html` says what TripLog, MileIQ, Driversnote and Everlance offered on 8 October 2026, from search extracts of their own websites (the session's network could not open those sites directly; FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-08). On purpose it gives no paid prices (it links to each pricing page: Everlance's own pages disagreed, and MileIQ's may have been old), quotes each tax claim as the vendor's ("says it is CRA-compliant"), says "not found" rather than "no" where a feature was not found, and leaves out what their privacy policies say. MilO's own column must change with the app: several vehicles and the purpose since 2026-10-08, not on Google Play.
- **The Wikidata item rests on MilO's own pages,** the site and GitHub. Wikidata asks for independent sources, so the item can be put up for deletion until a review or an article about MilO exists. If it is deleted, its address comes out of `sameAs` (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-10).

**Related**
[Settings](#settings) and [Connect and trip-start sounds](#connect-and-trip-start-sounds) (what the landing page says the app does); [Backup, export and import](#backup-export-and-import) (what the privacy page says leaves the phone).
