## Design system

**Status:** Built, not yet proven on the phone. The look described here (the "Bento" design, 2026-10-06) has been drawn on an emulator only (Android 16): Home, Trips, Setup, Log, Settings, the form for a missed trip, and the three dialogs; with the emulator set to light and to dark, with the font size turned up, and with gesture and with three-button navigation. Two of the four status dots, the question mark and the empty ring, were on no screen there; they were drawn outside the app from the same numbers. After the review of 2026-10-06 the form for a missed trip, the calendar, the clock and a greyed-out stepper button were drawn there again, in the build with its fixes. Later the same day the Report screen was drawn there too, and one fault was seen and fixed: two choices in one tile that touched. Its "Period" tile, Settings' two choices and the form for a missed trip were drawn again in the build with that fix. The same evening the design's typeface, Sora, was put in. Each of the eight screens and the three dialogs was drawn there again with it; several of them also with the font size at 1.3 and at 2.0. One thing no longer fitted and was fixed: the calendar's list of years at the largest font size. Later still, Home was laid out as the design draws it, with the shared parts that needed: it was drawn there with no trip, while a trip was recorded, and at a font size of 1.3; the truck's tile in its connected look only in a throwaway copy of the build, because an emulator has no truck. Nothing of this look has been on the phone. Device checks NL-1 to NL-30 · **Surfaces:** Phone · **Last updated:** 2026-10-09

**Added on 2026-10-07 (evening), for the choice of unit.** Two small things, both deliberate. `FigureText` takes an optional `spoken`: what a screen reader says in place of the figure and its unit ("48.2 miles"), because "mi" read out as written is no word. And `core/designsystem/text/DistanceWords.kt` chooses the words a distance is written with in a unit (`distanceRes`, `unitShortRes`, `distanceSpokenRes`, `unitNameRes`, `speedRes`); no screen names a unit by a string of its own. The "Units" tile itself is made of existing parts (`Tile`, `TileHeading`, `SegmentedChoice`, `Note`).

**What it does**
The one place colours, spacing, type and shapes are defined, and the shared components every phone screen is composed from. Check here before building any UI: reuse what exists, and add to it deliberately when something is missing (STANDARDS §8).

**The look (since 2026-10-06)**
- **It is the owner's design.** On 2026-10-06 Shawn supplied a design canvas with eight drawn screens in a direction called "Bento": a near-black page, on it rounded panels a shade lighter (called tiles here), one bright accent colour (a lime, a yellow-green), and no shadows. FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, has what was decided and why.
- **MilO is always dark.** It no longer follows the phone's light or dark setting: the design has no light variant, and one made up here would be a second design nobody drew. The window is painted in the page's colour from the first moment of a launch, so a launch does not flash white. The icons of Android's own two bars (the clock and battery at the top, the gesture line or three buttons at the bottom) are drawn light, always: with the phone set to light, Android would otherwise draw them dark on a dark page.
- **The foundation is everywhere; the design's layout is on Home only.** The colours, type, corners, spacing and every shared component are the design's on every screen. Home is laid out as the design draws it since 2026-10-06 (two tiles side by side, the large accent tile, the truck's tile). **The other screens still have the layout they had:** a column of tiles under a title. Their own layouts (Trips' month tile, the Log's coloured tags, Setup's count, the Report screen's two tiles) do not exist yet.
- **The typeface is the design's: Sora.** MilO carries it as one font file and does not take its letters from the phone. Shawn approved the download on 2026-10-06 (FINDINGS_LOG of that day, "Sora is in"). Sora draws the small "l" with a small flag at its top, so the name "MilO" looks a little like "Mi1O". That is the typeface, and the design's drawings show the same; it is not a fault. The one exception is decided: the Log's time and tag are to be set in the phone's own monospace, and no screen uses those two styles yet.
- **Nothing a screen does has changed:** no capability, wording, navigation or behaviour.

**What exists**
- Tokens in `core/designsystem/theme/`. A token is a named value: a screen asks for "the tile's corner", never for "26".
  - `Color.kt`: every colour value of the app, each written once. One dark scheme, with every one of Material's colour roles set, so that a stock Material component lands on the design's colours without being told one (a button is the accent with dark words, a dialog is a tile, a divider is the design's divider line). Beside it, reached through `MiloTheme.colors`, the design's colours Material has no name for: the fill of a control on a tile, the fill and border of a text field, the amber tile for something that is waiting (its fill, title, quieter line and button), the pair for an action that takes something away, the two pairs of the filter chips, and the five pairs of the Log's tags. A pair is a fill and the colour of the words on it. In use today: the control's pair, the text field's fill and border, the two chip pairs, and since Home was laid out, the amber tile's three, the quiet fill, the idle outline, the idle icon and the light on the truck's line. The others are named for the screens that are not laid out yet ("Tokens that wait", below).
  - **Status colours** (`MiloTheme.statusColors`): `ok` (the accent), `problem` (the design's red, also the scheme's error colour), `toConfirm` (a grey, for the empty ring: Shawn has to do or confirm something MilO cannot check) and `unknown` (the grey of secondary text: MilO could not find out), and `onDot`, the dark of the mark on a dot. Until 2026-10-06 there were a green, a red, an amber and a grey, in a light and a dark set.
  - `Type.kt`: the typeface and the type scale. **The typeface is named once** (`MiloFontFamily`): the one file `res/font/sora.ttf`, listed four times, once for each weight the design uses (400, 500, 600 and 700), and each entry tells the file how heavy to draw itself. Sora is a variable font, which is one file that holds every weight. Without that setting every entry would be drawn at 400, and bold text would look like regular text. A text style may ask for one of those four weights and no other. The scale is in sp, so it grows with the phone's font size setting. Material's fifteen text roles carry the design's sizes, from the 56 of the kilometres being recorded down to the 11 of a caption; the comment in the file says which role is which part of the design. `MiloTheme.textStyles` has the twelve the fifteen have no room for: the main button's words, typed text, a sentence of its own, the app's name, three figures, a tile's small label, the small line on an accent tile, the letter in the app's mark, and the Log's time and tag. The last two are monospace, the phone's own, for good. Nine are in use today; one figure (`spanFigure`) and the Log's two wait for their screens. **A tile's label has a style of its own** (`tileLabel`, 12 sp at Sora's own line): the 12 sp role the screens set their notes in keeps the taller line of a sentence, and a tile set in it would be 3 dp higher than drawn for every label. **The main line of a row is semi-bold** (`bodyLarge`, 14 sp at weight 600; the weight is how bold the letters are, from 400 for regular to 700 for bold): the screens set a row's main line in that role and its second line in `bodyMedium`, so the rows land on the design without a screen being edited. The price is that everything else the screens set in `bodyLarge` is semi-bold too, a plain note such as "Reading…" included, until the screens themselves are restyled. **The line heights are the design's** (since Sora is in): each style names its line as a share of its text size. Most text stands at Sora's own line, 1.26 times its size, which is what the font file asks for. A sentence that stands by itself has 1.4, the 12 sp of notes and second lines has 1.5, and the three largest figures stand on a line exactly as high as themselves. A style that named no line height would take over the one of whatever it stands in, a dialog or a button, so every style names one.
  - `Shape.kt`: the corners, named after what they round (`MiloTheme.shapes`): tile 26, small tile and bottom bar 22, main button 18, wide button 16, control 14, segment 12, app mark 11, and fully round for pills, dots and switches.
  - `MiloSpacing.kt` (`MiloTheme.spacing`): the five steps the screens were built on (4, 8, 16, 24, 32 dp; nothing uses the 24 and the 32 today) and six that came with the design, named after what they separate. What uses them today: 2 between a row's two lines, 6 between two buttons or two chips, 10 between the rows inside a tile, 12 between a row's dot and its text and above the bottom bar, 14 inside a small button or a text field, 18 beside the bottom bar and inside the main button. **Only Home is spaced as the design draws it:** 18 from the screen's edge and 10 between two tiles (`gutter`, `tileGap`, through `TileColumn` and `TilePair`). The other screens still stand 16 from the edge, with 8 or 16 between two tiles, and get the design's two with their own layouts.
  - `MiloTheme.kt`: applies all of it, once, around every screen. It also holds `MiloSystemBarStyle`, which `MainActivity` hands to Android so that the icons of Android's own bars are light.
  - **Two colours are written in a values file,** `res/values/colors.xml`, each a second time and each for Android, not for a screen: the page colour, for the window Android paints before any screen exists (`res/values/themes.xml`), and since 2026-10-07 the accent, as the colour of MilO's notifications. A unit test for each fails if it differs from the theme (`WindowBackgroundTest`, `NotificationAccentTest`). The only other colours in XML are inside the launcher icon's and the notification icon's drawables (edge cases, below).
  - **The font is the one part of the design system that is a file and not code:** `app/src/main/res/font/sora.ttf`, the font "Sora" as Google's fonts repository publishes it (`github.com/google/fonts`, `ofl/sora/Sora[wght].ttf`), unchanged. Its licence, the SIL Open Font License 1.1, is `licenses/Sora-OFL.txt` at the top of the repository, and has to stay there for as long as the font does. A unit test (`TypefaceTest`) holds the file to the SHA-256 of the download Shawn approved, reads from the file that it can be drawn at the four weights and which line it asks for, and checks that each entry of the family asks for its own weight.
  - **Tokens that wait.** The design's values were all written down in this package, also those whose part is not built yet. No component and no screen uses these today; each is described in its file by what the design draws with it:
    - colours (`MiloTheme.colors`): `logText`, `accentPressed`, `danger`, and the Log's five tag pairs (`logTags`);
    - text styles: `spanFigure`, `logTime`, `logTag` (`MiloTheme.textStyles`), and of Material's roles two of the three largest figures (`displayMedium`, `displaySmall`);
    - corners (`MiloTheme.shapes`): `wideButton`, `segment` (only as Material's smallest corner, for its menus);
    - spacing: `large` and `extraLarge`, which are older than the design.
  - **Sizes of the design that have no text style yet,** because nothing that is built is set in them: 28 sp at weight 600 (the Report screen's figure), 20 at 600 (the distance typed on the edit screen), 16 at 500 ("of 14 ready" on Setup), 14 at 500 (an address that is still being looked up) and 13 at 400 (a bare label beside a switch). Each is added with the part that needs it. A unit after a figure ("km") needs none: the design's units are 18, 16, 14 and 13 at weight 600, which exist.
- **Every pair of text and fill is held to a contrast** by a unit test (`ColorContrastTest`): 4.5 to 1 for words, 3 to 1 for an icon, a dot or an outline that carries meaning. Two things in the design did not pass, and both now use one grey (`#656A76`), made just light enough for 3 to 1 on a tile: the "to confirm" ring, drawn at 2.5 to 1, and the line around a text field, drawn at 1.2 to 1 (the review of 2026-10-06: with an empty field, nothing but its small label showed that there was a place to type in). The test holds the field's line to 3 to 1 too, idle, typed in and in error, against the field's own fill, a tile and the page.
- Components in `core/designsystem/component/`:
  - `PrimaryButton`: the main action on a screen. The accent colour with dark words, 56 dp high. Switched off it is a quiet grey that can still be read.
  - `SectionCard`: a titled group of content, drawn as a tile: a flat rounded panel a shade lighter than the page. Its title is the tile's label, small and grey, above what the tile holds. It is still a heading for a screen reader. Since 2026-10-06 it is a `Tile` underneath, and looks as it did.
  - **Added on 2026-10-06, with Home's layout. Each is for the other screens too, which the design draws from the same parts:**
    - `Tile`: the design's panel itself. Three kinds (`TileKind`): plain, the accent one with dark words (a screen's "hero"), and the amber one. Three paddings as drawn (`TilePadding`): 16 all round for a tile that shares a line, 16 by 18 for one as wide as the screen, 18 all round for a hero that holds more than a title. Given a `TilePress`, the whole tile is one button, and the press carries the words a screen reader says for it. `TileLabel` is the small grey label that stands first in a tile, with a small icon before it where the design draws one.
    - `TileLinkLabel` (since 2026-10-07): the label of a tile that opens another screen: `TileLabel`'s small grey words, and an arrowhead that points on at the end of the line. It only draws the sign; the tile's `TilePress` is the button. First used by the Setup tile at the top of Settings.
    - `TileColumn` and `TilePair`: the column a screen of tiles is laid out in (18 dp from each side, 10 dp between tiles), and two tiles side by side, each half the width and equally high.
    - `PinnedButtonColumn`: a `TileColumn` that scrolls above its main button, which stays in view at the bottom of the screen, as wide as a tile, 10 dp under the tiles. **Added on 2026-10-09** for the first start (the welcome page's OK, Setup's "Done, go to Settings"); no other screen uses it.
    - `FigureText`: a figure with its unit small after it on the same line ("48.2 km"), in the design's three sizes (`FigureSize`: 30 with 14, 26 with 13, 56 with 18). On a plain tile the unit is grey; on the accent tile it is the figure's colour. One piece of text, so a screen reader reads "48.2 km".
    - `Pill` and `DotWord`: a few words on a dark, fully round patch on an accent tile ("Recording", with a lit dot before it); and a word behind a small dot that is lime while the state is on and grey while it is not ("Connected").
    - `ProgressLine`: the thin bar, 5 dp, filled with the accent from its start by a share. It stands between a figure and the words that say the same share, so a screen reader is told nothing about it. (Setup's bar is drawn 8 dp; that comes with Setup.)
    - `HeroActionTile` and `HeroButton`: the accent tile that is one button (a title, a small line, a round dark mark with an icon: "Start trip"), and the dark button that stands on an accent tile ("End trip", 52 dp high). At most one accent tile on a screen, and never beside a `PrimaryButton`.
    - `AttentionTile`: the amber tile for something that is waiting for Shawn, with a title, a line and one round button ("Send", "Open Setup"). The tile is exactly as high as drawn; the place a finger can hit still reaches 2 dp past the drawn button, into the tile's own padding (`takesUpOnly`, since 2026-10-07; until then the tile had a copy of its own).
    - `AppHeader`: **the top line of every screen** (since 2026-10-07; until then only Home's, with a square button at its end). At the start the app's mark (the "M" on the lime square), "MilO", and today's date under it, which it reads itself each time MilO is looked at. At the end the screen's name, 17 sp like "MilO", with an optional quieter line under it (the Report screen's "For the accountant"). On a screen opened from another one (`onBack`), a lime arrowhead stands before the name and the two are one button that goes back; while it is pressed both are the lighter accent, as a link is, with no ripple. The line is never lower than 48 dp, so it stands at the same height on every screen. The screen's name is the heading a screen reader announces; the arrowhead and name are read as the name, a button, "double tap to go back". A long name breaks onto a second line rather than squeeze "MilO". Shawn's request of 2026-10-07: "the top MilO icon, name and date on the top left of the screen stays on all interfaces", the screen's name "on the top right for all interfaces, with the back arrow chevron to the left of that text"; for the bar's screens he chose the name without the arrowhead. **Since 2026-10-09 it can leave the end of the line empty** (`titleShown = false`), which only Home does.
    - `TruckTile`: the tile for the truck's connection: a title with the vehicle's name small at the end of its line, a sentence, and a drawing as wide as the tile of the phone, the line and the truck, in the design's three looks (`TruckLinkLook`; the third, "Connecting…", since 2026-10-07). Not connected is still. Connecting: lime dashes that run toward the truck, a Bluetooth mark that pulses in a lime ring, and a ring that spreads from it. Connected: a light along the solid line, and a ring from the truck. The times are the design's, and a unit test holds them (`TruckLinkMotion.kt`). The movement runs only while the drawing is in view and its screen is in front; with the phone's animations switched off it is drawn at rest. Each was measured on an emulator on 2026-10-07: 50 to 60 frames a second while it moves, and none out of view, in the background, or with animations off. Which look is shown, and for how long, is the screen's to say.
      - **Lower since 2026-10-09** (Shawn asked for a tile that is "not as tall", and chose this form of three drawn for him). Built, not yet proven on the phone (device check TT-1). It is 104 dp high with a sentence of one line and 120 with two; it was 152 to 187.
      - **Its sizes.** 14 dp above and below what it holds and 18 at the sides, 2 between the title and the sentence, 10 between the sentence and the drawing, 12 between the title and the name. Each is a spacing step that existed (`controlPadding`, `gutter`, `textGap`, `tileGap`, `rowGap`): **no token was added.** The title is `titleSmall` (15 sp at weight 600), the name `labelMedium` (12 at 600), the sentence `tileLabel` (12 at 400). It lays its own panel out, like the small tile, because a `Tile` as wide as the screen keeps 16 dp above and below.
      - **The drawing is the design's, made smaller.** The two ends are 30 dp across (the design draws 48), the mark in the middle 24 (36), the icons 13, 14 and 11 (20, 22 and 16), the solid line 3 dp (4), a ring's line 1.5 (2) and the mark's outline 1.5 (2). The dashes are as drawn, 2 dp thick and 6 long, so they run toward the truck at the design's speed. A ring is sized by the round patch it spreads from, so it is smaller with the patch.
      - **The title comes before the name.** The title stays on one line whenever that leaves the name room for about five letters, and the name has what is left: one that is longer is cut with "…". A title that cannot do that goes onto a second line, and the name may then take up to two fifths of the line, unless that would cost the title one more line.
      - **A screen reader is read the tile as one thing:** the title, the name, the sentence. The drawing repeats the words and is not read. A tile that is a button is read as that button.
      - **Seen on an emulator (Android 16) on 2026-10-09, as previews:** every state at 360 and at 411 dp wide, and at font sizes 1.3 and 2.0. The two moving looks were filmed for three seconds each: 44 to 70 frames changed while a look moved, for the old tile and for this one alike (the Mac was busy with other builds), and 1 or 2 with the tile scrolled out of view or the phone's animations off.
  - `StatusRow`: one requirement and its state. A round dot, a label, an optional second line, and up to two buttons on a line of their own under the text. **Four states** (`RowStatus`), each with its own mark as well as its own colour: `OK` (accent dot, tick), `PROBLEM` (red dot, exclamation mark), `UNKNOWN` (grey dot, question mark), `NEEDS_CONFIRMATION` (an empty ring). The marks are in `StatusIcons.kt`; they are drawn shapes, so they keep their size when the font size is turned up. The buttons are small filled buttons; the main one is the accent only while the row is a problem, and quiet otherwise. A setting that Shawn has confirmed by hand is drawn as `OK`, and its second line says that it is his word.
  - `MiloNavigationBar`: the bottom bar. A floating rounded bar that stands clear of the screen's edges, with one icon per top-level screen and **no words**: the icon of the screen that is showing is lime, the accent colour, as on the design's drawing of Home (white until 2026-10-06, the same day), the others grey. Each screen's name is still what a screen reader says for its icon, each button is as high as the bar (64 dp) and a quarter of its width, and the room Android's own bottom bar needs is left free under it. Until 2026-10-06 it was Material's bar with an icon and a name each. Added on 2026-10-05, with navigation.
  - `ScreenTitle` (with `ScreenTitleAction`) and `ScreenTitleWithLine`: **removed on 2026-10-07.** Every screen starts with `AppHeader` instead. The square back button before a title went with them; the screen's name with an arrowhead before it is the way back now. The Log's share button, the one action a title had, stands on a line of its own under the top line.
  - `SwitchRow`: a labelled on/off switch. The whole row is the target. The switch is drawn as the design has it: on, an accent track with a dark thumb at the end; off, a quiet track with a grey thumb at the start.
  - `StepperRow`: a setting that is a number chosen in steps. Its name, an optional second line saying what it is for, and the value between a minus and a plus button, each a 44 dp square (`StepperButton`: what a screen reader says for it, what it does, and whether it is greyed out). A greyed-out button keeps its square and shows its sign in the grey of secondary text, like a main button that is switched off: it can still be made out. The design's own grey for an idle icon was too faint for that on the square (2.1 to 1; the review of 2026-10-06). **Added on 2026-10-06** for the grace period and the minimum trip distance. Buttons and not a slider, because the values are few and exact, and a slider is hard to set to one of them with a thumb. The value stands in the middle of a place of fixed width (five times its text size, so it grows with the phone's font size), which keeps both buttons where they are while the value changes: with a place as wide as its text, the minus button moved 14 dp between "2 min" and "1.5 min", and every other press of a thumb held still missed it. A value wider than the place is still shown whole, and only then do the buttons move. A screen reader says the new value after each press.
  - `FigureRow`: a line or two of text with one figure at its end, such as a trip and its km. The figure can be greyed, for one that is in no total (or not in the total it stands beside), or left out. Where the design draws the number without its unit, a screen reader can be told the unit all the same (since 2026-10-06, for Home's list). **Added on 2026-10-06**, taken out of the Trips screen's rows when the home screen's Today card needed the same row: every list of trips is made of it.
  - `ChoiceRow`: one of a few choices of which exactly one is in force: a radio button (the accent when chosen), its name, and an optional quieter line saying what choosing it means. The whole row is the target, 48 dp at least, like `SwitchRow`. It keeps 4 dp of its own above and below its words, inside what is pressed, so two rows directly under each other are 8 dp apart and are never read as one paragraph (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, "Seen on the emulator"); a row whose words fit in the 48 dp is no taller for it. **Added on 2026-10-06** for "Save as Personal" and "Ignore them": a switch says on or off, and this setting has two sides that both need a name. The rows of one choice stand in a column with `Modifier.selectableGroup()`, so that a screen reader announces them as one group. The design draws this choice as two segments side by side; that is the next package.
  - `ChipChoice`: one choice out of many short ones, each a chip (Material 3's `FilterChip`, drawn as the design's pill: the one in force light with dark words, the others the colour of a tile), wrapped onto as many lines as they need. `ChipOption` is one of them: its label, whether it is the one in force, and what choosing it does. **Added on 2026-10-06 (phase 4)** for the Log screen's filter by category, which has fourteen choices of one word each: as a column of `ChoiceRow`s they would fill the screen before the list they narrow, and a row that scrolls sideways hides the choices that are off its edge. A screen reader announces the chips as one group.
  - `TimeDialog`: asks for a time of day with Android's usual clock dial, under a title that says what the time is for, with a button that takes the time and one that does not. Nothing is chosen until the first is pressed; pressing beside it, or Back, changes nothing. It is told whether to show AM and PM. **Added on 2026-10-06** for the start and the end of a work day. It wraps Material 3's time picker, which is still marked experimental in the pinned Material release; the opt-in is in this one file and nowhere else. The dialog is a tile, and whatever is chosen (the hour, AM or PM, the number on the dial) is the accent. Two things the theme alone did not do are put right in this file: Material draws this one dialog in the page's colour, whatever colour it is handed, and gives its title neither a style nor room under it (seen on an emulator, 2026-10-06). A third (the review of 2026-10-06): Material sets this dialog's two buttons side by side with nothing between them, so two filled buttons touched; they now stand 8 dp apart, as in the other two dialogs.
  - `DateDialog`: asks for a day with Android's usual calendar, a month at a time, with a button that takes the day and one that does not; the companion of `TimeDialog`. It is told the last day that may be chosen, and greys out every later one. **Added on 2026-10-06** for the date of a trip on the edit screen. It wraps Material 3's date picker, which is marked experimental in the pinned Material release like the time picker; the opt-in is in this one file. The picker counts days in milliseconds at midnight UTC, and two small functions with a unit test turn a calendar day into that and back, so that no time zone can move the chosen day to its neighbour. **The calendar scrolls inside the dialog** (the review of 2026-10-06): Material's calendar has one height, and with the phone on its side the dialog is lower than that, so the weekday letters were drawn over the first weeks and the last week lay under the buttons. Upright nothing changes. (`TimeDialog` needs none of this: Material's clock dial lies on its side by itself.) The chosen day is the accent. **Its two buttons are moved in from the corner** (the review of 2026-10-06): Material keeps them 6 dp from the dialog's right edge and 8 dp from its bottom, the place for its own text buttons, and a filled button there sat in the dialog's round corner. They now stand 24 dp from both edges, as in the other two dialogs. **The text inside the calendar follows the phone's font size up to one and a half times, and no further** (2026-10-06, with Sora): Material gives a day and a year a place of a fixed size, and Sora is a wide typeface. With the font size at its largest on an emulator (2.0), the list of years drew "2040" as "204". The dialog's two buttons grow as everywhere. It is one value in `DateDialog.kt`.
  - `TextEntry`: one line of text to type in, under a label that stays visible while it is filled: an address, or a distance (then with the keyboard for decimal numbers). It is told how long the text may be. **Added on 2026-10-06** for the edit screen; the first field of MilO that is typed into. **Since the new look** the label stands small and grey above the field, and the field is a dark rounded well with a hairline around it, which turns into the accent while it is typed in. The hairline is a lighter grey than the design draws (see the contrast test, above), so that an empty field can be found on its tile. The label is part of the field: a screen reader reads it with the field, and a tap on it puts the cursor in the field. **The field keeps what is typed itself** and reports each change; the text it is given is only where it starts. A text field has to show a keystroke in the frame it is typed in, and text that travels to a ViewModel and back through a flow arrives a frame later, so that the cursor jumps or a letter is lost. It can be given **an error line** (the review of 2026-10-06): what is wrong with what the field holds is then written in red directly under it, the field is outlined in red, and a screen reader is told what is wrong as part of the field. Told that it holds **an email address** (2026-10-06, for the accountant's address), it brings up the keyboard for one: "@" on it, no capitals and no corrections.
  - `ConfirmDialog`: the question before something is taken away. A title, a text that says what will happen and how it is undone, a button that names the action ("Delete", never "OK") and a button that does not do it. Pressing beside it, or Back, does nothing. Its text scrolls when the dialog cannot show all of it (the long question before an import, the phone on its side, large type): Material's dialog cuts off what does not fit, and the end of a question is where it says what is kept and how the action is undone. **Added on 2026-10-06** for deleting a trip; the first dialog of MilO's own. Also the question before "Restore recorded values", the one before a report is sent a second time and, asked by the app itself, "Leave without saving?". **Since 2026-10-06 it can be told that a press beside it, or Back, is something else than its second button** (`onPutOff`): "Did you send it?" has two answers, "I sent it" and "Not sent", and a stray tap must be neither, so there it only puts the question off. **The design has no dialog in it,** so this one is made of the design's parts: it is a tile, and its two buttons are small filled buttons, both the same quiet colour. The dialog cannot know whether its action takes something away, and an accent on "Delete" would invite the press the question is there to slow down. The calendar and the clock dialogs have the same two buttons.
  - `MiloIcons`: the icons a screen may use: Home (a house), Trips (a list), Log (a sheet of paper), Back (a chevron), Settings (three sliders; until 2026-10-06 a cog; since 2026-10-07 the bar's third button, in place of Setup's box with a tick, which was removed), Add and Remove (the plus and the minus of a `StepperRow`), and since Home's layout Play and Stop, Phone, Truck, Bluetooth and Link (two links of a chain: connected). Since 2026-10-06 they are the design's own icons, drawn as lines and not as filled shapes; Play and Stop are the two that are filled, as drawn.
  - `SquareIconButton` and `RowButton`: the two small buttons the components above are made of, kept in the design system for its own use: the 44 dp square with one icon (the back button, a title's action, a stepper's minus and plus), and the small filled button with words (a status row's buttons, a dialog's). Each is drawn at the design's size and takes up 48 dp, Android's smallest target for a finger. **Added on 2026-10-06** with the new look.
  - `rememberTwentyFourHourClock`: draws nothing. It answers whether the phone is set to write times with 24 hours ("Use 24-hour format" in Android's date and time settings; until that switch has been touched, Android answers by the phone's language), and reads it again each time the screen resumes, because the switch is changed outside MilO and Android tells a screen nothing about it. A screen calls it once and hands the answer to `formatTimeOfDay` or `formatClockTime` (`core/util/TimeFormat.kt`) and to `TimeDialog`. **Added on 2026-10-06** by the review of the work schedule. It is the only place a phone screen reads that setting, and the only setting a Composable reads for itself.
  - `CameToFrontEffect`: draws nothing. It calls the screen's ViewModel when the screen is first shown and every time Shawn is looking at it again, on either of two signs: the screen resumes (it is entered, or MilO returns from a settings screen, another app or one of Android's dialogs), or MilO's window gets the focus back (the quick settings panel or the notification shade closing, which resumes nothing). Returning from a settings screen gives both signs, so what it calls must be safe to call twice. The rule for the second sign is a plain function with a unit test (`focusRegained`); the effect itself cannot be tested off the phone. Added on 2026-10-05, after the review of the screens.
  - **Added on 2026-10-09, with the greeting. The app's only 3D drawing (ADR-005):**
    - `Mascot`: the mascot, playing one clip of his model once, on a see-through background, and saying when he can be seen and when the clip is over. `MascotClip` names the clips the app's copy of the model carries (today the wave). It is an Android view inside Compose, the first in the app: Filament draws into a `TextureView`. With the phone's animations switched off it draws nothing and is over at once. A further use of the mascot goes through this component.
    - `MascotGreeting`: the mascot large in the middle of the screen over everything behind it dimmed: fades in, waves, fades out; a tap or Back ends it early. See [The greeting](#the-greeting).
  - **Added on 2026-10-07, with the Trips screen's layout. Each is for the other screens too:**
    - `ExpandableTile`: a tile whose rows are put away under its heading until the heading is pressed (a day of trips). The heading is one button as wide as the tile: a title, a quieter line, and an arrowhead that points down while it is closed and up while it is open. A screen reader reads it as a heading and a button, and is told "Expanded" or "Collapsed". Whether it is open is the screen's to keep. The opening is animated briefly and happens at once while the phone's animations are off; if the rows would stand below the edge of a list, the list moves.
    - `SteppedTitle`: a large heading with two square buttons at its end, one step back and one step on (the month before, the month after). A step that cannot be taken keeps its square, greyed. Since 2026-10-07 it stands under the top line and names the month ("October 2026"); the screen's own name moved to the top line.
    - `SmallTile` and `SmallLinkTile`: the design's small tile (the smaller corner, less room inside), two of which stand side by side in a `TilePair`; and the same as a way to another screen, an icon and a few lime words, where the whole tile is the button ("Add missed trip").
    - `PillButton`: a `Pill` that is a button ("Not submitted", which opens the month's report). Drawn 30 dp high as the pill is; the place a finger can hit is 48 dp.
    - `ActionButtonRow`: the buttons that come up under a row of a list, side by side and equally wide (`ActionButton`, `ActionKind`: the lime one that leads on, the quiet one, and the dark red one that takes something away). Each is drawn 44 dp high and can be hit over 48 dp. More than three go on to a second line.
    - `PressArea` and `takesUpOnly`, for the design system's own use: how a control that is drawn lower than 48 dp keeps 48 dp for the finger without taking up more room than it is drawn in. **`takesUpOnly` is the one helper for this since 2026-10-07:** the buttons of a `StatusRow` and the button of `AttentionTile` use it too. One that needs more than 48 dp, because its words run onto a second line, is left as high as it is.
    - `ChevronIcons`: two arrowheads, forward and down, built like the icons of `MiloIcons`.
    - `placesLine` (`core/designsystem/text/TripPlaceLine.kt`): "from → to" with the words that stand in for a missing address grey and lighter, as drawn. Trips uses it; Home still writes the line in one style.
    - **Small additions to older parts:** `FigureText` has a fourth size (`FigureSize.TOTAL`, 44 with 16, which takes up exactly the height of its figure, as drawn); `SwitchRow` can be `quiet` (a small grey label, for a switch that stands on the page between tiles); `SquareIconButton` can be told the colour of its icon while it is greyed; and there are two more text styles, `quietLabel` (13 at weight 400) and `standInWords` (14 at weight 500). The list of sizes without a style, further up, was written before these two. The colour pair `danger` is in use since the same day.
  - **Added on 2026-10-07, with Setup's and the Log's layouts.** Built, not yet proven on the phone (device checks NL-50 to NL-59):
    - `TileRow`: one row of a tile that is a list, as the design draws the checklist and the event log: the rows stand directly under each other in one panel, a hairline between two of them. Each row draws its own slice of the panel (`TileRowPlace`: the only, first, middle or last row; `tileRowPlace` works it out), so a long list can be a lazy one, and the first and the last slice have the tile's round corners. Two spacings as drawn (`TileRowSpacing`): 12 dp for a row with 4 more at the tile's ends, and 11 with 6. Given a `TilePress`, the whole row is one button.
    - `GroupLabel`: the small grey label that stands on the page above a tile and names what the tile holds ("Android", a day of the log). A heading for a screen reader.
    - `Tag`: one short word in capitals, in monospace, on a fully round patch of its own colour: the kind of a line of the log. It is the first user of the Log's five colour pairs and of the text style `logTag`. Since 2026-10-08 it also names the kind of a change on the What's new screen, in four pairs of its own (`MiloTheme.colors.changeTags`: New in the accent, Improved in the Bluetooth tag's blue, Fixed in the amber tile's colours, Security in the error tag's red). A deliberate addition to the system, made of colours it already had: no colour value was added.
    - **Changed, each by a small addition:**
      - `StatusRow` can be told where its buttons stand (`StatusRowButtons`). `BELOW` is what it always did and still does unless told otherwise, so no other screen changed. `AT_END` is the checklist's row as drawn: one button at the end of the row when there is room (`buttonStandsBeside`, in `StatusRowLayout.kt`), a button that takes up the 40 dp it is drawn at, and a second line that is no higher than its letters when it is one line long. **Only Setup asks for it.** The other screens' rows keep their buttons under the text until someone decides that they change too.
      - `ProgressLine` has a thick form, 8 dp, for the bar under Setup's count.
      - `FigureText` has one more size (`FigureSize.COUNT`): 36 with words of 16 after it, "11 of 14 ready". The words are a new text style, `countWords` (16 sp at weight 500).
      - `ScreenTitle`'s action can wait (`ScreenTitleAction.enabled`), and its square now lines up with the tiles' edge, as on Home's top line. The Log's share button is the first action of a title that is not the way to another screen: the design draws it there.
      - `MiloIcons.Share`: an open box with an arrow that leaves it upwards.
      - `ChipChoice` draws its chips itself and no longer uses Material's chip: 36 dp high, 14 dp beside the words, and two lines of chips 6 dp apart, as drawn. Material's chip keeps 16 dp beside its words and 48 dp of height for the finger, which put two lines of chips twice as far apart as drawn. A press within 48 dp still counts (tried on an emulator: a tap 4 dp above a chip chose it). A screen reader is told that each chip is one choice of a group, chosen or not.
    - **The largest figures and their line.** `displaySmall` (36 sp, Setup's count) now really stands on a line as high as itself. A text style that only names a line height of 1 still takes up 1.26 times its size when it is one line: Compose keeps the typeface's own room above the first line and under the last. `Type.kt` says what it takes. `displayLarge`, on Home's tile of a trip being recorded, was not changed and is about 15 dp higher than its line height says (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-07).
    - **Tokens that no longer wait:** the colours `logText` and `logTags`, the text styles `logTime`, `logTag` and `displaySmall`.
    - **The app's icon is the app's mark** (2026-10-07; Shawn asked for "the green M at the top for the app logo too"): a lime background and the dark "M" of the mark at the top of Home, in `res/drawable/ic_launcher_background.xml` and `ic_launcher_foreground.xml`. The letter is a filled shape whose sixteen points were read from the font file, `res/font/sora.ttf`, and moved to weight 700 by the font's own weight axis, so it is the letter the mark is written with. It is as large on the icon as on the mark (a third of the width), in the middle. The two colours are written in the drawables a second time, as before; the comments there and at the top of `Color.kt` say which. The letter's shape is also what Android tints when "themed icons" is switched on. The small notification icon was not changed then; since later the same day it is the mark too (below). On an emulator the icon was seen in the app list and on Android's "App info" page, in the launcher's one shape, a circle, and measured beside the mark: the letter takes up the same share of the icon as of the mark, to within a hundredth.
    - **The notification icon is the app's mark** (2026-10-07; Shawn asked that "the phone's notification icon for the app is the same one, M with green square", and chose "Square, M cut out"). `res/drawable/ic_stat_trip.xml`, the small icon of all five notifications, was Material's navigation arrow; it is now a rounded square with the mark's "M" cut out of it. **Android only uses a small icon's shape** and paints it in one colour of its own (white in the status bar), so the lime cannot be inside it. The lime is the notifications' colour instead (`setColor`, `R.color.milo_notification_accent`), which stock Android paints around the icon in the shade; it may darken it on a light shade so that the icon stays readable. The letter is the launcher icon's, halved: 12 of the square's 20 units wide, not a third as on the mark, because at the mark's proportions its strokes would be about a pixel wide in the status bar. **HyperOS is reported to show the app's launcher icon in its shade** in place of the small icon; there the full-colour mark shows anyway, and the small icon is what the status bar shows. Not yet seen on the phone (device check NL-100).
  - **Added on 2026-10-07, with Settings' layout.** Built, not yet proven on the phone (device checks NL-60 to NL-74):
    - `SpanSlider`: the design's slider with two handles, for a span chosen on a line (the hours of a work day). A grey line 6 dp thick, the chosen part of it lime, and two round handles of 28 dp, light inside a dark ring. Under the line stand the names of a few of its values, each under the place its value has on the line; one that would run into another at a large font size is left out. **Only a handle moves it:** a finger takes a handle within 24 dp of its middle and drags it sideways, and a tap on the line does nothing, so a thumb that scrolls the screen over it changes nothing. Which handle is meant is decided where the finger came down, and the handle then moves as far as the finger has. The handle that is held has a lime ring. The two handles cannot meet or cross. For a screen reader each handle is a slider of its own, with a name and its value in words, and one step is one step of the line. It keeps nothing: it shows the span it is handed and reports each change. Material's own range slider is not used: its handles are bars and its sizes are not the design's.
    - `SpanScale` and `Span` (`SpanScale.kt`): the slider's arithmetic, apart from all drawing. Where a value stands on the line, which step of the line a place is, how close the two handles may come, and one step of a screen reader. A value that stands between two steps is drawn where it is and goes to a step when its own handle is moved. Pure, with unit tests (`SpanScaleTest`).
    - `SegmentedChoice` (`Segment`): one choice out of two or three short names, side by side and equally wide inside a dark frame with the 16 dp corner; the one in force is lime with dark words, on the 12 dp corner. Each part is 44 dp high and the frame keeps 4 dp around them. A screen reader hears one group of choices. The first users of the shapes `wideButton` and `segment`. Settings uses it for "Save as Personal" and "Ignore them", and since 2026-10-07 the Report screen for "A whole month" and "A date range". The edit screen's "Business" over "Personal" are two `ChoiceButton`s in the same colours.
    - `ToggleButtonRow` (`ToggleButton`): a row of buttons that are each on or off by themselves, equally wide and 5 dp apart: the seven days of the work schedule. On is lime with dark words, off is the quiet fill of a control with grey words. Each is 48 dp high. Seven across a tile are a few dp narrower than 48, and a press near one counts as a press on it. A screen reader is told each button's full name and "on" or "off" in the caller's words. At the phone's largest font sizes a word that is wider than its button is written smaller, down to 8 sp, and not cut off.
    - `QuietExpander`: one small grey line with an arrowhead that opens what is put away under it, inside a tile or on the page. For the sentences that say what a tile's settings do, and for a part of a tile that is seldom needed. It is drawn 28 dp high and can be hit over 48 dp. A screen reader hears a button that is "Expanded" or "Collapsed". Told that it is not `pressable`, it is a plain caption above what it holds. **What has happened is never put away behind one.**
    - `SwitchTile` and `TitledSwitchRow` (`SwitchTiles.kt`): the two ways the design draws a switch inside a tile. The first is a tile for half a line that is one switch: a small grey label, a sentence, and the switch under it ("Driving alert"). The second is a tile's own title with a quieter line under it and the switch at its end ("Monthly reminder"), drawn 36 dp high. Each is pressed as a whole and read as one switch, and a screen reader can jump to it as to a heading. Both draw the switch of `SwitchRow`.
    - `TileButton`: the small button of a status row, for a screen to place where a tile needs one ("Pair truck" under the truck's name). Drawn 40 dp high, and it takes up no more; lime only for the one that puts something right. It can be told what a screen reader says for it, for a button that shows only a time.
    - **Changed, each by a small addition:**
      - `StepperRow` is laid out as drawn: the name at the start of the row, and the minus, the value and the plus at its end, the value in a place 72 dp wide. The row takes up the 44 dp its squares are drawn at. At a font size so large that the name would be squeezed beside the buttons, it stands above them. Only Settings uses it.
      - `ActionButton` can carry a small icon before its word (the triangle of "Play") and can be told to wait (`enabled`): its word is then grey, and a screen reader hears that the button is switched off.
      - `TextEntry` can show grey words inside the field while it is empty (`placeholder`), as drawn. A screen reader is not told them.
      - `SwitchRow`'s drawing of the switch can be used by the design system's other parts.
    - **Tokens that no longer wait:** the shapes `wideButton` and `segment`, and the text style `spanFigure` (22 sp at weight 600: the hours of the work week).
    - **Settings no longer uses a part that is not the design's.** It has no `SectionCard`, no Material text button and no `ChoiceRow` left. Where the notes further down ("The screens' own buttons are not the design's yet", "Two screens still add a gap of their own between two choices") name Settings, they were true until this layout.
  - **Added on 2026-10-07, with the layouts of the Report screen, the edit screen and the pairing screen.** Built, not yet proven on the phone (device checks NL-75 to NL-94). Each is a new file:
    - `ScreenTitleWithLine` (`TitleWithLine.kt`): the title of a screen opened from another, behind the square back button, with a quieter line under it ("September report" over "For the accountant").
    - `ValueButton`: a value that is chosen with a picker, as a quiet button on a tile with its words at the start. 48 dp high alone (a date), 56 dp with a small grey label over the value ("Start" over "7:42 AM").
    - `ChoiceButton`: one of two or three choices that are each a button; the one in force is lime with dark words ("Business" over "Personal" on the edit screen, its one user). Choices that stand side by side are a `SegmentedChoice`. Drawn 44 dp high; the place a finger can hit is 48 dp. A screen reader is told which one is chosen.
    - `LinkButton`: a few words in lime that are a button ("Preview PDF", "Change"). It takes up only the room of its words; the place a finger can hit is 48 dp high. Pressed, the words are the lighter lime, the first use of the colour `accentPressed`.
    - `WideButton`: the quiet button that stands on the page under a main button, the colour of a tile, 48 dp high ("Save PDF and CSV", "Mark as sent"). It is the first use of the corner `wideButton`.
    - `PrimaryIconButton`: the main button with a small icon before its words, 58 dp high ("Email the report").
    - `ReportIcons`: one icon, a paper plane, built like the icons of `MiloIcons`.
    - `PageThumbnail`: a small picture of a printed page, 104 by 136 dp, made of plain shapes. Decoration: a screen reader is told nothing.
    - `DashedTile`: a tile that is only an outline of dashes, with a title, a line and one quiet button ("What MilO recorded", "Restore"). The button is drawn 44 dp high; the place a finger can hit is 48 dp.
    - **Changed, each by a small addition that leaves every other screen as it was:**
      - `FigureText` has one more size (`FigureSize.TILE`): 28 with a unit of 13, the Report screen's kilometres.
      - `TextEntry` can be a figure in a tile of its own (`figure`): what is typed is 20 sp at weight 600, and its label is the tile's label.
      - `SwitchRow` has a second quiet form (`quietOnTile`): the small label and the low row, with the label in the tile's own text colour.
      - `RowButton` can wait (`enabled`): greyed, never hidden.
      - `Type.kt` has the two text styles that were waiting for their screens: `tileFigure` (28 sp at 600) and `fieldFigure` (20 sp at 600). The line above that says these two sizes have no style is out of date.
    - **Three things the canvas draws that MilO did not do are built since:** "Preview PDF", "Mark as sent" and "Reminding daily" (the Report screen's entry says how each works). The gotcha below still lists them.
    - **Two choices that were `ChoiceRow`s are drawn as the design draws them now:** the edit screen's "Business" and "Personal" are `ChoiceButton`s, and the Report screen's "Period" is a `SegmentedChoice` (since 2026-10-07; for a day it was two `ChoiceButton`s side by side). The note below about the gap between two choices still names all three screens and is out of date: no screen has a column of `ChoiceRow`s today.
- **Words two features must say alike** are in `core/designsystem/text/`. Today one file, `TripCategoryWords.kt` (`categoryWordsRes`): which words say what a closed trip is saved as, used by Home and by Trips. It is here and not beside the rule in `core/schedule/`, because it chooses a string resource and the rules packages import nothing of Android (**moved here on 2026-10-06** by the review). It is the only file of the design system that knows a type from outside it (`TripCategory`). A second file since 2026-10-06, `SubmissionWords.kt` (`submissionWordsRes` and `submissionWords`): which sentence says whether a month's report has been sent, used by the Trips screen's month card and by the Report screen. It is handed plain times and a number, and knows no type from outside. A third since Home's layout, `TripPlaceWords.kt` (`routeText`, `startText`, `placesWords`): which words say where a trip went, moved here from the Trips screen when Home began to write the same. It knows one type from outside, `TripPlace`, what the address lookup knows about one end of a trip.
- **Patterns the screens share,** to be followed by the next ones:
  - A screen starts with `AppHeader` (since 2026-10-07; `ScreenTitle` before) and pads its content with `MiloTheme.spacing.medium`. A screen of fixed content is a scrolling `Column` of `SectionCard`s; a screen of stored rows (Log, Trips) is a `LazyColumn`. **Home is the first screen laid out as the design draws it, and the pattern for the others:** a scrolling `TileColumn` of `Tile`s, with `TilePair` for two side by side.
  - While a tile's figure is being read it shows a dash, never a zero, and a screen reader is told that it is reading.
  - A tile that leads to another screen is pressed as a whole, and its link ("All 4 today") is words in the accent colour inside it: one target the size of the tile, not a small word to hit.
  - While a screen's first read is under way it shows one line of text ("Reading…"), and an empty list says so in a sentence. Neither is a component.
  - A problem and the way to fix it is a `StatusRow` with a button, on Home, Setup and the pairing screen alike. A press that did not work (a refused sound file, a change that could not be stored, a trip that could not be changed) is a `StatusRow` in the red state without a button, shown until the next press. It stands beside what was pressed: in the sound's card, under the day whose time was refused.
  - A setting is stored at the moment it is changed, and no settings screen has a Save button. A setting that is picked in a dialog (a time of day) is stored when the dialog's own button is pressed. **A setting that is typed is stored as it is typed** (2026-10-06, the report's four). Where what is typed cannot be stored as it stands (an email address that is not one yet), nothing is stored, and the field says so with its own error line for as long as that is so.
  - **A form whose fields only make sense together is stored as a whole,** with one `PrimaryButton` at its end that names the action ("Save", "Add this trip"). The edit screen is the one such form (2026-10-06). **What stops a save is said in red where it is put right** (reworded by the review of 2026-10-06; until then every refusal was listed above the button, a screen away from what it named): a field that is typed into shows it as the `TextEntry`'s own error line, and anything else as a red `StatusRow` in the card it is about, under what is to be changed. The screen moves to the first of them, once for each press that stored nothing; a line goes away when what it names is put right; and the screen leaves when the form has been stored. Only a failure of storage, which is about the press and no field, is a red `StatusRow` at the button. What is typed is held by the screen's ViewModel, so it survives the phone being turned.
  - **A screen that holds something typed and not saved is not left without a question.** The screen reports whether it does (a plain function it is handed, like the ones for navigation), and the app asks "Leave without saving?" with a `ConfirmDialog` before it carries out any way out: the phone's Back, the screen's own Back arrow, a button of the bottom bar. The screen never asks itself: it sees only its own arrow.
  - **The keyboard takes room from a screen and never slides it away.** The activity is `adjustResize`, and `MiloApp` ends every screen where the keyboard begins. A screen with a text field is a scrolling one, so that the field and whatever is under it can still be reached.
  - A day is a text button that shows the date and opens a `DateDialog`, as a time of day opens a `TimeDialog`.
  - Text is typed into a `TextEntry`. The screen is told what was typed and never tells the field what to show.
  - A time of day is written by `formatTimeOfDay` or `formatClockTime`, with the answer of `rememberTwentyFourHourClock`: 24 hours or AM and PM as the phone is set, never as a screen decides for itself.
  - A setting that is on or off is a `SwitchRow` (and so is a yes or no in a form: "Ended the next day"); one that is a number in steps is a `StepperRow`; one that is a choice between named things is a column of `ChoiceRow`s, and the column adds no gap, because the rows keep their own; a time of day is a text button that shows the time and opens a `TimeDialog`. One choice out of many one-word ones, above a list that it narrows, is a `ChipChoice` (the Log screen's categories); it is not for a setting.
  - A total that must not be read as part of another stands in a line of its own, quieter than the figure above it (Personal under Business, on Home and on Trips). **Every total is the sum of the figures that are shown for its parts** (`sumOfTenths`), never the parts' metres added up and rounded once: what can be added up by hand must add up.
  - **A question that Android cannot answer is asked of Shawn, and nothing is recorded that he did not say** (2026-10-06: "Did you send it?"). Both answers are buttons that name what he did; a press beside the question only puts it off, and it is asked again the next time he is back on the screen. What the question is about is stored, so it outlives the screen.
  - **What a press needs and does not have is said before the press,** beside the button, with the way to where it is set (the Report screen's missing name and address, and "Open Settings"). The press then makes nothing and the screen moves to those lines.
  - Something that can be undone is done at once (Restore, Count this trip, a setting); only what takes something away asks first, with a `ConfirmDialog` (deleting a trip; "Restore recorded values", which drops what was typed in place of the recorded figures; leaving the edit screen with something typed and not saved). Sending a report for a period a second time asks first as well: it takes nothing away, but it puts a second email in the accountant's inbox. Removing a sent report asks first as well, and the question says what it does for that report in particular. **A question whose answer depends on what is stored says the answer that applies,** chosen by a pure function (`sentEffect`, `removalEffect`), and never one sentence for every case. **Where a press has more than one thing to ask about, the questions come one after the other,** each with its own two buttons, and "no" to any of them does nothing (sending a month that has not ended and was sent before).
  - A trip whose figures are typed and not recorded carries an asterisk after its times and a line that starts with the same asterisk and says what it means. The report for the accountant uses the same mark, after the trip's kilometres, with a legend.
  - A secondary action is a text button at the end of its line, on a line of its own under the text it belongs to. That holds for an action on a list as a whole too ("Add a missed trip", "Share the whole log") and for one on a single line of it ("Remove" under a sent report): the icon beside a screen's title is for the way to another screen only.
  - A row that is pressed as a whole is at least 48 dp high, Material's smallest target for a finger. `SwitchRow` sees to that itself; a row that a screen makes pressable (a finished trip on Trips) uses Material's own `minimumInteractiveComponentSize()`, so no dp value is written in the screen. A row that cannot be pressed is not given a click handler that is switched off: a screen reader would call it "disabled".
  - Something that appears because of a tap appears where it can be seen: if it would be below the edge of a list, the list moves (`BringIntoViewRequester`; the Delete button on Trips, the refusal of a day's hours on Settings, and the first thing the edit form refuses). Only a tap moves the screen: what is drawn again because the phone was turned stays where it is.
  - A screen that shows something Android reports no changes of (a permission, a setting, the paired devices, which month is the current one) reads it again through `CameToFrontEffect`, never through a lifecycle effect of its own.
  - **A press whose work takes seconds says what it is doing, makes its buttons wait, and says what it came to in the card it was pressed in** (2026-10-06: export and import). One quiet line while it works; then one `StatusRow` for each thing there is to say, the tick for done, the red exclamation mark for not done, the empty ring for something left for Shawn to do; they stay until the next press. A button that has to wait is greyed out, with the reason said beside it, and never hidden.
  - A ViewModel is handed plain functions for navigation; a feature never imports another feature.

**Edge cases & gotchas**
- No colour, dp or sp value may be written outside `core/designsystem/`. The only exceptions are the two launcher-icon drawables, which the launcher reads before Compose exists; `drawable/ic_stat_trip.xml`, the notification icon, which Android draws itself outside Compose; the window's background in `res/values/colors.xml` (since 2026-10-06), which Android paints before any screen exists; and the notifications' colour in the same file (since 2026-10-07), which Android paints around the notification icon.
- **Sora is a wide typeface.** A word takes more room than in most phones' own fonts, so a sentence breaks sooner and a button's words fill more of it. Whatever is added to a screen has to be looked at with the font size turned up as well. On an emulator (2026-10-06) nothing was cut off at the usual size, at 1.3 and at 2.0 on the screens that were looked at, once the calendar was fixed (`DateDialog`, above). On the phone: device checks NL-18 and NL-19.
- **A fifth weight needs a fifth entry.** A text style that asked for a weight the family does not list (300, or 800) would be drawn in one of the four without a word. `TypefaceTest` fails first. And a new version of the font file is a new look at its licence and at every screen: the SHA-256 in that test is changed in the same change, never only to make the test pass.
- **The app shows the font's licence on no screen.** The licence is in the repository, and the font file names its copyright and its licence inside itself. MilO is on Shawn's own phone and handed to nobody. If it is ever given to someone else, the licence has to go with it (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, "Sora is in").
- **The screens' own buttons are not the design's yet.** The feature screens use Material's text button directly in 25 places ("Previous month", "Play", "Export all data", a day's "Start: 8:00 AM", the daily check's "Check at: 12:00"). The theme makes them words in the accent colour, which is as far as a theme reaches; the design draws most of them as filled buttons. They wait for the next package, like the Log's lines, which the design draws with a small coloured tag and a monospace time: the colours and text styles for those exist as tokens, and no screen uses them yet. **A text button that is switched off is very faint** (Material draws it at about 2 to 1 on a tile, and the theme cannot change that): "Next month" is in that state on the current month, on Trips and on the Report screen. The button that replaces the text buttons has to be given its switched-off colours by name.
- **Three of Material's own components do not land on the design through the theme, and none is used today.** A tile is `SectionCard`, or a `Surface` in the tile's colour with the tile's corner, never Material's `Card`: that one takes the colour of a control and the smaller corner. A question is asked with `ConfirmDialog`, never a bare `AlertDialog`, whose title would be too large and its text small and grey. And the design has no outlined button: Material's would draw its outline in the divider's colour, which cannot be seen on a tile. The comment in `Shape.kt` says the same.
- **Also left for the next package, because each needs a screen edited** (the review of 2026-10-06):
  - *What scrolls ends in a straight line above the bottom bar.* The bar floats now, so a tile that is scrolled under it is cut off 12 dp above the bar, with nothing drawn there; a full-width bar used to hide that edge. The design does not draw a scrolled screen. Either the lists run under the bar with room left at their end, or the bar draws a short fade above itself.
  - *The screen that is showing is marked by the colour of its icon alone:* lime beside grey, as drawn on Home. A screen reader is told which one is selected. Whether a driver can tell at a glance is device check NL-10; if not, a second sign that is not colour (a dot under the icon, a quiet patch behind it) is still to add.
  - *Two screens still add a gap of their own between two choices.* A `ChoiceRow` keeps its own air since the fix of 2026-10-06, so in a plain column two choices are 8 dp apart: the Report screen's "Period". Settings ("Trips outside the work hours", `feature/settings/ScheduleCards.kt`) and the edit screen ("Business or Personal", `feature/tripedit/TripEditCards.kt`) each add 8 dp more between the two. On Settings the choices therefore stand 16 dp apart, twice as far as on the Report screen. Both read as two choices; they are not yet the same. The edit screen's two rows are one line each and stand as they did. The two columns' own gap goes when those screens are laid out.
  - *Two comments in the tests said "cog"* for the button that led to Settings. Both are gone since 2026-10-07, with the button: Settings is in the bottom bar.
- **The canvas draws things MilO does not do.** A work schedule with one range of hours for all days, "Mark as sent", "Preview PDF", "Reminding daily", five filters on the Log, and "11 of 14 ready" on Setup. Nothing was rebuilt for the look, so none of them exists. Each is Shawn's to decide before the screens are laid out (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, `[FINDING]`).
- **A filter chip that is not in force is the colour of a tile,** so a `ChipChoice` belongs on the page, as on the Log screen. Inside a tile only the chosen chip would be seen.
- **The steps between the dark surfaces are small on purpose** (page, tile, control on a tile, the inside of a text field): the design tells them apart by a shade, not by a line. Words and marks on them pass the contrast test; whether the surfaces themselves can be told apart in sunlight is device check NL-8. The one exception is the line around a text field, which is lighter than drawn (the contrast test, above): a field is something to find and press, not only a surface. It is one value in `Color.kt` (`fieldBorder`), should Shawn prefer the fainter line of the design after seeing both.
- The previews in the feature files are still marked for a light and a dark picture. Both now show the same dark look; the mark goes when each screen is restyled.
- Notifications are not Compose and cannot use these components. Their text is in `strings.xml`. There are five channels: the three of trip recording (`platform/trip/TripNotifications.kt`), the monthly reminder's (`platform/reminder/ReminderNotification.kt`) and the daily check's (`platform/nothingrecorded/NothingRecordedNotification.kt`).
- **The PDF is not a screen, and since 2026-10-07 it borrows the look all the same.** It is paper: its sizes are points, its text styles (Sora's sizes and weights) and its tiles are set in `core/report/ReportPage.kt` and `ReportBlocks.kt`, and its colours are `ReportInk` in `ReportPage.kt`: four of the theme's, repeated because the PDF is drawn outside Compose (the page colour, the accent, and the two colours of words on a dark tile; `ReportInkTest` keeps them equal), and three made for white paper (a quiet grey for words, 5.9 to 1 on white; a tile grey; a rule grey). The font is the same file, `res/font/sora.ttf`, each paint told its weight on the font's own axis. Its words are in `strings.xml` like every other text.
- The icons are built from path data in `MiloIcons.kt` and `StatusIcons.kt`, not taken from the material-icons library, which is frozen. A unit test proves that each one parses. Since 2026-10-06 they are the line icons of the design canvas; several match, or come close to, icons of the Feather and Lucide sets, which are published under the MIT and ISC licences. **Whether MilO should carry a line that credits them is an open question for Shawn** (FINDINGS_LOG, 2026-10-06, `[FINDING]`); nothing credits them today. **None of them has been seen on the phone.** On an emulator (2026-10-06) the four of the bottom bar, the back chevron, the sliders, the plus, the minus, the tick and the exclamation mark were drawn; the question mark was drawn outside the app only. Device checks 52 and NL-10.
- A card is only as wide inside as its widest line. A button that is to sit at the end of a card must be in a row that fills the width, or it lands in the middle of a card with short lines (seen on the emulator with the Settings screen's truck card).
- The bottom bar is always there, on the pairing screen, Setup and the Report screen too. Only the keyboard covers it, while something is typed on the edit screen.
- The Android Auto screen cannot use these components. It is drawn by the car from Car App Library templates.
